## 41. [Milky Way Near Twins (MWNeTs). II. Applications of a Hierarchical Framework to NGC 3521, NGC 2903, UGC 11487, and CGCG 047-026](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23327) <a id="link41"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.23327

**Authors:** I. B. Vavilova, I. O. Izviekova, A. M. Dmytrenko, O. V. Kompaniiets, O. N. Kukhar, O. S. Pastoven, S. I. Denyschenko, D. V. Dobrycheva, P. N. Fedorov, V. P. Khramtsov, O. Sergijenko, A. A. Vasylenko

**Abstract:** Galaxies resembling the Milky Way in present-day global properties may have followed different evolutionary histories. The Milky Way Near Twins (MWNeT) framework introduced in Paper~I addresses this through seven successive stages of present-day compatibility and evolutionary diagnostics. We apply this framework to NGC~3521, NGC~2903, UGC~11487, and CGCG~047-026, spanning different levels of observational completeness and outcomes within the hierarchy. We combine archival measurements with analyses of galaxy structure, spectral-energy-distribution similarity, atomic-gas profiles, and optical nuclear diagnostics. Stages~I--IV assess environmental, structural, nuclear, and global compatibility; Stages~V--VII examine SEDs, rotation curves, and evolutionary fingerprints. We distinguish supported compatibility, partial agreement, measured incompatibility, and unconstrained diagnostics. We jointly assess these four galaxies within the MWNeT framework for the first time and derive new structural, SED-comparison, and H\,{\sc i} profile measurements for UGC~11487. NGC~2903 is a well-constrained benchmark, whereas NGC~3521 combines extensive evolutionary information with uncertain bar identification. UGC~11487 has a securely detected bar, quantitative UV--IR SED similarity, and distinctive transient nuclear tracers, but its nominal stellar mass lies below the adopted interval and its extended rotation curve remains unconstrained. CGCG~047-026 meets the initial environmental, structural, and nuclear-state criteria but remains largely unconstrained at higher stages. The four applications demonstrate the framework's ability to organise complementary evidence and identify observational priorities without equating missing data with physical incompatibility. They establish a basis for future systematic searches and for using candidate near twins as external laboratories of Milky Way evolution.

## 42. [Effect of galaxy mergers on dust masses](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26166) <a id="link42"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.26166

**Authors:** W. J. Pearson, M. Romano, K. Małek, D. Donevski, K. Lisiecki, A. P. Cotter, S. Dey, T. Goto, S. C. -C. Ho, D. J. Santos

**Abstract:** Dust mass (M$_{Dust}$) is one of the key physical properties of galaxies that track the cold interstellar medium. However, the M$_{Dust}$ in merging galaxies is poorly understood. We aim to derive how the cold dust mass of galaxies is affected by major mergers at low redshifts. M$_{Dust}$ was estimated for approximately 500 major mergers in the North Ecliptic Pole extragalactic field using spectral energy distribution fitting runs with CIGALE, with different dust attenuation and emission models. We also analysed over 12 000 mergers from the SIMBA simulation using its M$_{Dust}$. We matched merging and non-merging systems in stellar mass, redshift, and local density, to enable a comparison of M$_{Dust}$. We then performed a second comparison using a control sample also matched in SFR. AGN host galaxies were then removed, and this matching repeated. Observed mergers have larger M$_{Dust}$ than the non-mergers by up to a factor of 1.82$\pm$0.35, or 1.60$\pm$0.44 without AGN-hosts. When matched by SFR, observed mergers show no significant change in M$_{Dust}$, both when including and excluding AGN-hosts. SIMBA galaxies show similar trends when AGN-hosts are included but there is still an increase in M$_{Dust}$ when matched by SFR. When AGN-hosts are excluded, the SIMBA mergers show smaller M$_{Dust}$ by a factor of 0.98$\pm$0.03, or 0.97$\pm$0.02 when matched in SFR. The higher the mass of a merger, the greater M$_{Dust}$ increase for observations for two of the CIGALE runs, or the smaller the decrease for SIMBA. Observed mergers are typically more dusty than their non-merging counterparts but only because they typically have higher SFRs, regardless of whether they host AGN or not. SIMBA mergers follow observations when AGN hosts are included, but show reduced M$_{Dust}$ when AGN hosts are removed. M$_{Dust}$ in SIMBA is linked to AGN in a way not seen in observations.

## 43. [IMF-aware N-body forward modelling of membership selection functions for young open clusters](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25200) <a id="link43"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.25200

**Authors:** Yue-Yue Shen, Chao Liu, Zepeng Zheng, Long Wang, Xiaoying Pang, Bo Zhang, Hao Tian, Yang Xiang

**Abstract:** We present an IMF-aware N-body forward-modelling framework, in which the stellar initial mass function (IMF) is an explicit input, to quantify the membership selection functions of open clusters, namely completeness and contamination as functions of magnitude. Accurate derivation of the IMF requires correcting for these selection effects, which otherwise distort the inferred present-day mass function. Our framework simulates clusters with IMF slopes $α_2 = -2.7, -2.3, -1.9$, converts them into synthetic Gaia observations, adds mock field populations, and recovers members with HDBSCAN to mimic observational membership selection. Applying this framework to 35 young open clusters (ages $\lesssim100$ Myr, distances $\lesssim600$ pc), we find declining completeness towards the faint limit; older, more expanded, and more diffuse models have lower completeness and higher contamination. For a given cluster, models with flatter IMFs yield modestly but systematically lower global completeness and higher contamination, despite initial cluster masses calibrated to the same observed member count. These models undergo more stellar evolution mass loss and expand more, resulting in lower projected stellar densities; their lower completeness is consistent with more diffuse clusters being harder to separate from field stars. Corrections derived from a mismatched IMF largely recover the total star counts but leave a larger distortion in the shape of the present-day luminosity function than those derived with the correct IMF. Because membership recovery is tied to a cluster's structural evolution and the specific membership algorithm used, our findings emphasize that selection functions should be inferred jointly with the IMF rather than assumed in advance.

**Novelty:** 4

## 44. [Analysis of Binary O-type Star Systems Using Recurrent Neural Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24015) <a id="link44"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.24015

**Authors:** Miguel Flores R., Luis J. Corral, Celia R. Fierro-Santillán, Silvana G. Navarro

**Abstract:** This work presents an initial analysis of the estimation of effective temperature and surface gravity for simulated O-type binary systems using a recurrent neural network. The proposed system uses 34 equivalent-width measurements from the optical region of a stellar spectrum. Two methods were applied to observed IACOB spectra before normalization: the addition of a blackbody continuum and the addition of a second observed spectrum. The first method produces only small differences in the normalized line profiles and in the estimated physical parameters over 3700-7000~Å. In the second method, the secondary spectrum is added with contributions of 5, 10, 20, 30, 50, and 75 per cent, as defined relative to the original primary spectrum. The general comparison includes 192 simulated systems and 1152 modified spectra. For most of these spectra, the estimated effective temperature and surface gravity decrease as the contribution of the secondary component increases. Individual examples using HD14947, HD190429, HD48099, and HD206267 show that this behavior is not the same for every combination: some estimated effective temperatures fall below the estimates for both isolated spectra, and some surface gravity estimates increase. Comparisons with previously published values also depend on the physical parameter and reference considered. The results show that the proposed system is sensitive to the addition of a secondary spectrum, but do not establish a unique contribution for the secondary component of an observed binary.

## 45. [ENFR: Evidence Networks with Flow-Matched References for Amortized Simulation-Based Model Inference](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23825) <a id="link45"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.23825

**Authors:** Kai Lehman, Jameson Dong, Carolina Cuesta-Lazaro, Niall Jeffrey, Benjamin D. Wandelt, Francisco Villaescusa-Navarro, Sven Krippendorf, Jochen Weller

**Abstract:** The evidence, $\mathcal{Z} = p(x | \mathcal{M})$, of a data-generating model $\mathcal{M}$ is a powerful tool in Bayesian frameworks: it enables model inference, model misspecification tests, and goodness-of-fit assessment as an alternative to standard discrepancy statistics, e.g. $χ^2$. While versatile, the evidence is notoriously difficult to compute, even when the likelihood is available. In both likelihood-based and simulation-based inference, estimating $\mathcal{Z}$ provides an immediate solution to high-dimensional goodness-of-fit or model misspecification tests: using $\mathcal{Z}(x)$ as the test statistic induces a minimum-volume level-$α$ test. Such tests are essential to assess model fidelity, which is necessary to trust simulation-based parameter inference. Existing learned approaches largely target Bayes factors, which only measure evidence ratios. Instead, we provide accurate, fast, and amortized estimates of the absolute evidence. We present ENFR: Evidence Networks with Flow-matched References. Building on the Evidence Network framework of Jeffrey & Wandelt (2024), the proposed method first learns Bayes factors with respect to a flow-matched reference, from which the absolute evidence is recovered. We show improvement in estimation of $\mathcal{Z}$ over the flow-based baseline in all applications considered and the best performance of all density estimation methods considered in our benchmarks. We furthermore introduce and provide the proof for an architecture that jointly learns the individual evidences of a set of models $\{\mathcal{M}_i\}$. We demonstrate the performance and scaling behavior of these approaches on data from a variety of scientific applications.

**Relevance:** 2
**Novelty:** 6

## 46. [Leviathan: a very massive, extended sub-millimetre galaxy spectroscopically confirmed at $\rm z = 4.05$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27101) <a id="link46"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.27101

**Authors:** William M. Baker, Francesco Valentino, Jens Hjorth, Gabriel Brammer, Ugnė Dudzevičiūtė, Kei Ito, Antonio Pensabene, Pengpei Zhu

**Abstract:** We present Leviathan, a sub-millimetre galaxy (SMG) spectroscopically confirmed at $z=4.051$ with JWST/NIRSpec. Modelling 31 bands of photometry from the rest-frame ultraviolet to the millimetre, we find a stellar mass of $\log(M_\star/M_\odot)=11.34^{+0.15}_{-0.22}$, making Leviathan one of the most massive spectroscopically confirmed galaxies at $z>4$, yet consistent with $Λ$CDM for a star-formation efficiency of approximately 20%. Although SMGs are often thought to be building the dense cores of compact quiescent galaxies, Leviathan is extended, with a rest-frame $0.9\,μ\mathrm{m}$ half-light radius of 4.3 kpc. Spatially resolved SED fitting gives a half-mass radius of $3.1\pm0.4$ kpc, consistent with its size in rest-frame $1.5\,μ\mathrm{m}$ imaging. This is three times larger than quiescent galaxies of the same mass, but consistent with the size-mass relation of star-forming galaxies at $4<z<5$ extrapolated to $M_\star=10^{11.3}M_\odot$. Leviathan forms stars on the main sequence (approximately $400\,M_\odot\,\mathrm{yr}^{-1}$) with dust spread across its disc. Both its stellar mass and dust are more extended than those of $z\approx3$ SMGs. Alongside GN20.2b, which has a similar mass and star-formation rate but is three times smaller, these show that massive dusty galaxies at $z\approx4$ can have very different stellar structures, and that not all have their stellar mass concentrated in a compact core.

## 47. [Learning without relearning physical symmetry: an SO(3)-equivariant convolutional network (CNN) on the sphere](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23821) <a id="link47"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.23821

**Authors:** Joshua Williamson, Niall Jeffrey, Lorne Whiteway

**Abstract:** Many of the most important datasets in the physical sciences live natively on the sphere, and these data often carry no preferred position or orientation. Using methods that do not reflect this symmetry leads to far higher simulation requirements, which can be catastrophic for scientific analyses (forcing reliance on cheap, imperfect models that bias results). We present a novel spherical implementation of a gauge equivariant mesh architecture that is specifically designed for the Hierarchical Equal Area isoLatitude Pixelization (\healpix{}) of the sphere. The $\mathrm{SO}(3)$-equivariant convolution is built from steerable filters, parallel transport and spin-weighted features. The architecture supplies the exact symmetry, so the network does not spend training data learning it. The convolution operations are constructed to give provably exact equivariance, given a band-limit. Our full CNNs can achieve SO(3)-invariance error at per-cent-level and lower, which is controlled through well-understood hyperparameters. On benchmarks, our technique gives the most accurate dense gradient regression and competitive cosmic microwave background foreground removal and denoising. The improved simulation efficiency matters most in simulation-based inference (SBI), where high-fidelity simulations are expensive and neural compressors require large training sets, so a fixed budget forces analyses with less accurate simulators. In cosmology lensing SBI, our technique yields the tightest posteriors at matched simulation count. This approach is a novel method that will enable new convolutional neural network analyses, including generative and inference tasks, for processes and data on the sphere.

## 48. [Galactic warps constrain equivalence-principle violation in the dark sector](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24618) <a id="link48"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.24618

**Authors:** Harry Desmond, Pedro G. Ferreira

**Abstract:** Current measurements of the expansion of the Universe allow for the presence of evolving dark energy, distinct from a cosmological constant, with an equation of state that seems to violate the null energy condition. One notable explanation for this behaviour is that the dark energy interacts with the dark matter, violating the equivalence principle yet evading conventional laboratory and astrophysical constraints. Such models produce fifth forces acting only on dark matter. We show how such forces separate halo and stellar disk centres, creating a potential gradient across the disk that warps it into a cup shape. Measuring the warps of $4\times10^3$ edge-on disks in Sloan Digital Sky Survey images and modelling the fifth-force signal together with competing baryonic effects, we find no sign of the predicted correlation between the measured warps and the fifth-force field. This constrains the fifth-force strength relative to gravity to $ΔG/G_N<7.7\times10^{-4}$ at 95% credibility (or $<1.7\times10^{-3}$ once systematic uncertainties are included) for force ranges of 8 Mpc and above. In the infinite-range limit of a scalar rolling today, even the conservative bound is three times stronger than the tightest cosmological bound (which, unlike ours, depends on the scalar's cosmological evolution) and about two orders of magnitude below other astrophysical bounds. It disfavours at 2-3$σ$ the couplings preferred by recent fits of interacting dark energy to cosmological data unless gravitational screening is invoked.

## 49. [Linear gas mass density: a unifying descriptor of Cosmic Filaments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26306) <a id="link49"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.26306

**Authors:** Daniela Galárraga-Espinosa, Daisuke Nagai, Isabel Medlock, Mathieu Langer, Nabila Aghanim, Kentaro Nagamine

**Abstract:** Cosmic filaments connect the nodes of the large-scale web and are thought to harbour a substantial fraction of the low-z baryon budget, yet a physically motivated framework for organising their diverse gas properties has been lacking. Here we show that the linear mass density $Λ$ = M_gas/L_fil, measured within the entropy radius r_K (the peak of the radial gas entropy profile) plays this role, analogous to mass in halo physics. Using TNG300-1 at z=0, we characterise the structural, thermodynamic, and chemical properties of 5637 filaments spanning more than two decades in $Λ$. Filaments form a continuous thermodynamic sequence with $Λ$, ranging from long, thin, cool, metal-poor structures to shorter, wider, hotter, and more enriched ones, with core temperatures spanning two orders of magnitude across the full WHIM window. The ratio of mass- to volume-weighted temperature reveals a shift in heat distribution: low-$Λ$ filaments have their hotter gas preferentially associated with denser material, whereas in high-$Λ$ filaments the hotter gas is diffuse. Well-defined scaling relations connect $Λ$ to temperature, electron density, pressure, entropy, and metallicity, with the T-$Λ$ scaling qualitatively consistent with gravitational heating. We present the first measurement of the gaseous linear mass density function, revealing a broad distribution with a flat low-$Λ$ regime and a steep exponential decline at high $Λ$, analogous to the halo mass function. The integrated Compton-y parameter $Y^{\rm int}$ scales systematically with $Λ$, providing a tSZ proxy accessible to current surveys. Finally, galaxy richness per unit length correlates tightly with $Λ$ (Pearson r=0.80), enabling $Λ$-based classification of filaments from spectroscopic surveys alone and guiding targeted searches for the missing baryons in the cosmic web.

The models favor $f_{\rm esc} \gtrsim 0.75$, with $f_{\rm esc} \approx 0.85$ providing the best overall agreement with the observations. ## 51. [ERGO-ML and how to directly infer the multi-temperature nature of intra-cluster gas: simulation-based inference with TNG-Cluster](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26627) <a id="link51"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.26627

**Authors:** Joey Braspenning, Annalisa Pillepich, Marine Prunier, Dimitris Chatzigiannakis, Dylan Nelson

**Abstract:** Though galaxy clusters are virialized and mostly in thermodynamic equilibrium, their internal gas thermodynamics are complex. The hot gas is multiphase and spatially inhomogeneous. The exact state of the gas contains important information about the internal physics governing clusters, however, measuring the multiphase distribution of the X-ray emitting gas is hard if not impossible with current observational methods. In this work (part of the ERGO-ML series), we present a direct inference method of the multiphase temperature distribution of the gas in the intra-cluster medium (ICM), applicable over a large range of spatial and mass scales. It works using different spectral resolutions (XRISM-like and Chandra-like) and realistic photon noise. Our method is an application of simulation-based inference. We train a neural-network on X-ray spectra in the range 0.1-10 keV, generated for $\approx 10^5$ sightlines of massive clusters ($M_{\rm 200c} = 10^{14.2-15.2}~\mathrm{M_\odot}$) from the TNG-Cluster cosmological MHD galaxy simulation. We show that our method recovers the mass-weighted temperature of both individual sightlines and entire clusters with an average prediction error of $< 0.01 ~\mathrm{dex}$, and the total gas mass within $0.04 ~\mathrm{dex}$. On top of that it gives accurate measures of the fraction of sub- and super-virial gas. Our method is capable of extracting the cooler gas phase even if the flux contribution is 3 orders of magnitude below that of the virial gas phase. We then show that we can accurately recover the spatial distribution of multi-phase gas both in relaxed and merging systems. This opens the way to studies of the multiphase nature of the ICM and the violent physics of feedback, accretion and merging.

**Abstract:** Supernova (SN) 1987A showed that some hydrogen-rich core-collapse supernovae (SNe) rise slowly to peak, defining a distinct subclass. We present ultraviolet-to-infrared, radio, X-ray, and spectropolarimetric observations of SN 2023iop at $z = 0.1020$, the most extreme 87A-like event to date. It rose for $\gtrsim88$ rest-frame days to $M_r \approx -20.1$ mag, also placing it among the hydrogen-rich superluminous SNe, and radiated $\gtrsim4\times10^{50}$ erg. Its spectra resemble those of typical Type II SNe and remain photospheric at $\sim330$ days post-maximum. Continuum polarization of 0.8-1.0% indicates an aspherical system. Hydrogen-rich pair-instability supernova (PISN) models of a $190~M_\odot$ star reproduce the light-curve up to 150 days after peak, but not the late-time emission. A late-time spectrum shows a feature near 7300 Å, tentatively identified as [Ni II] $λ$7378. The implied $\approx0.02~M_\odot$ of stable $^{58}$Ni is reached only in the most massive PISN models. The low-mass host galaxy ($\sim10^{8.2}~M_\odot$) is consistent with a metal-poor progenitor as required by the PISN channel. Fallback accretion reproduces the light-curve morphology, but not the colors. We consider a pair-instability explosion the most plausible scenario, with the late-time light-curve excess powered by circumstellar interaction, which shows no signatures in the spectra. SN 2023iop shows that the most luminous 87A-like events occupy an underexplored region of massive-star evolution.

## 53. [Multigroup Implicit Monte Carlo Radiation Hydrodynamics on Moving Voronoi Meshes with STORM and RICH](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25174) <a id="link53"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.25174

**Authors:** Maor Mizrachi, Barak Raveh, Elad Steinberg

**Abstract:** We present and validate an Implicit Monte Carlo (IMC) radiation-transport solver in the open-source, mesh-agnostic storm library, coupled to the moving-mesh hydrodynamics code rich in three-dimensional Voronoi tessellations. The solver is multigroup, or continuous in frequency, throughout; it transforms photon packets exactly between the comoving and laboratory frames, books every exchange with the material as a laboratory four-momentum so that the IMC path conserves energy to roundoff, formulates the Fleck factor in the fluid's proper time, and accelerates optically thick cells with multigroup Discrete Diffusion Monte Carlo (DDMC) behind an emissivity-preserving interface. We tested the solver on a broad suite of benchmark problems, and it performed well on all of them. The solver is built on the MPI-parallel Monte Carlo framework of storm, which scales to 13,440 cores.

## 54. [Clustering of JWST LRDs. Implications for PBHs and other early-forming seeds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23806) <a id="link54"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.23806

**Authors:** Gert Hütsi

**Abstract:** We use the observed abundance and spatial clustering of JWST little red dots (LRDs) over z = 4--8 to confront two formation scenarios: the standard picture in which LRDs form in virialized dark-matter halos, and one in which they are seeded by a dynamically subdominant population of primordial black holes (PBHs). The halo formation scenario accounts for both observables naturally and without fine-tuning. In the PBH scenario, matching the observed LRD abundance requires a negligibly small PBH mass fraction, and a power-spectrum analysis shows that such a Poisson isocurvature component predicts a clustering signal far below what is observed. The same conclusion holds for any population of dynamically subdominant early-forming seeds, unless their initial clustering is finely tuned or their activity is tied to massive host halos. We conclude that the spatial clustering of JWST LRDs strongly favours the standard halo formation picture and disfavours PBHs, or similar early-forming seeds, as a randomly activated LRD population; PBHs remain viable as seeds only if their activity is governed by massive host halos, in which case clustering alone cannot distinguish them from astrophysical seeds.

**Abstract:** We present a suite of 128 cosmological simulations in $w_0w_a$CDM cosmologies with massive neutrinos, designed to enable simulation-based inference of dynamical dark energy parameters from weak gravitational lensing and other cosmological observables. Each simulation follows $1024^3$ dark matter particles in a $512\,h^{-1}$Mpc periodic box, with cosmological parameters sampled using a Sobol sequence over the ranges $Ω_m \in [0.28, 0.35]$, $σ_8 \in [0.74, 0.90]$, $w_0 \in [-1.0, -0.5]$, and $w_a \in [-1.5, 0.0]$, motivated by recent results from the Dark Energy Spectroscopic Instrument survey. The nonlinear evolution is computed with $\texttt{PKDGRAV3}$ using the N-body gauge to consistently incorporate relativistic corrections from massive neutrinos, photons, and dark energy. For each cosmology, we generate 10,000 ray-traced convergence maps at six source redshifts $z_s \in \{0.5, 1.0, 1.5, 2.0, 2.5, 1100\}$ using $\texttt{LensTools}$, and additionally provide halo catalogues and projected halo overdensity fields for galaxy--lensing cross-correlation analyses. We generate 100,000 realizations at the fiducial cosmology for the construction of the covariance matrix. This simulation suite provides a foundation for field-level inference and non-Gaussian statistics with next-generation weak lensing surveys such as the Rubin Observatory LSST, Euclid, and the Nancy Grace Roman Space Telescope, as well as CMB lensing experiments including the Simons Observatory.

**Abstract:** We assess the dark matter interpretation of the 20 GeV gamma-ray halo excess reported by Totani and its proposed connection to the LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) high-energy nuclear-recoil event, which independently favors dark matter masses above about 200 GeV. A component correlated with a Navarro-Frenk-White (NFW) annihilation template remains with finer spatial binning and the instrumental response included. Outside the Fermi bubbles footprint, it is detected at nominal significances of about 4-16$σ$ over 5.6-130 GeV, falling to 1-7$σ$ when the enlarged boundaries of a recent substructured bubbles model are also excluded. With the substructured model, which improves the fit substantially, the component remains at 7-18$σ$ in four of six representative foreground configurations. However, its amplitude and spectrum depend strongly on the foreground model, and no tested foreground yields a single NFW normalization across the sky, including with additional spatial freedom in the eROSITA bubbles. Simulations show that foreground mismodeling can produce spurious NFW-correlated emission of the observed amplitude, and that residual-derived bubbles templates can bias the recovered spectrum. The 420 GeV $W^+W^-$ spectrum motivated by the LZ connection describes the data for some foreground models but is disfavored by others. These results disfavor interpreting the full excess as annihilation in a smooth NFW halo and weaken the case for a common origin with the LZ event, but they do not exclude a dark matter contribution to the persistent halo-like residual. We also identify seven gravitationally lensed quasars, only one of which was previously known, including four doubly imaged and three quadruply imaged systems. These results substantially enlarge the census of spectroscopically confirmed compact multiple AGN systems at z > 0.5 and demonstrate the efficiency of ERIS-SPIFFIER for the systematic characterization of sub-arcsecond dual and lensed AGN candidates.

## 58. [Critical magnetic Reynolds number of the weakly-collisional turbulent dynamo in supersonic plasmas](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24394) <a id="link58"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.24394

**Authors:** Radhika Achikanath Chirakkara, Christoph Federrath

**Abstract:** Weakly-collisional plasmas are found in several turbulent astrophysical environments, such as the Intra-galaxy-cluster medium (ICM) and the solar wind, where magnetic fields can be amplified by the turbulent dynamo. The onset of dynamo action is determined by the critical magnetic Reynolds number, ${\rm Rm}_{\rm crit}$, above which turbulent motions amplify magnetic energy. Although ${\rm Rm}_{\rm crit}$ has been studied extensively in collisional magnetohydrodynamic (MHD) turbulence, its value in supersonic weakly-collisional plasmas remains unknown. Here we determine ${\rm Rm}_{\rm crit}$ using three-dimensional hybrid particle-in-cell (HPIC) simulations of solenoidally driven turbulence with a sonic Mach number $\approx 2$. We vary the magnetic Reynolds number over ${\rm Rm}\approx31$-$1000$ and consider two initial plasma magnetisations, characterised by initial Larmor ratios $r_{\rm Larmor, 0}/L_{\rm box}=1$ and $100$. We find that the magnetic energy grows exponentially at sufficiently large ${\rm Rm}$ and decays when magnetic diffusion dominates at low ${\rm Rm}$. By fitting the magnetic-energy growth rate as a function of ${\rm Rm}$, we measure ${\rm Rm}_{\rm crit}=190^{+21}_{-17}$ and $198^{+16}_{-18}$ for the initially magnetised and unmagnetised plasma, respectively. These values are the same within their uncertainties, indicating that the initial plasma magnetisation does not significantly affect ${\rm Rm}_{\rm crit}$ in the supersonic regime. The measured values are higher than those previously obtained for the subsonic weakly-collisional dynamo, showing that increasing compressibility makes weakly-collisional turbulent dynamo action more difficult to excite. The measured values are also approximately 1.5 times higher than the ${\rm Rm}_{\rm crit}$ value reported for supersonic MHD turbulence.

## 59. [Kinematic structure and age of a Scorpius--Centaurus subcomponent: implications for PDS 70 planetary system](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25987) <a id="link59"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.25987

**Authors:** Arunima Arunima, Mike Ireland, Mark Krumholz

**Abstract:** PDS~70, the only system with two directly imaged protoplanets embedded in their natal disk, is a benchmark for giant-planet formation theory. Interpreting the planetary luminosities requires a precise age for the host star, but the widely adopted isochronal age of $5.4\pm1.0$~Myr is substantially younger than ages inferred for the Upper Centaurus--Lupus (UCL) population with which it is associated. We address this discrepancy by determining a new kinematic age for PDS~70 using radial velocities for 405 candidate members of the Scorpius--Centaurus T subcomponent from the Wide Field Spectrograph (WiFeS) on the ANU 2.3~m telescope, reducing uncertainties by a factor of $\sim5.5$ compared to \textit{Gaia}. %We supplement these measurements with H$α$ and lithium equivalent widths as youth diagnostics. We combine these with \textit{Gaia} DR3 astrometry in the \texttt{Chronostar} kinematic reconstruction framework to derive a posterior-weighted kinematic age of approximately $10$--$15$~Myr for all the T subcomponent stars, with a kinematic age of $\approx 12$--$16$~Myr for PDS 70, ruling out ages as young as the published isochronal estimate. At this kinematic age, PDS~70~\textit{b}'s (and to a lesser extent, PDS~70~\textit{c}'s) luminosity is incompatible with warm-start cooling tracks under current dynamical mass constraints. The most natural interpretation is that PDS~70~\textit{b} formed $\approx10.5$ Myr after its host star, and no less than $\approx7.7$ Myr after it.

## 60. [The ALMA REBELS Survey: The [C II] Luminosity Function of UV-Selected Galaxies at $z\sim7$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27052) <a id="link60"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.27052

**Authors:** Thomas Herard-Demanche, Pascal A. Oesch, Rychard Bouwens, Jacqueline A. Hodge, Hiddo S. B. Algera, Manuel Aravena, Karin Cescon, Elisabete da Cunha, Pratika Dayal, Andrea Ferrara, Rebecca Fisher, Yoshinobu Fudamoto, Kasper E. Heintz, Hanae Inami, Andrea Pallottini, Lucie E. Rowland, Renske Smit, Laura Sommovigo, Mauro Stefanon, Michael W. Topping, Paul van der Werf, Ivana F. van Leeuwen

**Abstract:** Luminosity functions (LF) are some of the most basic quantities providing insights into galaxy evolution across cosmic time and enabling a direct comparison between observations and simulations. Here, we present the first [CII]158$μ$m LF at $z\sim7$ from a sizeable UV-selected ALMA sample. Our analysis makes use of 27 galaxies with significant [CII] emission line detections in the epoch of reionization from the REBELS (Reionization Era Bright Emission Line Survey) ALMA Large Program and its pilot programs. We used these line detections and similar from ALPINE to calibrate UV luminosity-$L_{\mathrm{[CII]}}$ and stellar mass-$L_{\mathrm{[CII]}}$ relations at $z\sim7$. Given the challenge in precisely defining a selection volume for the REBELS sample itself, we rely on published UV LF and stellar mass function (SMF) determinations at $z\sim7$ and three complementary approaches to derive the [CII] LF. The UV LF-based method provides a lower limit on the [CII] LF, due to its missing the most dust-obscured systems. Our SMF-based estimate yields a Schechter function with a characteristic luminosity of $L^*_{\mathrm{[CII]}}=10^{9.11^{+0.11}_{-0.27}} L_\odot$ and a steep faint-end slope. From $z\sim7$ to $z\sim5$, our inferred [CII] LF shows an evolution of +0.3 dex in normalisation and +0.1 dex in $L^*_{\mathrm{[CII]}}$. Our results indicate a general underprediction from models of the abundance of [CII] emitters at high-redshift and thus potentially the number densities of massive yet UV-faint systems implied by our SMF-based method. Additionally, they suggest that 63% of the star formation rate density occurs in massive [CII]-bright systems and that $\sim$60-90% of [CII] emitters at $z\sim7$ may be missed by bright UV selections. This highlights the importance of surveys focusing on especially massive, UV-faint sources at $z>6$.

## 61. [Dark Matter Interpretations of the Galactic Center Gamma-Ray Excess with 18 Years of Fermi-LAT Data and the FL16Y Source Catalog](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26648) <a id="link61"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.26648

**Authors:** Sang Hwan Kim, Hae Barg Kang, Seodong Shin

**Abstract:** We investigate dark matter (DM) interpretations of the Galactic Center Excess (GCE) using 17.7 years of Fermi-LAT data and the FL16Y source catalog. We test 80 Galactic diffuse-emission models and include correlated systematic uncertainties from Galactic-plane control regions. We derive combined 95\% CL annihilation limits from 30 dwarf spheroidal galaxies (dSphs), including $J$-factor uncertainties, and compare the GCE-preferred regions with these limits and AMS-02 antiproton bounds. For self-conjugate DM, we study two-body annihilation, effective $4b$, $4τ$, and $2b2τ$ channels, and cascades through on-shell scalar mediators. For Model X, the best-fitting diffuse-emission model in our template analysis including a DM component, the dSph limits exclude the 68\% CL GCE regions for $b\bar b$, $c\bar c$, light quarks, gluons, and effective $4b$ and $2b2τ$. Parts of the $τ^+τ^-$ and effective $4τ$ regions remain compatible, although their best-fit points exceed these limits. Heavy two-body final states are less constrained but fit the GCE less well. Mediator masses affect both the preferred regions and the constraints. Part of the 68\% CL $4b$ cascade region remains below the dSph limits for $r=m_{\rm mediator}/m_{\rm DM}\lesssim0.4$. Among the four-body benchmarks, $4τ$ retains a comparatively large preferred region compatible with dSph and antiproton bounds. With the adopted BIG propagation model, antiproton bounds are generally weaker but constrain part of the $4b$ cascade region. Finally, we examine a secluded singlet fermionic DM benchmark with a predominantly four-bottom cascade.

## 62. [Primordial black holes in braneworld gravity: A gateway to hidden dimensions and the dark Universe](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26482) <a id="link62"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.26482

**Authors:** Itzi Aldecoa-Tamayo

**Abstract:** This thesis investigates the cosmological dynamics of primordial black holes (PBHs) within the Randall--Sundrum Type II braneworld scenario. The work comprises a preliminary discussion on gravitational collapse in the small-black-hole limit at early cosmological epochs, and two principal studies. The first study presents a fully relativistic treatment of PBH accretion in the early Universe, employing Michel accretion theory within the covariant Shiromizu--Maeda--Sasaki formalism. The analysis reveals that higher-dimensional effects substantially enhance early-time accretion onto light PBHs, with discrepancies between our black hole masses and previous estimates growing with cosmic time as $t^{0.33}$ --- a difference that reaches several orders of magnitude by the end of the strong-accretion epoch. This finding fundamentally alters the mapping between the initial PBH mass spectrum and present-day populations. The second study reassesses the observational and cosmological constraints on higher-dimensional PBHs in Randall--Sundrum cosmologies. Constraints from direct Hawking radiation, unresolved diffuse gamma-ray backgrounds, microlensing surveys, and energy injection into the cosmic microwave background are reanalysed in light of the modified higher-dimensional evaporation dynamics and updated observational surveys. Taken together, these results identify viable regions of parameter space in which PBHs can account for the entirety of dark matter while remaining consistent with current observational bounds. Notably, due to the enhanced accretion epoch, PBHs capable of constituting a significant fraction of dark matter may originate from masses below the effective four-dimensional Planck scale. This thesis thus puts forward a dark matter candidate with a distinct evolutionary history and observational signatures today.

## 63. [Flashing with colours: Photometric indicators of an early interaction in type II supernovae](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23852) <a id="link63"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.23852

**Authors:** A. Mendez-Llorca, G. Folatelli, C. P. Gutierrez, K. Ertini, C. R. Burns, N. Morrell, M. D. Stritzinger, M. Bersten, J. M. DerKacy, L. Ferrari, L. Galbany, J. T. Hinkle, W. B. Hoogendam, M. A. Tucker, C. Ashall, K. Auchettl, E. Y. Hsiao, K. Medler, C. Pfeffer, H. Xiao

**Abstract:** We present early-time optical photometry and spectroscopy for a sample 27 previously unpublished type II supernovae (SNe II) observed by the Precision Observations of Infant Supernova Explosions (POISE) collaboration, combined with a literature sample of 59 events to form a total sample of 86 objects. The resulting light curves comprise near-daily cadence, percent-level precision $uBgVri$ photometry. From spectra acquired within four days of the estimated explosion time, transient narrow emission lines of highly ionised elements, referred to as `flash features', can be identified. Based on the presence or absence of these lines, the SNe were categorised. We estimate key photometric properties, including rise times, peak absolute magnitude, and colour evolution. Our tests indicate that flash SNe are, on average, more luminous at the peak than their non-flash counterparts, which is consistent with previous studies. Interestingly, statistically significant differences were found in the rise times in the $B$ band, with flash SNe exhibiting a tail towards longer rise times. Flash SNe are generally bluer than non-flash events, with distinct colour distributions at 20 days post explosion, likely reflecting intrinsic differences rather than host-galaxy extinction. Colour-curve breaks within 30 days of explosion provide a clear separation between flash and non-flash SNe. The change in slopes across the break, combined with the absolute peak magnitude, yield a strong diagnostic of the flash-spectroscopic properties. This indicates that photometric observations alone may be used to identify SNe experiencing an interaction with dense circumstellar material, even in the absence of early-time spectroscopy.

## 64. [Are Dynamical Dark-Energy Cosmologies Old Enough?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27209) <a id="link64"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.27209

**Authors:** Agripino Sousa-Neto, Susana J. Landau, Jailson Alcaniz

**Abstract:** Recent analysis combining baryon acoustic oscillation measurements with cosmic microwave background and Type Ia supernova data show an apparent preference for a time-dependent dark-energy equation of state within $w_0w_a$ parameterizations. Here we test the robustness of this result against an independent cosmological consistency requirement, namely that the expansion history must provide sufficient time to accommodate the oldest observed stellar populations. Motivated by recent stellar and globular-cluster age determinations, we impose progressively stronger lower bounds, $t_0 \geq 13.6$, $13.75$, $13.8$, and $13.85$ Gyr, on the expansion histories allowed by DESI DR2+CMB+Pantheon+ data. We find a systematic displacement of the posterior in the $(w_0,w_a)$ plane toward the $Λ$CDM prediction, $(w_0,w_a)=(-1,0)$, as the minimum allowed age is increased. For $t_0 \geq 13.85$ Gyr, the Mahalanobis separation from $Λ$CDM decreases from $2.56σ$ to $1.72σ$, bringing the $Λ$CDM prediction inside the 95\% credible region. Our results show that the current evidence for dynamical dark energy is sensitive to independent cosmic-age information and that stellar chronometry provides a direct robustness test of the DESI-favored expansion histories. Requiring sufficiently old cosmologies can appreciably reduce the statistical preference for departures from the standard $Λ$CDM model.

Adding the foreground term to a mean host baseline reduces the leave-one-out root mean square error (RMSE) and mean absolute error (MAE) in extragalactic DM by \(13.0\%\) and \(7.5\%\), respectively. For 20 of these FRBs with optical host axis ratio measurements, adding a foreground term calibrated on the other 57 FRBs to the host shape model reduces the observer-frame leave-one-out RMSE from \(76.3\) to \(64.0~\mathrm{pc\,cm^{-3}}\) and MAE from \(54.2\) to \(50.2~\mathrm{pc\,cm^{-3}}\). ## 66. [Spiraling into the `snail' cluster MCXC J0157.4-0550](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24360) <a id="link66"></a>

**Authors:** Chao Geng, Chong Ge, Ming Sun, Li Ji, Guilin Liu, Zhicheng He, Haiguang Xu, John ZuHone, Paul Nulsen, Craig Sarazin, Nobuhiro Okabe

**Abstract:** We present a deep Chandra (130 ks) study of a striking, tidally induced cold front in a subcluster merging with the galaxy cluster MCXC J0157.4-0550. The subcluster, nicknamed the `snail' (J0157), displays an extensive spiral structure that extends to $\sim 260$ kpc --- one of the largest spiral cold fronts identified to date. Surface brightness and spectral analysis across four sectors reveal a consistent density jump of a factor of $\sim 3$ across the front, together with significant temperature discontinuities, and thermodynamic mapping further delineates the coherent, low-entropy structure of the stripped gas. Unlike classical sloshing cold fronts, the spiral in J0157 arises not from oscillation of a displaced cool core, but from subcluster gas that has been ram pressure stripped and tidally wound into a spiral by orbital rotation. Applying Gaussian Gradient Magnitude filtering and azimuthal surface-brightness analysis, we identify quasi-periodic corrugations along the front consistent with candidate Kelvin-Helmholtz (KH) instabilities, with a characteristic scale of $\sim 90$ kpc. The presence of these KH rolls constrains the effective ICM viscosity to $\lesssim 0.59$ times the Spitzer value and the magnetic field strength at the interface to $\lesssim 5.9\ μ$G. These results establish J0157 as a useful laboratory for probing macro-scale merger-induced gas dynamics and micro-scale plasma transport processes in the intracluster medium.

## 67. [A Morphology-Based Cross-Tracer Ratio for Robust BAO Tests](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25862) <a id="link67"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.25862

**Authors:** Paula S. Ferreira, Carlos A. P. Bengaly, Renyue Cen

**Abstract:** We use the Cross-Tracer Clustering Ratio (CTCR) acoustic response estimator, a new probe of morphology-dependent galaxy clustering in photometric surveys. CTCR is the ratio of the auto-power spectrum of early-type galaxies (ETGs) to their cross-power spectrum with late-type galaxies (LTGs). In the matched-kernel deterministic linear-bias limit the CTCR cancels the complete projected matter spectrum, including the baryon acoustic oscillation (BAO) wiggles, leaving only the relative linear bias. This makes the ratio uniquely sensitive to physics beyond deterministic linear bias: any residual acoustic structure in the CTCR must arise from a difference between the normalised non-linear responses of the two tracers. We, for the first time, exploit this property to model the measured ratio as a smooth scale-dependent baseline multiplied by a scale-dependent acoustic template, and constrain the amplitude $A_{\rm morph}$ of the differential acoustic response jointly with the BAO dilation parameter $α$. Applying the estimator to nine bins of the DESI Legacy Survey Data Release 8 spanning $0.1 < z < 0.9$, we obtain$α= 0.9997 \pm 0.0383$, consistent with the Planck 2018 $Λ$CDM prediction, and $A_{\rm morph} = 0.369 \pm 0.307$, consistent with zero. A Bayesian model comparison yields at most a 2.6$σ$ preference for the acoustic template over a smooth-only baseline. The estimator is model-independent, requires no external calibration of the absolute bias, and is directly applicable to the wide-field imaging surveys that will define the next decade of cosmology. It offers a new route to testing halo occupation and assembly-bias models, and an independent, systematics-robust cross-check of BAO measurements from spectroscopic surveys.

## 68. [Revisiting the Mass and Scale Dependence of the "Lensing is Low" Effect with DECADE](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23828) <a id="link68"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.23828

**Authors:** Kimiko Queen, Abigail V. Fisher, Johannes U. Lange

**Abstract:** Galaxy-galaxy lensing (GGL) is a critical probe of cosmology and galaxy formation. However, predicted GGL amplitudes tend to be $\sim 20-40\%$ higher than observations, primarily on small scales, a tension called the lensing-is-low problem. If not resolved through astrophysics or measurement systematics, this discrepancy may indicate a tension in our cosmological standard model. J. U. Lange et al. (2021), using foreground galaxies from the Baryon Oscillation Spectroscopic Survey (BOSS) and lensing measurements from the Sloan Digital Sky Survey (SDSS), presented evidence that this mismatch extends to large scales where astrophysical explanations are disfavored. We revisit this claim using lensing data from the Dark Energy Camera All Data Everywhere (DECADE) project. Compared to SDSS, the new DECADE measurements reduce the large-scale tension while leaving measurements on small scales mostly unchanged. Ultimately, these new results favor a scale dependence in the lensing-is-low problem.

Our catalog spans $0.2 < z < 0.9$ and $M_{\rm 200c} \gtrsim 10^{14} {\rm M}_{\odot}/h$, with four-band ($griz$) photometric redshifts that have a precision $σ_z/(1 + z) \leq 0.008$ below $z < 0.65$, and $\leq 0.011$ between $0.65 < z < 0.9$ and that exhibit an outlier rate of $f_{\rm out} < 0.2\%$. Our cluster finding is calibrated using spectroscopic data, but we find that a larger spectroscopic sample does not improve the performance of our cluster redshift estimation. We cross-match our catalog to those produced from millimeter-wave and X-ray data, and extract scaling relations between various cluster observables. There is excellent agreement between our estimates for different subsets of the 13,000 ${\rm deg}^2$ dataset, and also with results from prior catalogs and simulations. We use our joint analysis of millimeter-wave and optical data to estimate the halo masses of individual clusters. We also provide lensing profiles, measured for each cluster using overlapping galaxy shape catalogs, to enable additional mass-calibration work from the community. We demonstrate from a suite of tests that spatial inhomogeneity in the quality of the imaging data does not have an appreciable impact on the properties of this catalog. We also test a cylindrical richness observable, which is defined such that it is significantly simpler to model, and show that it has precision competitive with the traditional optical richness observable. All data products and measurements are made publicly available.

## 70. [Newly Identified Planetary Nebula Candidates in M31 using Deep-Learning Search of Hubble Space Telescope Images](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24554) <a id="link70"></a>

**Authors:** Yiğit C. Atılgan, Aleyna Demirci, Nazım Aksaker, Ammar Abbas Elmas, Mehmet Sarıgül, Aysun Akyüz

**Abstract:** The Hubble Space Telescope has imaged approximately a quarter of a billion stars in M31's disc, yet only slightly over a thousand planetary nebulae (PNe) are known, mostly from ground-based surveys. We show that deep learning can find them in broad-band images alone. In what is the first such search, we scan the Panchromatic Hubble Andromeda Treasury (PHAT) and the Panchromatic Hubble Andromeda Southern Treasury (PHAST) mosaics, nearly one square degree of the disk, where a PN is a faint blue point among crowded stars. Because the existing catalogs are incomplete, we train four Faster R-CNN detectors with partial supervision, so that uncataloged PNe are not taught as background, and combine their rankings into one candidate list, an ensemble. On spatially separate test fields, this list places 73% of the known PNe in its top 200, 4 to 6 times the purity of classical color-magnitude selection. Newer detection transformers and segmentation networks trained on the same data do not surpass our detectors. The ranking also follows classifications that the detectors never saw: emission-line objects that the literature calls PNe enter the list at 47%, against 0.6% for those called H II regions, which makes them candidates for reclassification. Among the top 400 candidates, 175 appear in no published catalog, 12 of them flagged as cosmic-ray residuals. One of them, a point source with [O III] emission in nine independent exposures, meets the PN criteria of the three major PN studies of M31, so by those criteria it is a PN. Most of the others lack strong [O III], as expected if narrow-band surveys have found the bright PNe, although some may be PNe with weak [O III]. These results support the near completeness of ground-based surveys for bright PNe and extend it from the PHAT area to PHAST. Finally, we identify evaluation pitfalls for detectors of rare objects and recommend simple remedies.

## 71. [Gamma-ray calorimetry of Seyfert winds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23028) <a id="link71"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.23028

**Authors:** Alberto Domínguez

**Abstract:** Two stacking analyses with the Fermi Large Area Telescope (LAT) detected GeV emission from radio-quiet Seyferts, one selected by an X-ray ultra-fast outflow (UFO) and one hard-X-ray-bright, with similar luminosities attributed to different mechanisms: wind shocks and the accretion-disk corona. We investigate how efficiently Seyfert outflows convert mechanical power into GeV photons, whether the two detections require distinct mechanisms, and how Seyferts compare with low-ionization nuclear emission-line regions (LINERs). Without new Fermi-LAT data reduction, we homogenize published measurements to 0.1-100 GeV, place local mechanical accelerators on one L_gamma-Edot diagram, and express eta = L_gamma/Edot as a fraction rho of the complete-calorimetry efficiency eta_max ~ 2.5%, inverting rho for the ambient density required by pp losses. Winds from Seyfert active galactic nuclei (AGN) have rho = 0.020 (+0.018/-0.011) for acceleration efficiency 0.1, a factor of ~13.5 below starburst superwinds (rho = 0.26 (+0.37/-0.16)). The difference exceeds a factor of three with probability 0.95 and is independent of acceleration parameters common to both classes; it is conditional on the wind kinetic powers, collapsing only if every wind sits at its minimum launch radius. The implied densities, ~10 and ~100 cm^-3, are consistency values: density, shock radius and speed are degenerate. The two stacking detections agree within a factor of 1.75 in gamma-ray output per unit Eddington luminosity, but the 95% interval, 0.26-24, is too wide to distinguish them. A third stack, of 186 low-luminosity AGN, extends the comparison 1.5 dex lower, ordered by AGN power. GeV detectability of non-jetted AGN is set by the product of mechanical power and calorimetric fraction. LINER outflows fail on power; for the adopted wind energetics, Seyfert UFO winds fail on calorimetry; starburst superwinds succeed on both.

## 72. [Annihilation feedback on elastic scattering in self-interacting dark matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27056) <a id="link72"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.27056

**Authors:** Kalliopi Petraki, Thomas Pignard

**Abstract:** Sizable dark-matter self-interactions, motivated by observations of galactic structure, often arise from the exchange of light force mediators. Light mediators can render scattering nonperturbative and make inelastic processes important. Unitarity, via the optical theorem, implies that elastic and inelastic interactions cannot be treated independently: inelasticity induces absorptive contributions to the two-particle self-energy kernel that determines the elastic amplitude, while the resulting incoming-state dynamics also modifies the inelastic amplitudes. We study the feedback of dark-matter annihilation on elastic scattering in the presence of a long-range force. Although annihilation is a short-distance process, it can be strongly enhanced by such a force. We find that its feedback becomes significant near Sommerfeld resonances, where it suppresses the low-velocity growth of the baseline elastic cross section. Near baseline elastic-scattering zeros, it may instead generate a nonzero elastic floor required by unitarity. A companion paper studies the feedback of radiative bound-state formation on elastic scattering, which can generate sizable self-interactions.

## 73. [Constraints on the Cosmic Expansion Rate Using the DECam Local Volume Exploration Survey and the Fifth Gravitational Wave Transient Catalog](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25909) <a id="link73"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.25909

**Authors:** Sean Patrick MacBride, Isaac McMahon, Danny Laghi, David Sánchez-Cid, Felipe Andrade-Oliveira, Marcelle Soares-Santos, Maciej Bilicki, Sarah Ferraiuolo, Surhud More, Rachel Gray, Gergely Dálya

**Abstract:** Gravitational-wave standard sirens provide a calibration-free measure of distances, offering important input on the long-standing tension between early- and late-Universe measurements of the Hubble constant ($H_0$). In the absence of an electromagnetic counterpart, the dark siren method can be employed to statistically correlate a gravitational wave event's localization with a galaxy catalog of potential hosts. We present a measurement of the Hubble constant using the DECam Local Volume Exploration Survey Data Release 3 and the fifth Gravitational-Wave Transient Catalog. Applying the dark siren method to a large, photometrically heterogeneous footprint required revised catalog-construction choices: a faint apparent-magnitude threshold ($m_r<20$) to control known photometric-redshift bias, a bright apparent-magnitude threshold ($m_r>15.5$) to exclude saturated sources, implementation of an evolving luminosity function parameterization, and de-biasing survey regions based on survey depth and photometric redshift precision. With these choices applied to DELVE, we obtain $H_0= 67.4^{+13.0}_{-11.2}\ \mathrm{km\,s^{-1}\,Mpc^{-1}}$ from dark sirens alone, and $H_0= 70.6^{+8.7}_{-7.0}\ \mathrm{km\,s^{-1}\,Mpc^{-1}}$ when combined with the bright siren GW170817. We test other galaxy catalog configuration choices, and find subdominant influence from photometric band choice and luminosity function parameterization on the final $H_0$ posterior. Our results demonstrate that extending the dark siren method to wider-area, non-uniform galaxy surveys is feasible and provides additional constraining power on $H_0$ relative to the spectral siren method, opening the door for analyses with forthcoming galaxy catalogs from LSST, Euclid, and Roman.

## 74. [Statistical Strong Lensing V. Inference with Microlensed Quasars](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24423) <a id="link74"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.24423

**Authors:** Shuo Zhang, Alessandro Sonnenfeld

**Abstract:** Microlensing of strongly lensed quasars provides direct information on the stellar component of lens galaxies, and can help break the degeneracy between the stellar initial mass function (IMF) and the dark matter. However, a statistical framework to forward model microlensing data while accounting for strong lensing selection effects is still missing. We incorporated microlensing effects into the hierarchical model of Paper III, and investigated to what extent flux measurements of strongly lensed quasars can break the degeneracy between the stellar IMF and the dark matter distribution. We tested the method on a mock sample of 219 doubly imaged quasars, lensed by circularly symmetric lenses. When microlensing effects are modelled, the inference using image fluxes recovers the input foreground population parameters and provides tighter constraints than the inference without flux constraints. The improvement is clearest in the $μ_α-μ_5$ plane, where $μ_α$ describes the mean stellar population synthesis mismatch parameter $\logα_{\rm SPS}$ and $μ_5$ describes the mean projected dark matter mass within $5\,{\rm kpc}$. The 68% credible interval on $μ_α$ shrinks from 0.16 dex to 0.09 dex, with the degeneracy between $μ_α$ and $μ_5$ significantly reduced. By contrast, fitting the image fluxes without accounting for microlensing leads to large biases on the inferred parameters. This work is a first step towards an accurate modelling of microlensing data. Application to real lens samples will require dropping the circular symmetry assumption and adopting more realistic models for the microlensing magnification.

## 75. [Hostbusters: constraining $H_0$ and binary formation channels through gravitational-wave host galaxy associations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25866) <a id="link75"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.25866

**Authors:** Madeline L. Cross-Parkin, Ignacio Magaña Hernandez, Antonella Palmese, Paul D. Lasky, Eric Thrane

**Abstract:** Gravitational-wave dark sirens can be combined with galaxy catalogues to provide a measurement of the Hubble constant. This inference requires assigning each potential host galaxy a probability of hosting a merger, but the appropriate weighting is uncertain because the binary merger rate may trace different galaxy properties, such as stellar mass or star formation rate, depending on the underlying formation channel. We introduce a flexible framework in which the dark-siren population is described as a mixture of two host-weighting prescriptions, with the mixing fraction inferred jointly with $H_0$. These components may be interpreted as subpopulations associated with distinct binary-formation environments. Using simulated catalogues, we show that marginalising over uncertainty in the host-population mixture preserves unbiased constraints on $H_0$, while allowing the gravitational-wave data to constrain the relative contribution of the two host populations. In particular, an A#-like detector network with $\mathcal{O}(10^3)$ dark sirens may be able to exclude scenarios in which the host population traces only one of the two weighting prescriptions. Dark-siren analyses may therefore provide robust cosmological measurements and new information about how merging compact binaries trace the galaxy population.

## 76. [Ultraviolet accretion continua reveal a transition in T Tauri magnetic topology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25416) <a id="link76"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.25416

**Authors:** Ana I Gomez de Castro

**Abstract:** Magnetic fields regulate accretion in T Tauri stars (TTSs) and evolve as radiative cores emerge during pre-main-sequence contraction. Stars above ~0.35-0.5 Msun transition from fully convective to partially convective interiors, a change associated with increasingly complex magnetic topologies and evolving dynamo behaviour. Because accretion flows are magnetically channelled, this evolution is expected to modify ultraviolet (UV) accretion diagnostics, although observational evidence has remained limited. We identify a transition in the UV continuum properties of TTSs using archival International Ultraviolet Explorer observations, complemented by recent spectra from the Hubble Space Telescope ULLYSES survey. An empirical UV photospheric sequence constructed from G-M main-sequence stars shows that late K-M TTSs broadly follow standard photosphere-plus-shock model predictions, whereas G-K5 stars display systematically lower F270/F303 continuum flux ratios (flux at 270 nm relative to 303 nm). Statistical analysis shows that transition models describe the observations significantly better than a single continuous trend, locating the transition near spectral type K5-K6, with its precise location limited by spectral-classification uncertainties, and consistent with the fully convective boundary. Comparison with photosphere-plus-shock models further shows that, while the late-type population is broadly encompassed by the parameter space sampled by the adopted models, the warmer stars extend systematically beyond it in this UV diagnostic. We interpret this transition as evidence for a change in magnetospheric accretion associated with the evolution of magnetic topology across the radiative-core boundary, resulting in a corresponding change in the emergent UV continuum.

## 77. [Searching for lensed gravitational waves with unlensed templates](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26080) <a id="link77"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.26080

**Authors:** Digvijay Wadekar, Barak Zackay, Mark Ho-Yeuk Cheung, Tousif Islam, Javier Roulet, Ajit Kumar Mehta, Tejaswi Venumadhav, Jonathan Mushkin, Miguel Zumalacarregui, Matias Zaldarriaga

**Abstract:** Gravitational lensing can split the signal of a binary merger into two images, which either overlap and interfere or arrive as separate events. We show that the matched-filter output of every two-image template can be straightforwardly constructed from the signal-to-noise ratio (SNR) time series that searches already compute with unlensed templates. This enables a marginalized detection statistic that increases search sensitivity without additional matched filtering and is more than 150 times faster than an explicit lensed template bank. For overlapping images in the geometric-optics regime, our two-image method recovers $\gtrsim97\%$ of the optimal SNR from point-mass and singular isothermal lenses, compared with as little as $67\%$ captured by unlensed templates, with substantial gains also for Chang-Refsdal and cored isothermal lenses. Overlapping lensed signals moreover fail the signal-consistency test that is used to down-rank noise transients. Our two-image method restores the consistency and increases the sensitive volume by roughly $2$-$4\times$ for the strongly down-ranked signals. For separated images, our method enables an efficient coherent search for pairs in which neither image is significant alone. Near fold caustics, this increases the sensitive volume by factors of 1.8-2.6 over requiring two independent detections, and up to 1.6 over a targeted counterpart search. Our method is applicable to any matched-filtering search.

## 78. [The Future of Gamma-Ray Bursts as Probes of the Epoch of Reionization in the Era of JWST and the Extremely Large Telescope](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27282) <a id="link78"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.27282

**Authors:** H. M. Fausey, A. J. van der Horst, N. R. Tanvir, K. Wiersema

**Abstract:** The Epoch of Reionization (EoR) is a key era of cosmological history that remains poorly understood. An examination of the Ly-alpha damping wing of high-redshift GRBs can provide direct measurements of the fraction of neutral hydrogen (i.e., neutral fraction) of the IGM in the vicinity of the GRB host galaxy, in addition to the neutral column in the host along the sight-line to the GRB. The latter quantity can help infer the fraction of ionizing photons escaping the galaxy (i.e. escape fraction); the average escape fraction being a key input to EoR models. New and upcoming observatories such as the JWST and the Extremely Large Telescope (ELT) can obtain more robust estimates of the hydrogen column density and neutral fraction around high-redshift GRBs. We simulate spectra for the JWST NIRSpec and ELT HARMONI spectrographs using different GRB magnitudes and exposure times to determine their capabilities for measuring column densities and neutral fractions with the Ly-alpha damping wings of high-redshift GRBs. We find that both spectrographs will be highly effective for obtaining column density estimates. Despite the presence of telluric lines, we find that ELT HARMONI will be a powerful instrument for retrieving accurate neutral fraction estimates, but JWST NIRSpec will be vital for obtaining neutral fraction measurements for bright GRBs that fall in redshift ranges where ELT is less effective. Additionally, shorter JWST Target of Opportunity (ToO) follow-up times would greatly increase the accuracy of neutral fraction estimates obtained with NIRSpec spectra.

## 79. [20,000 Rotation Periods in Nearby Open Clusters and Moving Groups from ZTF and ASAS-SN](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27113) <a id="link79"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.27113

**Authors:** Yuxi Lu

**Abstract:** Open clusters provide uniquely powerful laboratories for studying stellar evolution because their members share a common age and chemical composition. Stellar rotation of low-mass GKM dwarfs in open clusters provides direct empirical constraints on magnetic braking, with implications for stellar interiors, magnetic activity, and star--planet interactions. However, existing cluster rotation samples are dominated by space-based observations, whose limited spatial coverage and short observing baselines can make it difficult to construct an unbiased sample. Long-baseline ground-based time-domain surveys therefore provide an important complement to space-based photometry. I present a catalog of $\sim$20,000 periodic photometric signals, including stellar rotations, eclipsing-binaries, and classical pulsators, measured from the Zwicky Transient Facility (ZTF) and the All-Sky Automated Survey for Supernovae (ASAS-SN) for members of nearby open clusters and moving groups within 1.5 kpc. This catalog provides a new empirical foundation for studying the mass, chemical composition, and age dependence of stellar angular-momentum evolution and for testing models of magnetic braking across a broad range of stellar masses and evolutionary states.

## 80. [Beyond the dust: JWST reveals extinction and ice content in local (U)LIRGs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24069) <a id="link80"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.24069

**Authors:** G. Speranza, M. Pereira-Santaella, I. García-Bernete, E. González-Alfonso, S. Arribas, I. Lamperti, M. Perna, A. Alonso-Herrero, L. Hermosa Muñoz, D. Rigopoulou, N. Thatte

**Abstract:** We investigate the spatially resolved extinction and ice column densities in four nearby interacting (U)LIRGs using JWST/NIRSpec integral field spectroscopy (R$\sim$2700; 0.97--5.14 $μ$m). This wide range encompasses numerous (>25) H recombination and H$_2$ ro-vibrational emission lines, as well as the prominent H$_2$O ($\sim$3.02 $μ$m) and CO$_2$ ($\sim$4.26 $μ$m) ice absorption features. We develop three methods to derive phase-specific extinction for ionized (A$_{\text K}^{\text HII}$) and hot molecular gas (A$_{\text K}^{\text H_2}$), and H$_2$O and CO$_2$ ice column densities (N$_{\text H_2O}$ and N$_{\text CO_2}$), exploiting H and H$_2$ emission lines and ice absorption features. Nuclear regions (0.2" radius) exhibit higher extinction and ice column densities than extended regions, with median A$_{\text K}^{\text HII}$ and A$_{\text K}^{\text H_2}$ of 1.25 and 0.75 in the nuclei, compared to 0.61 and 0.50 in the extended regions. The ionized gas is more extincted than the hot molecular gas, with A$_{\text K}^{\text HII}\approx1.5$ A$_{\text K}^{\text H_2}$, while H$_2$O ice column densities are comparable between phases. In some nuclei, continuum-derived N$_{\text H_2O}$ reaches $\sim10^{18.81}$ cm$^{-2}$, likely due to compact obscured components. These results suggest that ionized gas originates in more deeply embedded regions, whereas water ice envelopes both gas phases, with additional ice-rich material around the most obscured nuclei. The median N$_{\text CO_2}$/N$_{\text H_2O}$ abundance ratio is $\sim0.10$, a factor of 2--3 below typical Galactic values.

Vinciguerra, M. von Wietersheim-Kramsta, L. Wang, A. H. Wright, H. W. Yeung, D. Scott

**Abstract:** We present a simulation framework for Euclid galaxy clustering measurements that reproduces the essential behaviour of the spectroscopic reduction chain, bypassing the computation required by full pixel-level simulations. The framework comprises two complementary tools, FastSpec, which simulates 1-dimensional slitless spectra with a simplified instrument and noise model, and Pypelid, which computes detection probabilities on input catalogues, accounting for the survey dithering sequence and spatially varying noise. We develop calibrated prescriptions for the survey selection function, redshift errors, and redshift interloper populations. Applied to the Euclid Wide Survey, the baseline selection yields a redshift purity of 74% and a sample completeness of 45%, which improves with the use of Euclid photometric colours. The redshift error distribution is found to have a Gaussian core of width $σ_z = 9.4 \times 10^{-4}$, with no significant trend over $0.9 < z < 1.8$. Emission lines [OIII]5008 and [SIII]9530 misidentified as H$α$ contribute a few to ten per cent depending on redshift, while catastrophic redshift measurement failures dominate at low signal-to-noise ratio. Simulating the Data Release 1 footprint, we find that complex survey selection effects can be corrected with a forward-modelled random catalogue, recovering the monopole and quadrupole of the galaxy 2-point correlation function. The simplified noise model does not include residual contamination from overlapping spectra, or other artefacts seen in early Euclid data; this will be revisited with DR1 data. The framework provides an efficient route for characterising the Euclid spectroscopic selection function at the precision required for galaxy clustering science, and is the basis for systematic error assessments in Euclid galaxy clustering analyses.

## 82. [Joint Fit of Galaxy Cluster Scaling Relations from Individual Weak Lensing Data: Comparison with Stacked Analysis and a Real Data Test](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26492) <a id="link82"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.26492

**Authors:** André Z. Vitorelli, Eduardo S. Cypriano

**Abstract:** Stacking is the standard route to signal-to-noise in cluster weak lensing, but it destroys the cluster-by-cluster information that scaling-relation studies need. We instead fit every cluster's individual $ΔΣ$ profile simultaneously, tying its mass and concentration to its own richness, redshift and magnitude gap, and compare with two stacked analyses of the same data: a forward-modelled stack sharing the same model and priors, and the standard two-step analysis. The magnitude gap (the BCG-to-brightest-satellite magnitude difference) enters the concentration, which the fossil-group picture ties to earlier assembly and more relaxed systems. On mock populations with triaxial haloes and miscentering, at DES Y3-like and LSST-like density, all three analyses recover the injected gap slope $γ_{\mathrm{cm}}$ with uncertainties that match the scatter between realisations, but not equally precisely: the joint fit scatters 1.2--1.3 times less than the forward-modelled stack, and 2 (DES-like) to 6.5 (LSST-like) times less than the two-step analysis, which is also biased in several other scaling-relation parameters. Applied to WaZP clusters with DES Y3 shears, the joint and forward-modelled fits agree on the gap coupling once the mass at fixed richness also depends on the gap, $γ_{\mathrm{cm}} = 0.065 \pm 0.033$ (forward-modelled) and$0.007^{+0.032}_{-0.033}$~mag$^{-1}$ (joint); the joint value is consistent with no coupling, so neither is a detection. The mass--concentration slope is consistent with zero in both, as expected from the upturn of the \citet{diemer2019} relation over this mass range. A gap coupling obtained in a model where the gap enters only the concentration need not survive a modest extension of that model.

## 83. [The evolution of 3C 306 in the galaxy cluster RXCJ1454.4+1622: linking spectral ageing, ICM conditions, and jet dynamics through MHD simulations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24097) <a id="link83"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.24097

**Authors:** A. S. R. Antas, A. Caproni, L. Loinard, G. S. Souza, T. F. Laganá

**Abstract:** We present a methodology for analysing radio sources in galaxy clusters by combining multi-frequency radio observations, X-ray analysis of the intracluster medium (ICM), and three-dimensional magnetohydrodynamic (MHD) simulations, and apply it to the galaxy cluster RXCJ1454.4+1622 and its associated FR II radio galaxy 3C 306 (IC 4516). The radio emission exhibits a curved structure connecting the lobes, likely indicating a complex dynamical evolution influenced by environmental effects. Spectral index and break-frequency maps reveal a relatively homogeneous radiative ageing distribution, with characteristic spectral ages of $\sim220$ Myr and a smooth spatial gradient. From equipartition arguments, we estimate an equivalent jet magnetic field of $2.1 \pm 0.3\,μ$G, consistent with values observed in FR II radio galaxies. These observational constraints are used to initialise non-relativistic MHD simulations exploring three scenarios: jet precession, bulk motion of the source through the ICM, and a combined model including both effects. Neither pure precession nor pure source motion alone fully reproduces the observed morphology, whereas the combined model provides a more consistent representation of the jet curvature and lobe asymmetries. Nevertheless, the simulations indicate that source motion is the dominant mechanism shaping the large-scale radio morphology, with jet precession playing a secondary role. The inferred source velocity ($\sim280$ km s$^{-1}$) and precession timescale ($\sim250$ Myr) are compatible with the spectral ageing constraints. These results suggest that the morphology of 3C 306 is influenced by the interplay between jet precession and dynamical interaction with the ICM, highlighting the role of environmental effects in radio-galaxy evolution in clusters.

## 84. [Galaxy bias of radio AGN and star-forming galaxies from MIGHTEE and CMB lensing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23567) <a id="link84"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.23567

**Authors:** Joel Hamlett, Catherine L. Hale, Matt J. Jarvis, David Alonso, S. Lyla Jung, Natalia Stylianou, Rohan G. Varadaraj

**Abstract:** Radio galaxies play a significant role in galaxy evolution through feedback mechanisms, making it important to characterize the environments in which they reside. We measure the cross-correlation between the positions of radio sources in the MIGHTEE survey and cosmic microwave background lensing measurements from the Atacama Cosmology Telescope over $20\ {\rm deg}^2$ to flux densities $S_{\rm 1.4\,GHz}>40\ {\rm μJy}$, detecting the signal at $\sim4σ$ significance. By combining this with auto-correlation measurements of low- and high-redshift radio samples, we constrain the redshift evolution of the galaxy bias parameter, $b(z)$. Fitting different models for the redshift evolution of the bias, we find that both a model inversely proportional to the linear growth factor, $b(z) = (1.63^{+0.19}_{-0.22})/D(z)$, and a model where bias grows linearly with redshift, $b(z) = (0.49^{+0.44}_{-0.32}) + (1.62^{+0.37}_{-0.51})z$, provide comparably good fits to the data, with the current measurements unable to distinguish between them. Using a radio luminosity function, we estimate the fraction of sources that are active galactic nuclei (AGNs) or star-forming galaxies (SFGs) as a function of redshift, and model the individual contributions of AGNs and SFGs to the total bias. This approach allows us to measure the bias of AGNs and SFGs without individual source classifications. Though no single model is favoured significantly, the best-fitting model gives $b_{\rm AGN}(z) = (2.60^{+0.65}_{-0.91})/D(z)$ compared to $b_{\rm SFG}(z) = (0.52^{+0.47}_{-0.34})/D(z)$. Incorporating SFG bias measurements from previous work as a prior tightens these constraints to $b_{\rm AGN}(z) = (1.96^{+0.37}_{-0.40})/D(z)$ and $b_{\rm SFG}(z) = (0.90^{+0.06}_{-0.06})/D(z)$.
