## 85. [Granulation in Color: The Chromatic Stellar Flicker of LTT-9779 and WASP-121](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24550) <a id="link85"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.24550

**Authors:** William C. Waalkes, Nadiia Kostogryz, Valeriy Vasilyev, Rafael Luque, Alexander Shapiro, Jordi Blanco-Pozo, Ana Glidden, Jeff Valenti, Sami Solanki, Ignasi Ribas, Sara Seager, Laurent Gizon

**Abstract:** Granulation is a persistent feature of stars with outer convective layers and is the photospheric signature of near-surface convective energy transport. In disk-integrated observations, it produces stochastic brightness variations that contribute substantially to stellar variability on timescales of minutes to hours. High-precision, observations with JWST provide an opportunity to study the chromatic signatures of stellar granulation. This work aims to measure the amplitude and characteristic timescale of granulation-induced variability for solar-type stars and to investigate whether the granulation signal shows significant chromatic variation in the near-infrared. We accessed publicly available JWST/NIRISS SOSS time-series observations of LTT-9779 (G7V) and WASP-121 (F6V) and de-trended the light curves by fitting the transits, eclipses, and planetary phase curves. We computed power spectra from the de-trended light curves and fit them with a Harvey granulation profile. We first analyzed the broadband light curves to measure and fix the granulation timescale and then repeated the power-spectrum analysis in individual wavelength channels to measure the chromatic dependence of the granulation amplitude. For LTT-9779, we measure a broadband granulation amplitude of σ$=53.8^{+4.1}_{-4.0}$ppm at a timescale of τ$=4.8^{+1.5}_{-1.3}$min. For WASP-121, we measure σ$=59.3\pm4.3$ppm and τ$=6.3^{+2.1}_{-1.8}$min. We also measure peak oscillation frequencies of ν$=3382^{+166}_{-191}μ$Hz and $1649^{+55}_{-69}μ$Hz in the bandpass-integrated power spectra of LTT-9779 and WASP-121, respectively. In the spectroscopic fits, we find $σ$ decreases with wavelength. Our results demonstrate a new observational window on short-timescale stellar variability and provides a way to characterize chromatic stellar noise in transit light curves.

## 86. [The GAGE survey. I. Deep multi-band stellar variability characterization and automated classification toward Baade's window](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23326) <a id="link86"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.23326

**Authors:** M. Gholami, M. DallOra, A. Luna, J. Olivares Carvajal, S. Leccia, M. Marengo, G. Bono, V. Braga, A. Calamida, G. De Somma, G. Fiorentino, M. Marconi, D. Minniti, M. Monelli, I. Musella, V. Ripepi, P. B. Stetson, M. Brescia, R. Bonito, M. Di Criscienzo, M. Fabrizio, M. Jones, R. Molinaro, S. Nedhath, C. Salvo-Guajardo, M. Sanchez-Benavente, M. Tantalo

**Abstract:** Context. Long-term photometric surveys of the Galactic bulge are vital for characterizing its stellar populations and structure, providing a foundation for next-generation facilities. Aims. We present initial results of the GalActic bulGe with plEiadi (GAGE) project, focusing on variable star discovery in Baade's Window. Methods. Using archival DECam (ugriz) data on the Blanco 4-m Telescope spanning six years, we applied high-precision PSF photometry. We developed a pipeline combining Generalized Lomb-Scargle analysis and polynomial light-curve modelling to derive mean multi-band magnitudes and amplitudes. Results. Quality cuts yield 6,304 high-confidence variable sources. We expand multi-band coverage for 4,568 sources in common with OGLE IV and uncover 1,736 (~28%) new candidates absent from OGLE IV. Using a Random Forest classifier, we categorize these sources, finding that 1,391 were also missing from both Gaia DR3 and VVV. This population is concentrated at the fainter end (i >= 17.5 mag), enabled by our deeper detection limits. Conclusions. Our pipeline effectively probes deep, highly crowded fields, expanding the variable star inventory of the inner Galaxy as a benchmark for Rubin-LSST.

## 87. [Competitive roles of turbulence and magnetic field on the formation of unipolar outflows in isolated molecular cloud cores](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26176) <a id="link87"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.26176

**Authors:** Daisuke Takaishi, Yusuke Tsukamoto, Miyu Kido, Shigehisa Takakuwa, Yoshiaki Misugi, Yuki Kudoh, Yasushi Suto

**Abstract:** We investigate the impact of turbulence and magnetic field on the formation of unipolar outflows in the gravitational collapse of isolated low-mass cloud cores using three-dimensional non-ideal magnetohydrodynamics simulations. In the models explored in this study, we find that unipolar outflows are formed in cloud cores with the super-Alfvénic turbulence of $M_{\rm{A}}>1$, where $M_{\rm{A}}$ is the Alfvén Mach number of the initial cloud core. In contrast, bipolar outflows are formed in cloud cores with $M_{\rm{A}}\lesssim1$. Our results suggest that the relative strength of the turbulence to the magnetic field in the initial cloud core plays a key role in the formation of unipolar outflows. We also find that no outflow is formed in cloud cores with the strong magnetic field of the dimensionless mass-to-flux ratio $μ=2$. The outflow morphologies can be used as tracers to indirectly estimate the relative strengths of the turbulence and magnetic field of the parent cloud cores.

## 88. [A Hectospec Spectroscopic Census of Kinematic Members in the Orion Nebula Cluster](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23636) <a id="link88"></a>

**Authors:** Jesús Hernández, Ezequiel Manzo-Martínez, Lucía Adame, Sergio Sánchez-Sanjuán, Nuria Calvet, David Fernandez, Cesar Briceno, Javier Serna, Giovanni Pinzón, Lucia Martinez, Tom Megeath, John J. Tobin, Carlos Román-Zuñiga, Karina Maucó, Corina Zepeda, Andrea Bonilla-Barroso, Maria Gracia Batista, Javier Ballesteros-Paredes, Lori Allen, Luisa Zamudio-Ruvalcaba, Fabian Ahumada, Alejandro Alfonso, Johana Contreras, Jhordan Peña

**Abstract:** We present a comprehensive spectroscopic census of the Orion Nebula Cluster (ONC), based primarily on low-resolution optical spectroscopy obtained with MMT/Hectospec. We derive spectral types, youth indicators, and accretion diagnostics for 1513 stars located in the ONC region, enabling the identification and characterization of pre-main-sequence stars. We combine these measurements with a kinematic membership analysis based on Gaia Data Release 3 astrometry to identify and confirm cluster members. Using these combined diagnostics, we identify and characterize 780 bona fide T Tauri stars associated with the ONC. In addition, we construct a census of protoplanetary discs using infrared photometry obtained with the IRAC and MIPS instruments aboard the Spitzer Space Telescope. We further derive stellar masses, ages, and extinctions using the MassAge code, which combines photometric and spectroscopic inputs with stellar evolutionary models, and employs a Monte Carlo approach for uncertainty propagation. The resulting homogeneous catalogue of ONC members provides spectroscopic characterizations, kinematic membership constraints, disc diagnostics, and self-consistent estimates of stellar physical parameters. This dataset offers a refined view of the stellar population of the ONC and provides a robust foundation for investigating its kinematic structure, age distribution, and protoplanetary disc population.

**Abstract:** Following recent observations of unexpectedly massive galaxies at high redshift by the James Webb Space Telescope, the revision of Dark Matter (DM) models --specifically regarding how DM couples to gravity-- has become a central topic in Cosmology. In this paper, we develop an axion model for Cold Dark Matter (CDM) with a non-minimal coupling to gravity. From this framework, we evaluate the gravitational instability in the hydrodynamic limit. ## 90. [$N$-body simulations of the open cluster M67 -- I: global cluster properties](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22946) <a id="link90"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.22946

**Authors:** Jasmine S. M. Anderson-Baldwin, Jarrod R. Hurley

**Abstract:** The most recent Gaia data releases have seen the construction of many open cluster catalogues containing thousands of open clusters. Simulations of these clusters are needed in order to interpret observations, both existing and upcoming. M67 is an ideal open cluster to calibrate these simulations due to its advanced age and large, diverse binary populations. Moreover it is one of the most extensively observed open clusters with observations in several wavelengths. We aim to reproduce the global cluster properties of M67 with modern $N$-body simulations, as well as comparing its observed properties in the largest homogeneous open cluster catalogue with other available observations. We carry out several NBODY6++GPU simulations with varying initial conditions, including metallicity, half-mass radius, and the distribution of orbital binary parameters. Our models broadly reproduce the observed global properties of M67, and we are able to recreate the cluster's surface brightness profile, radial mass profile, and colour-magnitude diagram. Our favoured model indicates an initial half-mass radius of around 1.6 pc, although its total observable cluster mass may be too high. Further simulations are planned for a subsequent study, with a focus on recreating the present-day binary populations of M67.

## 91. [SN 2025wiu: A Type Ib Supernova with Post-Peak Optical Rebrightening](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23414) <a id="link91"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.23414

**Authors:** Cheng-Han Lai, Ting-Wan Chen, Yu-Hao Zhang, Liang-Duan Liu, Jesper Sollerman, Amar Aryan, Anirban Dutta, Sheng Yang, Christoffer Fremling, Matthew J. Graham, David Hale, K. -Ryan Hinds, Mansi M. Kasliwal, Frank J. Masci, Reed L. Riddle, Sam Rose, Ben Rusholme, Niharika Sravan

**Abstract:** We present optical photometric and optical/near-infrared spectroscopic observations of SN 2025wiu, a Type Ib supernova (SN) at z = 0.046927. The r-band light curve exhibits an initial maximum of approximately -18.2 mag, followed by a weaker optical rebrightening reaching approximately -17.8 mag at +38 days in the rest frame relative to the first maximum. However, the reconstructed bolometric light curve does not show a distinct second peak, but instead exhibits a reduced decline rate during the corresponding phase. The spectra consistently show Type Ib features without narrow emission components associated with circumstellar material. The late-time bolometric decline rate of 0.054 +/- 0.008 mag/day is faster than expected for complete trapping of 56Co decay. A radioactive-decay model for the first peak requires nickel mass = 0.47 solar masses, above the range typical of SNe Ib. A combined radioactive-decay and circumstellar-interaction model reproduces the full bolometric light curve with nickel mass ~ 0.38 solar masses, ejecta mass ~ 4.7 solar masses, and an effective circumstellar mass of ~ 0.10 solar masses, with the interaction contributing from the first peak onward. The lack of interaction signatures in the spectra favours an aspherical, disk-like circumstellar structure engulfed by the ejecta, in which the interaction energy is thermalized below the ejecta photosphere; the fitted circumstellar parameters are therefore effective values rather than direct measurements of the circumstellar geometry. SN 2025wiu demonstrates that optical rebrightening does not necessarily imply a genuine bolometric secondary maximum and highlights the importance of multi-band observations for identifying the powering mechanisms of double-peaked stripped-envelope supernovae.

## 92. [Constraints on the Progenitor of SN 2023bee from its Nebular-Phase Spectra](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25674) <a id="link92"></a>

**Authors:** Sophie von Coelln, Qinan Wang, Matthew R. Siebert, Ping Chen, Armin Rest, Luc Dessart, Yossef Zenati, Georgios Dimitriadis, Katie Auchettl, Siyuan Chen, Kyle W. Davis, Thomas de Boer, Ryan J. Foley, Avishay Gal-Yam, Willem B. Hoogendam, Mark E. Huber, Luca Izzo, Charles D. Kilpatrick, Chien-Cheng Lin, Chang Liu, Thomas Bernard Lowe, Gautham Narayan, César Rojas-Bravo, Aidan Sedgewick, Koji Shukawa, Richard J. Wainscoat

**Abstract:** We present late-time photometry (~100-400 days after peak) and nebular-phase optical/NIR spectroscopy (100-360 days) of the Type Ia supernova (SN Ia) SN 2023bee. This SN Ia was observed TESS immediately before and after explosion and showed a relatively weak early excess light-curve feature detected in multiple UV and optical bands. Possible explanations for this early excess feature include interaction between the SN ejecta and a non-degenerate companion, or the decay of radioactive isotopes synthesized near the ejecta surface in a sub-$\text{M}_\text{Ch}$ double-detonation scenario. However, the degeneracies and incompleteness of existing models have so far prevented a definitive conclusion about its progenitor system. Nebular-phase spectra provide a complementary probe of SN Ia progenitors by constraining stripped H/He-rich material and iron-group abundances sensitive to the progenitor white dwarf density. We report the non-detection of narrow H/He features in nebular phase optical/NIR spectra of SN 2023bee, placing upper limits of $m^{\text{stripped}}_\text{H}\lesssim5.0\times10^{-3}\text{ M}_\odot$ and $m_\text{He}^{\text{stripped}}\lesssim1.2\times10^{-4}\text{ M}_\odot$. These limits strongly disfavor a non-degenerate companion and suggest that companion interaction is unlikely to explain the early excess of SN 2023bee. We also performed line-diagnostic measurements on the 7200 Åcomplex using multi-Gaussian fitting and continuum estimation, with the inferred velocities validated against NIR spectrum at the similar phase. The resulting low Ni-to-Fe mass ratio, $m_\text{Ni}/m_\text{Fe}\lesssim 0.06$, is broadly consistent with a sub-$M_{\text{Ch}}$ double-detonation progenitor system.

## 93. [Activity Cycles in Solar-Type Stars](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25306) <a id="link93"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.25306

**Authors:** P. D. Colombo, C. A. Peralta, A. P. Buccino, C. G. Oviedo, R. Ibañez Bustos, C. F. Martinez, M. Flores Trivigno, F. Minotti, P. Mauas

**Abstract:** Systematic observational studies, mainly focused on solar-type stars in the F to early K range, have detected magnetic activity cycles analogous to the solar one. When analyzing the relationship between the length of these cycles and stellar rotation rate, a non-monotonic dependence is observed. Activity periods are discretely distributed into two distinct branches: inactive and active. Intriguingly, some stars with rotation rates faster than the Sun exhibit two coexisting activity cycles, with periods simultaneously falling on both the inactive and active branches. In this work, we analyze the time series of the Mount Wilson index of a set of 44 stars, covering a range of rotation periods (sub-solar, solar, and super-solar), to identify single or double activity cycles. We detected activity cycles in 19 of the analyzed stars, with 7 of these exhibiting double-cycle behavior. In a complementary approach, we also analyze the activity cycles simulated for identical solar twins with rotation periods both shorter and longer than the Sun.

**Authors:** F. Quispe-Huaynasi, F. Roig, V. Loaiza-Tacuri, V. M. Placco, V. Marra, F. Jiménez-Esteban, A. del Pino, B. Anguiano, J. A. Alzate-Trujillo, S. Daflon, C. López-Sanjuan, P. Cruz, A. Sodré, J. Varela, H. Vázquez-Ramió, J. Zaragoza-Cardiel

**Abstract:** The selection of candidates belonging to rare or underrepresented stellar populations, as well as novel objects, is essential for spectroscopic follow-up. In this work, we demonstrate how combining broad- and narrow-band photometric information with machine-learning techniques enables this selection. Using photometric data in twelve filters from the Javalambre Photometric Local Universe Survey (J-PLUS) as input, we applied an anomaly detection technique based on autoencoders to identify spectral energy distributions (SEDs) that deviate from the dominant pattern in the J-PLUS objects sample. Subsequently, we applied the t-distributed stochastic neighbor embedding (t-SNE) algorithm to the J-PLUS colors of the outlier SEDs population. Combining information from the SIMBAD database and other astronomical surveys to classify the outliers identified by the autoencoder, we are able to characterize over-densities in the t-SNE map. Our study specifically focuses on well-defined islands in the t-SNE space, corresponding to specific populations. We identified a large sample of white dwarfs and low-mass stars, including a small subset of polluted white dwarfs, and report potential candidates for spectroscopic follow-up, including 19 carbon-rich stars, 11 cataclysmic variables and 170 white-dwarfs-main-sequence binaries, all previously unreported in the literature.

**Abstract:** The G4Jy Sample consists of 1,863 of the brightest sources in the southern sky, as selected at 151 MHz using the GaLactic and Extragalactic All-sky MWA (GLEAM) survey. We supplement existing, low-frequency radio data by completing visual inspection of radio images from the Rapid ASKAP Continuum Survey (RACS) at 887.5, 1367.5 and 1655.5 MHz. Doing so on a source-by-source basis allows us to assess the quality of the association of the radio emission, which we indicate via a flag system. Having multiple radio flux-densities allows us to construct broadband radio spectral energy distributions (SEDs), and these extend up to 20 GHz for sources that also have data from the Australia Telescope Compact Array (ATCA). We fit polynomial functions to these SEDs, and determine the distribution of sources that are best-fit by absorption models, these accounting for 11 per cent of the sample. The SEDs are further modelled using the Radio AGN in Semi-analytic Environments (RAiSE) framework, for three assumed density-profiles (set by typical halo masses of 10^12.5, 10^13.5, and 10^14.5 Msol). The derived source ages show the expected distribution in the radio-power--size (P--D) diagram, whilst derived jet powers show non-linear behaviour. The latter is believed to be the result of higher densities, associated with higher magnetic fields, restricting the expansion of the radio jets/lobes.

## 96. [Possible detection of Baryon Acoustic Oscillations imprinted in the HI sources from the FASHI catalog](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23564) <a id="link96"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.23564

**Authors:** Felipe Avila, Camila Franco, Armando Bernui

**Abstract:** Motivated by curiosity as to whether the observed Universe at low redshifts is well described by the concordance cosmological model, we investigate the large scale angular clustering of extragalactic neutral hydrogen HI sources in the local Universe. Using the second data release of the FAST All Sky HI Survey (FASHI), we analyze a sample of 142,204 HI sources up to $z<0.09$, with a median redshift of $\bar{z} = 0.034$, covering $\sim 40\%$ of the sky. We compute the 2-point angular correlation function, $w(θ)$, and estimate its covariance using an analytical Gaussian model. We report the possible detection of a broad angular Baryon Acoustic Oscillation (BAO) signal, peaking near $50^\circ$. A comparison between a 5th-order polynomial fit and a quadratic baseline yields $Δχ^2 = 9.06$ for $Δ\mathrm{dof} = 3$ (difference of 3 degrees of freedom), corresponding to a nominal $1.9\,σ$ significance for a non-monotonic feature in $w(θ)$ relative to the monotonic quadratic baseline, although this result alone does not establish a BAO detection. Our analyses show that, due to the projection effect, the expected transverse BAO scale shifts from $64^\circ$ in an infinitesimally thin redshift bin to $52^\circ$ in a bin of thickness $Δz = 0.09$, like the data we study, a value close to where our detection peaks, i.e., $\sim 50^\circ$. Furthermore, comparisons with an ensemble of lognormal mocks show that the observed broad bump is consistent with statistical fluctuations at the $1σ$ level, meaning that current uncertainties prevent us from unequivocally establishing it as a cosmological signal. Comprehensively, our study establishes a valuable observational baseline for wide-field HI surveys, showing the potential to trace primordial acoustic features even in the low redshift regime, where the transverse BAO signal is dominated by projection effects.

## 97. [Upper bounds on the number of images produced by point-mass lenses at different distances](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25594) <a id="link97"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.25594

**Authors:** J. An

**Abstract:** If a light source is sequentially lensed by $n$ intervening point-mass lenses at distinct distances, the number of isolated images for a generic source position cannot be greater than $2\cdot 3^{n-1}$. If lensing shear is present in each lens plane, the image counts could be as large as $4^n$, however. For a general multi-plane point-mass lens system, we also devise the recurrence for the BKK bound on the image counts, which is strictly less than Perry's bound due to the resultant degree for $n\ge 2$. None the less, this bound is still quadratic in the number of lenses on each plane, so is likely to overestimate the true sharp bound, which is expected to grow linearly with the number of lenses.

## 98. [The Solar System through dust maps. Encounter statistics with interstellar gas](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26063) <a id="link98"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.26063

**Authors:** E. Maconi, J. Alves, S. Hutschenreuter, S. Ratzenböck, M. Pantaleoni, C. Zucker, C. Levy, G. Edenhofer

**Abstract:** During its orbit around the Milky Way, the Solar System crosses different environments, including dense ISM regions. These encounters increase dust influx, leave geological signatures, and affect the heliosphere. For the local ISM, we estimate crossing frequencies and durations, the fraction of time spent above gas density thresholds, and how Solar vertical motion and ISM stratification influence these statistics. We quantify gas influx and dust exposure. We use Gaia-based 3D dust maps of the Solar neighborhood, sampled by integrating the Solar System along its Galactic orbit while rigidly translating the maps. From the resulting density time series, encounter statistics and fluxes are derived. We find that the Solar System spends most of its time in low density environments, while crossings of dense gas become rarer with increasing density threshold. For nH>=1cm^-3, the crossing frequency is above 600Gyr^-1, whereas thresholds around 200cm^-3 are crossed once per Gyr. Individual crossings last tens of kyr and become shorter at higher density thresholds. The inferred durations soon become nearly constant, reflecting the finite spatial resolution of the maps. The Sun spends 18 to 47% of the sampled time above 1cm^-3, and 1 to 10% above 10cm^-3. The residence fraction can be reproduced by combining the vertical volume filling factor of gas with the fraction of time spent by the Sun at each height. Within the current vertical range sampled by the Sun, we find no clear enhancement of crossing statistics during midplane passages, suggesting that these do not necessarily lead to periodic perturbations of the Solar System. About 50% of the gas influx is contributed by gas with nH<=3-4cm^-3, and about 90% by gas with nH<=48cm^-3. Our results provide a first order, data driven estimate of ISM encounter statistics, gas influx, and dust exposure along the Solar System's Galactic orbit.

## 99. [Tidal Disruption Events in Active Galactic Nuclei: Thermal Radiation and Jets](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24849) <a id="link99"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.24849

**Authors:** Nimrod Tripto, Tsvi Piran, Julian Krolik

**Abstract:** Over the past decade, dozens of Tidal Disruption Events (TDEs) have been identified, with many more expected from upcoming surveys. A small subset has been observed to launch transient, highly luminous relativistic jets, for which the origin of the requisite magnetic flux has not been identified. A non-negligible fraction of TDEs are expected in Active Galactic Nuclei (AGNs), where a magnetized accretion flow is already present. As shown first by Chan et al. (2019), the collision of a TDE debris stream with this flow can substantially alter both disk and debris dynamics, leading to a possibly Eddington-limited flare. Here, we use the Athena++ radiation magnetohydrodynamic (RMHD) code to simulate the time near peak fallback rate of a TDE in an AGN, studying the debris-disk interaction, the radiation emitted, and the accumulation of magnetic flux near the horizon. In the example simulated, the accretion rate climbs to $\sim 12\,\dot{M}_{ \rm Edd}$, while the escaping solid angle-integrated luminosity approaches $\sim L_{ \rm Edd}$. The photon luminosity is strongly asymmetric, brighter in the hemisphere from which the debris strikes the disk and dimmer in the hemisphere toward which the debris travels. The photon spectrum peaks in the EUV. The polar magnetic flux grows $\sim 33$-fold, enough to raise the Blandford--Znajek luminosity by a factor $\sim 10^3$, reaching a level sufficient to power observed TDE jets. The polar magnetic flux is also asymmetric, likely making any jet similarly asymmetric, but in the opposite sense to the photon flux.

**Novelty:** 6

## 100. [A Unified Solution to the Low-Mass X-ray Binary Diversity Puzzle: Saturated Magnetic Braking Coupled with Irradiation-Driven Winds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22816) <a id="link100"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.22816

**Authors:** Zhu-Ling Deng, Xiang-Dong Li, Bo Wang, Philipp Podsiadlowski, Yong Shao, Dong-Dong Liu, Zhan-Wen Han

**Abstract:** The standard magnetic braking (MB) model has long failed to explain key properties of low-mass X-ray binaries (LMXBs)---including their accretion rates, the formation of ultracompact binaries, and orbital expansion---casting doubt on binary evolution and gravitational-wave progenitor predictions. Phenomenological modifications exist, but a unified, physically grounded mechanism has been missing. Here we show that the overlooked coupling between magnetic saturation and irradiation-driven winds (IDW) provides this missing framework. Accretion-powered irradiation enhances the donor wind, which in turn amplifies MB exactly where needed. Our Saturated MB + IDW (SMB+IDW) model simultaneously resolves three outstanding discrepancies with no fine-tuning: (1) it reproduces the full observed distribution of accretion rates across persistent and transient LMXBs; (2) it naturally explains ultracompact X-ray binary formation; and (3) it quantitatively matches the orbital period distribution of descendant millisecond pulsar (MSP) binaries---a critical independent test failed by all previous models. Critically, the SMB+IDW model transforms MB from an externally imposed condition into a self-consistently regulated process tied to the accretion history. By unifying LMXB evolution to MSPs in a single physical picture, our work resolves the long-standing diversity puzzle and establishes a physics-based foundation for next-generation population synthesis, gravitational-wave progenitor predictions, and compact binary interpretation across cosmic time.

**Relevance:** 1

## 101. [Modeling Multi-band Active Galactic Nuclei Lightcurves Using an Self-supervised Attentive Neural Process Network](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24195) <a id="link101"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.24195

**Authors:** Tzuken Shen, Chengcheng Xin, Joshua Fagin, Ashley Villar, Matthew J. Graham, K. E. Saavik Ford

**Abstract:** With the the Legacy Survey of Space and Time (LSST), observations of tens of millions of quasars are expected. We present a machine-learning framework based on an attentive neural process (ANP) that models stochastic quasar variability and jointly performs two tasks: (i) probabilistic reconstruction of quasar light curves in all six LSST bands, and (ii) inference of physical parameters of the AGN system --- black hole mass, Eddington ratio, and intrinsic variability timescale --- with joint, full-covariance posteriors. The model processes all six bands within a single masked attention mechanism, so that observations in any band constrain the shared driving process, and natively handles irregularly and asynchronously sampled light curves with realistic heteroscedastic noise. We train and evaluate on full ten-year light curves simulated with a relativistic accretion-disk reprocessing model, observed through the fielded Rubin baseline cadence with per-visit photometric noise. The light-curve model is trained with self-supervised, learning from the sparse noisy observations alone and never accessing the underlying noiseless light curves. On a held-out test set, we show that our ANP model reach comparable performances to that of the near-optimal baseline Gaussian process model, while exceeding it in power spectrum reconstruction and damped-random walk timescale estimation. The compact learned latent representation supports future downstream applications, and the fully observation-driven training objective enables direct fine-tuning on real LSST light curves.

## 102. [Topological Defects in Cosmic Shear](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23401) <a id="link102"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.23401

**Authors:** Jiachen Bai, Benjamin Giblin, Andy Taylor

**Abstract:** In this work, we present a novel approach for extracting cosmological information from cosmic shear maps based on topological defects in the shear orientation field. These defects are defined purely by the phase structure of the shear field and are therefore complementary to conventional weak lensing statistics that primarily probe amplitude information. We characterise their statistical properties, including number densities, spatial clustering, and the convergence distribution at defect locations, and demonstrate with Fisher analyses under idealised and realistic survey conditions that topological defect statistics provide a valuable and complementary source of cosmological information. With Euclid-like simulated data, the inclusion of defect statistics leads to constraints on $Ω_{\rm m}$ and $σ_8$ that are up to 3.9 times tighter than those obtained from the convergence two-point correlation function alone.

## 103. [Relations between optical spectral parameters and the (far-)infrared continuum in Type~1 active galactic nuclei](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23200) <a id="link103"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.23200

**Authors:** Maša Lakićević, Luka Č. Popović, Sladjana Marčeta-Mandić, Oliver Vince, Paola Marziani

**Abstract:** This study combines multi-wavelength continuum data with optical spectral properties for a far-infrared (FIR)-selected sample of Type 1 active galactic nuclei (AGNs) from AKARI data. We explore the relation between AGN physics and concomitant star formation processes using empirical correlations of optical and multiwavelength data. We measured spectral parameters including full widths at half maximum (FWHMs) and velocity shifts of broad and narrow optical emission lines, as well as continuum luminosities from GALEX FUV to AKARI 160 microns. The sample contains 88 AGNs observed at both optical and FIR wavelengths. About 50% are Seyfert 1s with both broad H$β$ and H$α$ lines; almost all remaining objects are S1.9s with only broad H$α$. The FWHMs of broad H$β$ and H$α$ are anti-correlated with luminosity in most bands. Shifts of narrow H$β$, H$α$, and [O III] 5007 lines and FWHM of [O III] 5007 correlate with optical and IR luminosities. Luminosities at 65 and 90 microns are more strongly connected with optical and other spectral properties than those at 140 and 160 microns. These trends extend the well-known Eigenvector 1 (EV1) correlations into the MIR and FIR domain. The anti-correlation between broad-line FWHMs and NIR and MIR luminosities is probably caused by the inclination of the torus and accretion disc: higher inclinations see less AGN radiation. However, most objects fall into Population A along the quasar EV1 main sequence, where narrower broad H$β$ profiles and FIR excess emission are commonly linked to higher Eddington ratios.

## 104. [Impact of cosmic evolution on the gravitational wave spectrum from cosmic strings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26652) <a id="link104"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.26652

**Authors:** Xi-He Hu, Danny Marfatia, Ye-Ling Zhou

**Abstract:** In the standard cosmological paradigm, the gravitational wave (GW) spectrum from a cosmic string network is well defined. However, early-Universe dynamics can significantly alter this relic signal. We systematically investigate how different new physics epochs in the radiation era--including extra relativistic degrees of freedom, early matter domination, intermediate vacuum domination, and kination--modify string network evolution and loop production. We find that a kination era enhances the stochastic GW signal, whereas the other scenarios suppress the signal to varying degrees. We also demonstrate that late-time dynamical dark energy has a negligible impact on the GW spectrum from cosmic strings.

**Abstract:** Primordial black holes (PBHs) formed from the collapse of curvature perturbations in the early universe are known to have an associated gravitational wave signal sourced by the same scalar perturbations. In this work, we show that there is a complementarity between these gravitational waves and modifications of the 21 cm global signal during the cosmic dark ages due to PBH evaporation. For PBHs in the range $ M_{\rm PBH} \sim 5 \times 10^{13} \, {\rm g} - 10^{17} \, {\rm g}$, there is a correlated signal in gravitational waves in the LISA, deci-Hz, and ground based regimes from $f_{\rm GW} \sim 10^{-3} \, {\rm Hz} - 20 \, {\rm Hz}$ and in the 21 cm global signal from $z \sim 300 - 30$. These observations are entirely independent of each other and within the reach of near-future experiments, making a combined measurement a novel multimessenger probe of primordial physics.

## 106. [Exploring the Evidence for Evolving Dark Energy with the Jeffreys prior](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26493) <a id="link106"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.26493

**Authors:** Joshua Layton, Alex Hall, Naomi Clare Robertson

**Abstract:** We investigate the impact of applying the uninformative Jeffreys prior in a re-analysis of DESI DR2 BAO, Planck 2018 CMB, and Type Ia Supernovae (DES-SN5YR, DES-Dovekie, Pantheon+, Union3). We incorporate this prior into new posterior inference and evidence calculations. We introduce novel techniques for interpreting the Jeffreys prior physically. To overcome the computational expense of the Jeffreys prior, we develop a fully $\texttt{JAX}$-based pipeline consisting of $\texttt{CosmoPower-JAX}$ emulators, gradient-based posterior inference using the No-U-Turn Sampler (NUTS), and evidence calculations using $\texttt{JAX}$-accelerated nested sampling. We demonstrate that while the parameter constraints on $w_0$ and $w_a$ remain robust to prior choice (exhibiting only minor shifts of the marginalised posterior towards the $\textit{Maximum A Posteriori}$ in less constraining dataset combinations), the Bayesian evidence is highly sensitive to the use of the Jeffreys prior; replacing uniform priors with the Jeffreys prior reverses the model-selection preference in dataset combinations that favour dynamical dark energy under the uniform prior, while strengthening existing preferences for $Λ$CDM. This is driven primarily by the significantly larger Occam penalty applied by the Jeffreys prior which penalises the overfitting of the data by a $w_0w_a$CDM model.

## 107. [Modular Bayesian Inference for Partially Misspecified Mixtures with Uncertain Class Labels](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27085) <a id="link107"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.27085

**Authors:** Maximilian Autenrieth, Konstantin Karchev, David A. van Dyk, Kaisey Mandel, Roberto Trotta

**Abstract:** Scientific inference often involves observations from multiple latent populations whose class memberships are uncertain and must be inferred from available data. While full Bayesian mixture modelling provides a principled approach to joint uncertainty quantification in this setting, often the model is misspecified or less trustworthy for populations that are not the target of the inference, which can distort posterior inference for the parameters of interest. We propose a modular Bayesian framework that safeguards against such biases. We decouple classification from downstream inference using fixed class probabilities, for example from a classifier trained on a subset of labelled data. We then protect inference on the parameters of interest by preventing the likelihood contributions of less trustworthy populations from influencing them. A key benefit of this principled modularization is that it greatly simplifies inference, reducing a fully joint mixture analysis to sampling label allocations, fitting the protected downstream model for each sample, and averaging the resulting posteriors. We establish calibration conditions on these class probabilities under which the proposed procedure preserves the inferential target of the analysis with known labels, and demonstrate the method in synthetic experiments and in cosmological analysis of photometric supernova samples.

## 108. [Planets surviving common envelope evolution should be rare, massive, and orbit very close to their host star](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27055) <a id="link108"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.27055

**Authors:** Yadira S. Gaibor, Andrew Vanderburg, Juliette C. Becker, Melinda Soares-Furtado, Saul Rappaport, Tansu Daylan

**Abstract:** The discovery of exoplanets around white dwarfs uniquely informs our understanding of planetary survival through stellar evolution. Common Envelope (CE) evolution is one of the proposed mechanisms that could shape the orbital architecture of these planets around stellar remnants. In this work, we predict the occurrence rate and orbital separation/planet mass distributions for planets that could form around white dwarfs through CE evolution. We simulate populations of giant-planet systems, sampling initial conditions from observed mass and semi-major axis distributions for main sequence stars. We evolve the host star from main sequence to white dwarf to assess which planetary configurations could survive, and we predict the resulting post-CE planet population. Our results suggest that such planets should be rare, massive, and have very close orbital separations. We find that most of the surviving planets have masses of $>10\,M_{\rm{Jup}}$ and orbital separations $<1.4$ times the tidal disruption radius, $a_{disrupt}$ ($\lesssim0.006$ AU, also called the Roche limit), which is in contrast to high eccentricity migration, which produces planets with orbital separations $>2a_{disrupt}$. The properties of CE survivors from our simulations contrast with the known closely-orbiting planet WD 1856+534 b, suggesting a different mechanism may be responsible for its formation.

## 109. [Intermittent turbulence as the driver of stochastic heating in the near-Sun solar wind](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24296) <a id="link109"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.24296

**Authors:** Tamar Ervin, Trevor A. Bowen, Alfred Mallet, Stuart D. Bale, Kristopher G. Klein, Mihailo Martinovic, Benjamin D. G. Chandran, Philip A. Isenberg, Orlando Romeo, Roberto Livi, Ali Rahmati, Davin Larson

**Abstract:** Understanding the mechanism by which the energy is dissipated at small scales to heat the outer corona and expanding solar wind, is a primary goal of the Parker Solar Probe (PSP) mission, and an outstanding question in heliophysics. Recent observational case studies have suggested that the dominant dissipation mechanism(s), stochastic heating (SH) or resonant heating (RH), may be modulated by the Alfvén `surface' ($M_A = 1$), while theoretical and simulation works predict that the mechanism should be modulated by the level of turbulence imbalance. We measure the local energy transfer (LET) rate from $9.8$ to $55$ solar radii from PSP observations, showing that the LET rate peaks at $M_A \sim 0.4 - 0.9$. We connect enhanced LET rates to signatures of intermittency, highlighting the key role that intermittent fluctuations play in heating the near-Sun solar wind. We compare the LET rate with estimates of the SH rate, finding that below $\sim 15$ solar radii the SH heating rate matches the LET rate provided intermittency is accounted for, regardless of $M_A$, while farther from the Sun, SH is unable to account for the observed LET rate. In contrast to predictions by recent theoretical works, this implies that `imbalanced' intermittent stochastic heating may be a viable mechanism to heat highly imbalanced, low-$β$ collisionless plasmas, and is likely an important mechanism of dissipation in the outer solar corona and near-Sun solar wind.

## 110. [Localizing binary neutron stars with the Einstein Telescope: a full Bayesian comparison of the triangular and 2L configurations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27081) <a id="link110"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.27081

**Authors:** Filippo Santoliquido, Jacopo Tissino, Marica Branchesi, Ulyana Dupletsa

**Abstract:** The Einstein Telescope (ET), the European next-generation gravitational-wave observatory, is at a decisive stage: the choice of its detector configuration is still open, with profound implications for its scientific output. We provide a quantitative basis for this choice by comparing two candidate configurations at their proposed sites: the triangular design ($Δ$) at the Euregio Meuse-Rhine site, and the 2L design, with interferometers in Sardinia, Italy, and Lusatia, Germany. We present the first realistic estimates of sky-localization uncertainties for binary neutron star (BNS) mergers, moving beyond Fisher-matrix forecasts to full Bayesian parameter estimation that captures the multimodal structure of the sky posterior. We focus mainly on the high-frequency interferometers alone, providing an expected scenario for the first years of ET operations, as the more challenging low-frequency instruments will come online later. BNS injections span different sky positions and luminosity distances between 40 and 2000 Mpc, corresponding to signal-to-noise ratios (SNRs) from 12 to 720. Our analysis relaxes the long-wavelength approximation and includes Earth's rotation and higher-order modes, all of which improve sky localization. We find that the $Δ$ configuration localizes low- and moderate-SNR sources in eight degenerate sky modes spread across both hemispheres and over the day and night sides of the Earth, with 90% credible areas reaching $\sim 2 \times 10^4 \, \mathrm{deg}^2$. In contrast, the 2L configuration recovers a single dominant mode that contains at least 50% of the posterior probability and is one to two orders of magnitude smaller in area. This represents a substantial advantage for multimessenger follow-up campaigns.

## 111. [The Imprint of Core-collapse Asymmetries on Supernova Shock Breakout](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27183) <a id="link111"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.27183

**Authors:** David K. Khatami, David Vartanyan, Hai P. Le, Daniel N. Kasen, Adam Burrows

**Abstract:** Shock breakout is the first electromagnetic signal of a supernova, generating a highly luminous and fast-timescale burst of thermal radiation. Motivated by recent theoretical progress in producing successful three-dimensional supernova explosions of massive stars, we undertake a detailed numerical study on the impact that the core-collapse asymmetries might have on the breakout signal. We perform the first fully 3D multi-group radiation hydrodynamics simulations of shock breakout for the case of a red supergiant explosion. We find that large-scale asymmetries formed during the core-collapse process can extend the breakout flash duration to days, with the timescale set by the supernova shock fully wrapping around the progenitor star. Using detailed 3D radiative transfer post-processing, we generate synthetic multi-wavelength light curves and spectra to more closely connect to observations. The light curves show a significant viewing-angle dependence of the overall shape and peak brightness of shock breakout. We find that core-collapse asymmetries have the largest impact on the breakout light curve when observed in shorter UV wavelengths. On the other hand, at optical wavelengths, we find a diminished impact of the asymmetric breakout that more closely resembles a 1D spherical breakout. Our results show how multi-dimensional effects originating from the core-collapse mechanism can play a major role in setting the shock breakout properties, and are particularly relevant to upcoming UV space missions such as ULTRASAT and UVEX.

## 112. [Andreev Beta Conversion and Bulk Viscosity in Superfluid Neutron-Star Matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23581) <a id="link112"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.23581

**Authors:** Tingyu Zhang, Honoka Hoshino, Hiroyuki Tajima, Motoi Tachibana, Mariusz Sadzikowski

**Abstract:** Bulk viscosity in neutron-star matter is attributed to the phase lag between a density oscillation and weak beta equilibration. We point out a distinct coherent channel when the baryonic component that stores the condensate is superfluid: an effective beta-conversion event between a neutron condensate and normal protons can proceed as an Andreev process, converting a particle-like excitation into a hole-like excitation while adding or removing a Cooper pair from the condensate. The resulting Andreev contribution to beta equilibration is fourth order in the effective conversion vertex, is enhanced by anomalous neutron Green's functions, and is not captured by standard Urca process. We identify the density, temperature, and oscillation-frequency regimes where this channel can dominate viscous damping of neutron-star oscillations and mergers.

## 113. [Ion Heating in Pair-loaded Relativistic Turbulence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26731) <a id="link113"></a>

**Authors:** Yusen Wang, Zhonghai Zhao, Xinyu Zhu, Xianxu Jin, Zhipeng Zhang, Bin Qiao

**Abstract:** Pair-loaded relativistic turbulence is expected in compact high-energy astrophysical environments and may sustain plasma heating. Using self-consistent three-dimensional radiative particle-in-cell simulations, we identify the mechanism of ion heating mediated by pair-enhanced radiative feedback in relativistic turbulence. We derive the theoretical criterion for the transition to a regime dominated by radiative feedback and show that pair loading promotes the growth and persistence of large, connected optically thick structures. These long-lived structures sustain intense localized ion heating, repeatedly generating prominent hot-ion clumps and substantially enhancing the mean ion temperature. Our results establish a physical link between pair creation and sustained ion heating, providing a theoretical basis for investigating intermittent hot-ion production in black-hole coronae and gamma-ray burst outflows.

## 114. [AI and The Enclosure of the Scientific Commons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27251) <a id="link114"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.27251

**Authors:** P. K. G. Williams

**Abstract:** The scientific literature can be viewed as an "information commons:" an intangible resource that "no one owns and is shared by everyone." There are well-established economic and political frameworks for understanding such commons, emerging from the history of agricultural common lands found in places like preindustrial England. This article provides a primer on the history and these frameworks, and considers a key difference between the information commons and the fields of yore: while information commons are robust and immensely valuable --- some can be said to rank among humanity's greatest achievements --- the English agricultural commons no longer meaningfully exist. The latter are gone not because of the well-known "tragedy of the commons," but rather a phenomenon known as the enclosure movement (~1600--1800) that transformed the common lands into private property. Many observers, then and now, judge enclosure to have been theft at a societal scale. This article argues that artificial intelligence (AI) provides precisely the tools needed to enable a previously-infeasible enclosure of the information commons. Although AI-enabled enclosure does not operate identically to physical enclosure, it shares the key features of turning the commons into private wealth and undermining the social systems that support them (via "slop spam"). The concepts presented in this article can provide a scholarly basis for interrogating the foreboding feeling that AI may represent not just a smash-and-grab, but an attack upon a whole way of life.

**Relevance:** 1
**Novelty:** 5

---

## 115. [The Dancing Bonfire: An Unusual Persistent X-ray Source in M81 Exhibiting Recurrent Luminous Flares](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23339) <a id="link115"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.23339

**Authors:** Jiachang Zhang, Zhiyuan Li, Xiangdong Li, Tong Bao, Ziqian Hua, ShanShan Weng

**Abstract:** Short-timescale X-ray variability provides a direct probe of unstable accretion onto compact objects, yet extragalactic X-ray binaries (XRBs) with recurrent flaring remain largely unexplored. Here we present a detailed X-ray study of M81F4 (2CXO~J095524.2+690957), a persistently luminous X-ray source towards M81 that exhibits recurrent short X-ray flares. Based on multi-mission X-ray observations spanning two decades, it is found that M81F4 remains persistently bright at $L_{0.5-8.0\rm~keV}\sim10^{38}-10^{39}\rm~erg~s^{-1}$, while producing flares lasting a few hundred seconds and reaching peak luminosities of a few $10^{39}\rm~erg~s^{-1}$, with an observed mean (minimum) recurrence time of $\sim0.5$ day ($\sim$6 ks). The stacked flare profile shows a rapid rise followed by a slower decay, but no significant hardness evolution. Spectral analysis reveals that both the flare and non-flare states are dominated by a hard power-law continuum, with marginal evidence of highly ionized Fe lines in the non-flare state spectrum. These properties disfavor classical Type I and Type II bursts and have no close analog among known XRBs. We favor a magnetized neutron star accreting from an intermediate mass donor at a persistently high rate with the flares arising from instabilities at the disk-magnetosphere boundary. If confirmed, M81F4 might represent a hidden population of XRBs in which sustained, magnetically-regulated accretion can lead to brief episodes of super-Eddington emission.

**Abstract:** Gravitational-wave observations of neutron star mergers allows us to probe the dense portion of the nuclear equation of state. ## 117. [Breaking the axion abundance-isocurvature relation: Scale invariance and high-scale inflation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24070) <a id="link117"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.24070

**Authors:** Mariaveronica De Angelis, Javier Rubio

**Abstract:** A QCD axion accounting for dark matter through pre-inflationary misalignment is strongly constrained by CMB isocurvature bounds, which place the classic axion window in tension with high-scale inflation. The tension arises because the same decay constant controls both the relic abundance and the primordial axion fluctuations. We show how this link can be broken in a broad class of scale-invariant models, without introducing any dimensionful parameter into the classical action. Rather than imposing a fixed Peccei-Quinn scale, the axion's kinetic normalisation is set by a dynamical field ratio. The resulting field-space metric controls the abundance through its value today and the primordial fluctuations through its value at horizon crossing. A sufficiently large metric during inflation suppresses isocurvature, allowing high-scale inflation in the classic axion window with an untuned misalignment angle and sub-Planckian decay constants. The exact scale and Peccei-Quinn symmetries also enforce a non-turning inflationary attractor, conserving the super-Hubble curvature perturbation and preserving the single-field adiabatic spectrum. We realise the mechanism in the Higgs-Dilaton model by deforming the Peccei-Quinn kinetic term. The same deformation turns the inflaton sector into an $α$-attractor pinned near the Starobinsky point. This explicit realisation has a super-Planckian present decay constant, which places it in the anthropic window, now reopened at high inflationary scales, with an ultralight QCD axion constrained by black-hole superradiance.

## 118. [Illuminating Light Dark Matter with Fixed-Target Experiments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27131) <a id="link118"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.27131

**Authors:** Taylor R. Gray

**Abstract:** Astronomical and cosmological observations provide overwhelming evidence for a non-luminous matter component, dark matter (DM), whose particle nature remains unknown. Experimental searches for non-gravitational DM interactions have largely targeted DM with GeV-TeV masses via nuclear recoils, yielding no discovery thus far. DM in the MeV-GeV mass range, however, is theoretically motivated, reproducing the observed relic abundance while remaining invisible to nuclear recoil searches. Such light DM is better probed via electron recoils or at fixed-target experiments. This thesis advances the theoretical and statistical toolkit for light DM searches at fixed-target experiments, spanning model building, statistical inference, global fits, and signal simulation. We survey the landscape of fixed-target searches for light DM, focusing on the Light Dark Matter eXperiment (LDMX). We also simulate DM signatures at the next-generation experiment SHiP to assess its sensitivity. Within minimal DM models, we present a likelihood-based statistical framework for LDMX using the full two-dimensional recoil electron distribution, spanning exclusion, discovery, parameter estimation, and model comparison in frequentist and Bayesian formulations. This establishes the discovery potential of LDMX, and we quantify the direct detection exposure needed to independently confirm a potential signal. Beyond this paradigm, we perform global fits of scalar and fermionic light DM, combining cosmological, astrophysical, and laboratory constraints. We then extend fixed-target searches to spin-1 DM and higher electromagnetic moment dark photons, yielding rich signatures with strong complementarity to other probes. Finally, we go beyond standard nuclear modelling of dark photon production, incorporating elastic nuclear form factors and spectral functions from many-body ab initio methods in place of analytic treatments.

**Authors:** Elena Manao, Elisa Resconi, Frank M. Rieger, Paolo Padovani, Gia Dvali

**Abstract:** We show that horizon-scale magnetic fields around supermassive black holes (SMBHs), only limited by gravity, yield a universal, model-independent ceiling on electromagnetic energy density. We identify a parameter space in accretion rate and magnetic field strength in which quiescent nuclei simultaneously satisfy the transparency conditions for Ultra-High-Energy Cosmic Rays (UHECR) escape and the field requirements for reaching Ultra-High Energies (UHE), naturally complementing photon-rich Active Galactic Nuclei (AGN), which favor neutrino production but suppress UHECR escape. We observe that, when including curvature energy losses, the energy of the primaries scales weakly as $E_{\rm curv}\!\propto\!M^{1/4}$ and allows to achieve UHE across different compositions and SMBHs masses ($10^6$--$10^{10}\,M_\odot$), without requiring extreme spins or super-Eddington accretion. A cross-correlation of the Pierre Auger Observatory (PAO) public data with galaxies hosting reliably measured SMBH masses, separating AGN from quiescent hosts, reveals marginal local excesses ($\lesssim\!2.8σ$) consistent with isotropy after correcting for the look-elsewhere effect. The framework provides a direct link between black-hole physics and the maximum energies of cosmic particles, and defines concrete observational tests for next-generation UHECR datasets.

## 120. [A Natural Dark Matter-Dark Energy Interaction from Axion-Monopole Dynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27059) <a id="link120"></a>

**Authors:** Hengameh Bagherian, Matthew Reece, Lian-Tao Wang, Huangyu Xiao

**Abstract:** We propose a natural framework for interacting dark matter and dark energy based on axion-monopole dynamics in a dark gauge sector. Dark matter is composed of dark 't Hooft-Polyakov monopoles, while dark energy is an ultralight axion whose coupling to the dark gauge field induces an axion-dependent dyon mass through the Witten effect. This generates a direct dark matter-dark energy interaction. We show that generic models with a significant dark matter-dark energy coupling suffer from a naturalness problem: the Coleman-Weinberg potential induced by the interaction is typically much larger than the dark energy scale. In our model, the corrections arise from a monodromy tower of dyon states, allowing them to be exponentially suppressed. In the minimal setup, the interaction strength is predicted to be $\sim 0.1\%$, below the sensitivity of current observations. We discuss scenarios in which cosmological population of higher-charge dyons can enhance the interaction to the percent level, producing an apparent phantom dark energy equation of state. We further show that the parameter space of the axion-monopole model preferred by fitting the Dark Energy Spectroscopic Instrument (DESI) results can also naturally explain the cosmic birefringence with a small anomaly coefficient.

## 121. [A Unified Model for the Gravitational Wave Background and Quasar Luminosity Functions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28680) <a id="link121"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.28680

**Authors:** Cayenne Matt, Kayhan Gültekin, Ryan Hickox

**Abstract:** The supermassive black hole mass function (BHMF) is a necessary ingredient in our understanding of how BHs grow and evolve with their host galaxies. Estimations of the BHMF based on observations of local BHs, quasars, and gravitational wave data yield inconsistent results, leading to large uncertainties in, e.g., BH accretion and seeding models. The nanohertz gravitational wave background (GWB) has been a powerful tool to provide constraints on the BHMF across cosmic time. We take posterior BHMFs that describe the GWB and fit them to bolometric quasar luminosity functions (QLF) across $0.2 \leq z \leq 6.2$ to constrain the Eddington ratio distribution function (ERDF). Compared to a fiducial BHMF, based on galaxy and a local black hole mass scaling relation, the BHMFs that match the GWB can reproduce the observed QLFs with lower Eddington fractions and without requiring significant super-Eddington accretion. We use a double power-law form of the ERDF and find that the fits using the GWB-inferred BHMF have systematically lower break-points and marginally steeper slopes in the high-Eddington fraction portion of the function. Furthermore, the best-fit QLFs using the GWB-inferred BHMFs provide better fits to the high-luminosity end of the QLF data than the fiducial model. For both models the ERDF shows significant redshift evolution of the break-point which reaches a maximum around $z \sim 2$. Overall, our prescription can produce realistic QLFs from BHMFs and our GWB-based model is consistent with electromagnetic and gravitational wave data sets.

## 122. [Neutrino self-interactions in light of DESI and CMB-SPA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26654) <a id="link122"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.26654

**Authors:** Hernán E. Noriega, Josue De-Santiago, Gabriela Garcia-Arroyo, Ivan Pérez-Castro, Jorge Venzor

**Abstract:** We present an updated analysis of non-standard neutrino self-interactions, where the interaction is effectively mediated by a heavy particle, using the latest available data from DESI and the CMB. The considered data include combinations of DESI DR1 full-shape galaxy clustering (FS), BAO distance measurements, Lyman-$α$ 1D power spectra (Ly-$α$), and a combined CMB likelihood (CMB-SPA) using data from Planck, ACT, and SPT. First, we probe two DESI DR1-only datasets, where the effective interaction parameter $G_{\rm eff}$ is multimodal in the profile likelihood, leading us to analyze two different regimes: moderately interacting (MI$_ν$) and strongly interacting (SI$_ν$) neutrinos. With FS+BAO, the evidence disfavors the SI$_ν$ mode, a trend that becomes slightly stronger with the inclusion of Ly-$α$. However, in the latter dataset, the MI$_ν$ region shows a bimodality in which the intermediate mode is very sharp and peaks around $G_{\rm eff\,} \simeq 10^{-3} \mathrm{MeV}^{-2}$; interestingly, this mode is not penalized versus the weaker standard-like mode. Furthermore, we test CMB and DESI joint data and show that the SI$_ν$ mode is more disfavored by CMB-SPA than by Planck-only data. In particular, the evidence against this mode becomes the strongest thus far (with odds of 5:100000) in the FS+BAO+CMB-SPA combination.

## 123. [Multiline Spectropolarimetric Evidence for Photospheric Reconnection in Ellerman Bombs from DKIST](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24049) <a id="link123"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.24049

**Authors:** Rahul Yadav, Maria D. Kazachenko, Viggo Hansteen

**Abstract:** Ellerman bombs (EBs) are compact, transient brightenings observed in the wings of Balmer lines and are believed to result from magnetic reconnection in the lower solar atmosphere. However, their signatures have rarely been observed simultaneously in multiple spectral lines sensitive to the lower atmosphere. We present novel high-resolution multiline spectropolarimetric observations of EBs obtained with the Visible Spectro-Polarimeter (ViSP) on the Daniel K. Inouye Solar Telescope (DKIST). Simultaneous observations reveal remarkable H$β$ wing enhancements extending up to $\sim$4~Å from the line core and intensity enhancements in the \ion{Na}{1}~D1 and \ion{Fe}{1}~6302 lines. Using multiline non-LTE inversion of the \ion{Na}{1} and \ion{Fe}{1} lines, we infer localized photospheric heating of up to $ΔT\sim$800~K, accompanied by weak upflows and downflows at EB locations. Comparing with synthetic observables from Bifrost radiative-MHD simulations, we find that synthetic spectra reproduce the observed H$β$ and \ion{Na}{1}~D wing enhancements and yield atmospheric stratification broadly consistent with those inferred from the non-LTE inversion. This study provides strong evidence that EBs are driven by photospheric magnetic reconnection below the temperature-minimum region, and places new constraints on the realistic EB models and small-scale energy release in the lower solar atmosphere.

## 124. [Statistical Analysis of Neutron Star Cooling: Implications for the Nuclear Equation of State and Nucleon Pairing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25427) <a id="link124"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.25427

**Authors:** Afonso Ávila, Ricardo Z. Ferreira, Violetta Sagun

**Abstract:** We perform a statistical analysis of the thermal evolution of isolated neutron stars by confronting theoretical cooling curves with luminosity-age measurements for 24 sources. For each object, we explore five purely nucleonic equations of state (APR4, FSU2R, DD2, IST, and NL3$ωρ$) combined with representative models of neutron superfluidity and proton superconductivity, while varying the gravitational mass and envelope composition. The analysis spans approximately $10^5$ cooling curves. Within this framework, we find that only the NL3$ωρ$ equation of state and, to a lesser extent, IST, both including neutron triplet pairing, provide an overall satisfactory description of the data. The remaining equations of state are in significant tension with the observations. These results demonstrate that NS cooling provides complementary constraints on dense matter, alongside nuclear structure, global NS observables, and gravitational-wave observations, while probing the interplay between the EoS and nucleon pairing.

## 125. [Prospects for submillimeter VLBI of black hole photon rings with a stratospheric balloon-borne telescope](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24396) <a id="link125"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.24396

**Authors:** Mayukh Bagchi, Dominic W. Pesce, Felix M. Thiel, Laura M. Fissel, Lindy Blackburn

**Abstract:** Submillimeter very long baseline interferometry (VLBI) achieves higher angular resolution and probes more optically thin emission near supermassive black holes, with less interstellar scattering toward Sgr A$^*$. Above 345 GHz, however, the atmosphere confines ground-based VLBI to a few high, dry sites. Using 2012-2022 weather, we forecast what a 2.5 m telescope floating at 35-40 km, a successor to the 22 GHz Balloon-borne VLBI Experiment (BVEX) pathfinder, would add to ground-VLBI arrays at 345-690 GHz. The balloon adds little baseline length but reaches higher frequencies, where a fixed baseline is longer in wavelengths. On an ALMA-balloon baseline, Sgr A$^*$ at 460 GHz is at the detection threshold, with ten or more 20 s detections on about half of the simulated April days. These detections lie beyond the polarimetric photon ring transition, where the $n=1$ ring dominates the polarized visibility amplitudes. M87$^*$ at 460 GHz would need frequency phase transfer from a 230 GHz reference to be detected. With phase transfer, most Sgr A$^*$ detections beyond the transition at 460 GHz are on balloon baselines, whose thermal noise meets the linear-polarization threshold within one 300 s scan. At 690 GHz such detections require phase transfer that stays coherent at three times the reference frequency. In Fisher forecasts, a 460 GHz ring-geometry constraint requires balloons but is weaker than at 345 GHz, where balloons add little improvement. High-resolution 460 GHz VLBI science would require a 32 GHz bandwidth backend, submillimeter receivers, and simultaneous 230 GHz reference recording at every station.

## 126. [Destinies of Plasmoids Formed from the Magnetic Reconnection Occurring within the Ergo Region of a Rotating Black Hole](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25097) <a id="link126"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.25097

**Authors:** Ho-Yun YuChih, Ye Shen

**Abstract:** It is widely accepted that magnetic reconnections occur successively in black hole accretion flows, especially on the equatorial plane, owing to the formation of the equatorial current sheet. When magnetic reconnection takes place within the ergosphere of a rotating black hole, one of the ejected plasmoids (denoted as $p_-$) may acquire negative energy-at-infinity density, while the companion plasmoid (denoted as $p_+$) correspondingly carries the energy extracted from the black hole. This mechanism, known as the Comisso-Asenjo process, provides a viable astrophysical pathway for extracting energy from a rotating black hole. Generally speaking, two conditions have to be satisfied for successful energy extraction. First, $p_-$ is generated via magnetic reconnection with negative energy-at-infinity density. Second, the associated $p_+$, which carries the extracted energy, must escape to infinity. Though a number of studies have promoted the theory of the Comisso-Asenjo process, the escape of $p_+$ lacks consideration. In this work, we adopt geodesic approach to systematically examine the destinies of $p_+$ after ejection, thereby establishing the complete conditions for energy extraction from a rotating black hole via magnetic reconnection. We redefine the covering factor, for which only escaping $p_+$ are counted. This quantity reflects how probable the successful energy extraction occurs in an accretion system. Through analysis of this revised covering factor, we identify a lower bound on the black hole spin, $a=0.8005$, below which energy extraction via magnetic reconnection can never succeed, no matter how high the magnetization of the bulk plasma is. This limit suggests that the spin-down of a black hole caused by successive magnetic reconnections would terminate once the spin decreases to approximately 0.8005.

**Relevance:** 1
**Novelty:** 5

## 127. [Moving Beyond Quasinormal Modes and Reinterpreting Direct Waves in Binary Black Hole Merger-Ringdowns](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25176) <a id="link127"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.25176

**Authors:** Richard Dyer, Anuj Kankani, Gareth Arturo Marks, Sean T. McWilliams, Christopher J. Moore

**Abstract:** Based on black-hole perturbation-theory calculations and an effective-one-body picture of binary black hole mergers, it has been suggested that a direct wave may be present and measurable in the ringdown. Furthermore, it has been suggested that this signal might originate from substantially inside the light ring (or photon sphere) of the remnant black hole, perhaps even from close to the event horizon. In this paper, we present three independent strands of evidence against such claims. In the process, we show that there is indeed an early-time part of the ringdown signal that cannot be directly modeled by quasinormal modes. However, there is no evidence that this is a direct wave as it has previously been defined, and we show that the properties of this component are related to the remnant's light ring rather than its horizon.

## 128. [X-ray Comptonization in an Accreting Kerr Black Hole System with a Tilted Disc and Thermal Corona; A General-Relativistic Monte Carlo Simulation: I. Model, Radiative Transfer and Spectral-Timing Properties](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26769) <a id="link128"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.26769

**Authors:** Mainak Chatterjee, Dipankar Bhattacharya

**Abstract:** Quasi-periodic oscillations (QPOs) in the X-ray emission of accreting black hole binaries are commonly attributed to the relativistic Lense--Thirring precession of the inner accretion flow, in which a hot inner flow, tilted with respect to the black hole spin axis, precesses as a solid body while a cooler outer disc remains at its own, different tilt -- a warped, Bardeen--Petterson-type geometry. Testing this picture requires a radiative-transfer model that self-consistently follows photons through such a system: from emission near the innermost stable circular orbit (ISCO), through Compton scattering in a hot corona, to possible reflection off the misaligned outer disc, all under the full effects of light bending in Kerr spacetime. In this first paper of a two-part series, we present a general-relativistic Monte Carlo (GRMC) radiative-transfer model of a misaligned, warped, two-zone accretion system around a Kerr black hole. We describe the disc--corona geometry, the seed-photon prescription, the photon-transport engine -- including the geodesic integration scheme, the coronal Compton-scattering module and the outer-disc reflection treatment -- and the observer pipeline used to construct energy spectra, photon path-length and arrival-time distributions. We present the resulting energy spectra, light curves and spectral lags as functions of the black hole mass, corona size, outer-disc geometry, reflection albedo, photon-injection radius and observer inclination, and show how gravitational light bending systematically lengthens the path-length and arrival-time distributions of the escaping radiation. This model and its spectral-timing predictions form the foundation for Paper II, which extends the framework to track the full photon polarization state and presents the resulting energy- and precession-phase-resolved polarimetric signatures.

**Relevance:** 1
**Novelty:** 5

## 129. [Floquet Instability of Photon-Axion Conversion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26908) <a id="link129"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.26908

**Authors:** Yuuki Sugiyama, Kazushige Ueda

**Abstract:** The discovery of axions would provide evidence for physics beyond the Standard Model. In this work, we investigate axion-photon conversion in the presence of a background magnetic field with simultaneous spatial and temporal periodicity. By performing a Lorentz transformation, the coupled axion-photon system is mapped onto a time-periodic linear system that can be analyzed using Floquet theorem. We find unstable modes associated with resonant crossing between Floquet replicas. The resulting complex Floquet exponents lead to exponentially growing modes, which we identify as a Floquet instability. Then, we determine the parameter regions in which this instability appears in a perturbative analysis. We further show that the Floquet instability enhances the axion-field amplitude generated from an initial photon field. Our results demonstrate that spatiotemporally periodic magnetic backgrounds provide a distinct mechanism for modifying axion-photon conversion through Floquet instability.

## 130. [A precessing AGN with S-shaped morphology discovered in MALS](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23738) <a id="link130"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.23738

**Authors:** J. D. Wagenveld, N. Gupta

**Abstract:** We present J1221$-$1354, a precessing AGN discovered in the MeerKAT Absorption Line Survey (MALS) using deep L-band (0.9--1.6\,GHz) radio data. Its `S'-shaped morphology, revealed by the excellent surface brightness sensitivity of MeerKAT, is consistent with jet precession. Ancillary optical and infrared data establish the source as a Type 2 AGN in a moderately overdense environment. The approaching (southern) jet is brighter but shorter than the receding jet, with the northern edge of the receding jet showing a significantly younger spectral age. These features are peculiar, but consistent with a jet reorientation event in an asymmetric ambient medium, in which the jet re-enters a cavity of previously deposited plasma. A simple ballistic model yields a precession period of $P=290\pm30$\,Myr, consistent with geodetic precession in a supermassive black hole binary (SMBHB) with a binary separation of less than 1.1\,pc. We expect that deep radio continuum surveys in the near future will uncover many more such systems, helping to further strengthen the connection between jet precession and SMBHBs, and the latter's implications for the gravitational-wave background detected with pulsar timing arrays.

## 131. [Single-Transit Parameter Estimation with Calibrated Uncertainties](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24882) <a id="link131"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.24882

**Authors:** Helem Salinas, Rafael Brahm, Greg Olmschenk, Melinda Soares-Furtado, Karim Pichara, Joshua E. Schlieder

**Abstract:** The growing volume of photometric data from current and upcoming exoplanet surveys demands automated analysis pipelines to detect and characterize exoplanet transit signals. Among these, single-transit events present a challenge, since only one event is observed, and standard transit-fitting techniques for periodic signals do not directly apply because they rely on phase-folding. Beyond detection, characterizing these events is equally critical for follow-up, yet existing approaches are either sensitive to stellar variability and require prior detrending, or depend on stellar priors and per-target fitting. This work presents a convolutional neural network that predicts transit depth, duration, and mid-transit time, together with calibrated uncertainties, directly from a single transit signal. Our network is trained on synthetic light curves and evaluated against a trapezoidal least-squares baseline. On 120 confirmed exoplanets, both methods achieve comparable median errors, but the baseline fails to locate the transit in 10% of cases, producing unreliable estimates that would propagate into follow-up prioritization. In contrast, our network fails in only 1% of cases, yields lower mean absolute errors, and does not require detrending or stellar priors. Detrending would distort or remove the transit along with high stellar variability, precisely where the baseline fails and our network succeeds. Finally, we identify 169 new transit candidates in TESS Sectors 27-60 and apply our approach to obtain their physical parameters, creating a catalog that includes single-transit, multi-transit and multi-planet systems. This approach offers a scalable path toward automated characterization of single transit events, a population that will grow with upcoming surveys.

**Novelty:** 5

## 132. [Seeing Through the RFI Mask: DT-FISTA Inpainting of Pulsar Dynamic Spectra via Fourier Sparsity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23140) <a id="link132"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.23140

**Authors:** Yuanshang Huang, Xun Shi

**Abstract:** Radio frequency interference (RFI) frequently contaminates radio observations, and excising affected samples leaves masked regions in sampled images. Replacing masked samples with zero or the mean causes spectral leakage in Fourier-domain power representations. This contamination is particularly severe for modern pulsar scintillation studies, which rely on identifying and characterizing faint parabolic scintillation arcs in secondary spectra, the 2D power spectra of dynamic spectra. Scintillation arcs probe discrete scattering screens in the interstellar medium (ISM) and constrain their physical properties. However, leakage can obscure faint arcs whose power is often $<10^{-3}$ of the dominant pulsar signal power. Wiener inpainting can mitigate this leakage, but a dense implementation requires $\mathcal{O}(N^2)$ memory and $\mathcal{O}(JN^2)$ time for $N$ pixels and $J$ iterations, which is computationally expensive for full-resolution spectra containing millions of pixels. We introduce the decreasing-threshold fast iterative shrinkage-thresholding algorithm (DT-FISTA). Using a sparse Fourier representation, DT-FISTA initially estimates masked intensities from the strongest Fourier components and progressively refines them with weaker components as the soft threshold decreases. Nesterov momentum extrapolation accelerates these updates. Each iteration uses 2D fast Fourier transforms (FFT) and pointwise operations, requiring $\mathcal{O}(N\log N)$ time and $\mathcal{O}(N)$ memory. Tests on $300\times300$ pixel patches from Five-hundred-meter Aperture Spherical radio Telescope (FAST) observations yield lower reconstruction errors than the dense Wiener implementation, with runtime and memory requirements reduced by factors $>10^3$. Applied to full-resolution dynamic spectra, DT-FISTA suppresses mask-induced artifacts and reveals previously obscured faint arcs, enabling efficient ISM studies.

**Relevance:** 1
**Novelty:** 5
