## 133. [High-cadence GW astrometry in the $μ$Hz gap](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27221) <a id="link133"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.27221

**Authors:** Giorgio Mentasti, Carlo Contaldi

**Abstract:** We forecast the sensitivity of a dedicated, fast-spinning astrometric survey to a stochastic gravitational-wave background (SGWB) in the microhertz gap between pulsar timing arrays (PTAs) and LISA. Spinning the satellite faster samples each star more often, raising the Nyquist frequency and opening higher frequencies at no cost in noise: the shorter, noisier exposures are exactly compensated by their number, so the white-noise floor of each position stream is independent of cadence. High frequencies are where astrometry is most competitive, because the deflection measures the strain directly: its strain noise is white, whereas the equivalent noise of pulsar timing grows as $f^2$. With a cross-correlation estimator over $N$ reference stars and the astrometric overlap computed in the distant-source limit, we find that a Gaia-like satellite scanning the ecliptic poles, with $N\sim10^4$ bright stars over $10$~yr, falls short of the background measured by PTAs by a factor $\sim\!60$. A larger, $8$-m-class aperture, or a constellation of satellites covering the whole sky, reaches it, while extending the reach continuously across the $μ$Hz band.

## 134. [BRISK: An accelerated wide-field imager for radio astronomy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23671) <a id="link134"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.23671

**Authors:** Oliver Ogden, Wesley Armour

**Abstract:** Wide-field interferometric imaging with non-coplanar arrays is computationally expensive, particularly as we move towards imaging large datasets from next-generation telescopes such as the Square Kilometre Array (SKA). The dominant imaging cost arises during the gridding and degridding steps, where large $w$-terms require correction. In $w$-projection, this correction uses $w$-dependent convolution kernels whose support grows rapidly with field of view (FoV) and maximum $|w|$. In $w$-stacking, visibilities are partitioned into discrete $w$-layers, each requiring a 2D Fourier transform and image-domain phase correction; the required number of layers increases with $|w|_{\max}$ and FoV. We present BRISK (Baseline-aware Rank-compressed Interferometric Sky-imaging Toolkit), a wide-field interferometric imaging algorithm that accelerates $w$-projection by exploiting the low-rank structure of its convolution kernels. For each $w$-plane, the full 2D gridding kernel is compressed using a truncated randomised singular value decomposition (SVD), yielding a compact representation from which the SVD-compressed kernel can be reconstructed on the fly while preserving the dominant kernel energy. We benchmark memory usage, runtime, and accuracy against CASA's $w$-projection and WSClean's $w$-stacking, $w$-gridding and image-domain gridding algorithms on a simulated $15 \times 15$ grid of point sources, as well as the SKA1-Low GLEAM simulated measurement sets. BRISK runs up to 2443 times faster than CASA, and up to 132 times faster than WSClean, while maintaining a peak-flux bias of less than 1% at the largest image sizes and comparable peak memory usage.

**Novelty:** 5

## 135. [A Multipole Technique for the Simulation and Analysis of Gravitational Waveforms from Extreme Mass Ratio Inspirals](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27153) <a id="link135"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.27153

**Authors:** Courtney McIntosh, Jeremy Schnittman

**Abstract:** We introduce EMRICode, a Python-based numerical framework for evolving generic extreme mass-ratio inspirals (EMRIs) in Kerr spacetime. The code combines adaptive numerical integration of test-particle geodesics with a prescribed model for the secular evolution of the orbital constants of motion, allowing eccentric and inclined trajectories to be followed over long inspiral timescales. We focus in particular on the treatment of the Carter constant, whose evolution is not directly determined by the leading-order fluxes for energy and axial angular momentum used in the present model. We investigate several prescriptions for determining its evolution and find that a radial-potential-based prescription provides the most stable behavior among those considered, while also exhibiting a systematic residual bias for nearly planar orbits. We compare the resulting inspirals with established radiation-reaction approximations, including the weak-field treatment of Peters and Kerr-based prescriptions from the literature, and use these comparisons to assess the qualitative behavior and limitations of the implementation. The modular structure of EMRICode allows the radiation-reaction prescription to be replaced independently of the underlying geodesic evolution, providing a framework for incorporating more accurate flux models and additional physical effects. We present two simple examples of how interactions with accretion disks and partial tidal disruptions can drive the evolution of a stellar EMRI orbit. EMRICode therefore provides a flexible computational framework for studying the orbital dynamics of EMRIs and for exploring extensions beyond the idealized vacuum, point-particle approximation.

**Novelty:** 5

## 136. [Source velocities of fast radio bursts constrained by their dispersion measure variations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27311) <a id="link136"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.27311

**Authors:** S. K. Ocker, A. L. Piro

**Abstract:** Growing evidence suggests that many fast radio bursts (FRBs) originate from extragalactic magnetars. Like all neutron stars, magnetars may be kicked at birth to large velocities, the values of which directly inform the physics governing stellar explosions and compact object formation. A subset of repeating FRBs show significant variability in their dispersion measures (i.e., electron column densities) over time, often exhibiting apparently secular trends. Here we show that all repeating FRBs with significant dispersion measure variations can be generically explained by motion of the source. We use existing measurements to constrain the minimum space velocities of the FRB sources, which are found to be $\gtrsim 20-2800\,{\rm km\,s}^{-1}$. We simultaneously constrain the plasma properties of the circumsource media, which are comparable to those of compact HII regions. For sources with compact radio nebulae, we directly infer the maximum source ages, and find them to be consistent with the ages of known Galactic magnetars. Our findings suggest that significant DM variability is a hallmark of young sources born with natal kicks through prompt formation channels. Applying our methods to a larger sample with continued monitoring of repeating FRB dispersion measures will reveal key physics of compact object formation.

## 137. [Deglobalising the fit: how to search for physics beyond vacuum General Relativity in LISA catalogues](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27069) <a id="link137"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.27069

**Authors:** Francisco Duque, Jonathan Gair

**Abstract:** The Laser Interferometer Space Antenna will observe up to $\sim 10^{7}$ overlapping signals, which have to be characterised simultaneously with a global fit. This is a challenge for model comparison since rerunning the global fit for every physical model of interest, including deviations from vacuum General Relativity, is computationally demanding. An alternative is a two-step post-processing strategy: a first global fit with vacuum models only, after which a selected group of target sources is reanalysed under a different model with all other sources subtracted. We show that in this strategy a beyond-vacuum effect in a target source can be partly absorbed by the overlapping nuisance sources in the first fit, subtracted with them, and never reach the reanalysis. In the linear-signal approximation, the loss is captured by a single number, the masking ratio $\mathcal{R}\in[0,1]$. A deviation the global fit would detect at $n_σ$ is reported by post-processing at $\mathcal{R}\,n_σ$, and bounds in null tests are also reported too tight by the same factor. We show how to compute $\mathcal{R}$ from the posterior samples of the vacuum global fit, and give an algorithm that ranks the nuisance sources by how much of the effect each can absorb, so that only those most correlated with it need to be refitted with the target in a down-scaled reanalysis with a few sources. We validate our method with full Markov-chain Monte Carlo inference on a toy model of 21 overlapping chirping sinusoids, where one carries a small non-linear chirp that represents the missed physics, while the other 20 act as nuisance sources. This method can serve as a basis for tests of General Relativity in realistic LISA mock datasets and can be adapted to study the impact of systematics between different gravitational-wave sources.

**Novelty:** 5

## 138. [Extending SOMPZ beyond Spectroscopic Coverage for Roman Weak Lensing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24066) <a id="link138"></a>

**Authors:** Yufei Zhen, Chun-Hao To, Boyan Yin, Junzhou Zhang, Chihway Chang, Finian Ashmead, Brett H. Andrews, Daniel C. Masters, Jeffrey A. Newman, Tianqing Zhang

**Abstract:** Precision weak lensing cosmology requires accurate galaxy redshift distributions. However, the spectroscopic samples used to characterize these distributions do not fully span the magnitude--color--redshift space of weak lensing source galaxies, necessitating extrapolation. This challenge will be critical at the depth of the Roman Space Telescope's High Latitude Imaging Survey (Roman HLIS). Using simulated LSST Year 4 and Roman HLIS photometry from the Cardinal simulations, we quantify how spectroscopic incompleteness biases photometric-redshift (photo-z) calibration and cosmological constraints. We find that the standard Self-Organizing Map p(z) (SOMPZ) method produces substantial tomographic bin overlaps and biased source redshift distributions. To address these limitations, we develop Roman-SOMPZ, which uses dedicated individual-galaxy photo-z estimators to supply redshift references for tomographic binning and redshift estimation. For the latter, we introduce UMAP-flow-z, an estimator that uses a UMAP embedding of deep-field galaxy colors to infer redshifts for phenotypes lacking spectroscopic representation. Compared with the standard pipeline, Roman-SOMPZ produces better-separated tomographic bins and lower absolute mean-redshift biases in eight of nine bins. Propagating the estimated redshift distributions into cosmological inference, we find that existing spectroscopy is adequate for early-stage weak lensing analyses in the Roman deep fields. Conversely, full medium-tier cosmology will require further improvements in redshift characterization.

## 139. [The metric-affine $\mathring{R}^2$ scalaron: parameter-free inflation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23522) <a id="link139"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.23522

**Authors:** Christian Dioguardi

**Abstract:** We present a model of slow-roll inflation in metric-affine gravity, where the connection is independent of the metric. We require that the Einstein-Hilbert term is the only operator that breaks three out of four shift-symmetries of the connection, and impose them on the metric-affine Starobinsky term $αR(Γ)^2$. This replaces it by $α\mathring{R}^2$, the square of the invariant part of the scalar curvature. The theory propagates only the graviton and one healthy scalar, the metric-affine $\mathring{R}^2$ scalaron. Its potential approaches a plateau polynomially, $U\simeq U_0(1-μ^2/χ^2)$, with $μ=\sqrt{6}\,M_P$ fixed by the curvature of the connection. Once $α\simeq2\times10^{8}$ is set by the scalar amplitude, the model has no free parameters. It predicts $n_s\simeq1-3/(2N_*)$ and $r\simeq2\sqrt3\,N_*^{-3/2}$ at leading order in $N_*$, and $n_s=0.968$--$0.974$ and $r=0.011$-$0.008$ with exact slow-roll for $N_*=50$-$60$ $e$-folds. These values agree with current data, and $r$ is within reach of the next generation of $B$-mode measurements.

## 140. [Does the inverse distance ladder rule out late-time solutions to the Hubble tension? A model-independent test using DES-Dovekie supernovae and DESI DR2 BAO](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23657) <a id="link140"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.23657

**Authors:** José Antonio Nájera, Indranil Banik, Harry Desmond

**Abstract:** It has been argued that the inverse distance ladder disfavours late-time solutions to the Hubble tension. We test this with a cosmographic expansion where $H$ is a fourth-order polynomial in $1 - a$. We combine the uncalibrated DES-Dovekie SNIa with DESI~DR2 BAO, assuming a standard sound horizon $r_{\rm d} = 147.05 \pm 0.30$~Mpc. Using only DES SNIa yields $H_0 = 76.25 \pm 2.87$ km/s/Mpc, which is $3.1σ$ above the CMB-calibrated $Λ$CDM prediction but consistent with SH0ES ($1.0σ$). Adding the legacy SNIa decreases $H_0$ to $67.98 \pm 0.69$ km/s/Mpc, $4.7σ$ below SH0ES. The $8.27$ km/s/Mpc drop in $H_0$ upon including the legacy SNIa cannot be attributed to expected sample variance: under a fiducial $Λ\text{CDM}$ model, only $1.24\%$ of $10^4$ mock realisations exhibit such a large $H_0$ shift due to the changed redshift sampling. This points to a systematic tension between DES and legacy SNIa datasets. We next add local $H_0$ anchors (the bright siren, SNII, TRGB + SBF) and test a parametrisation where the $Λ$CDM $H(z)$ is enhanced by $ΔH(z) = A \exp (-z/z_1)$. We recover $H_0 = 70.00^{+4.78}_{-0.98}$ km/s/Mpc and $z_1 < 0.086$ at $1σ$ confidence using DES SNIa, but $H_0 = 67.66 \pm 1.01$ km/s/Mpc and $z_1$ is unconstrained using DES + legacy. Late-time solutions are thus viable with the homogeneous DES catalogue, but not with the legacy SNIa as published. Allowing them to be arbitrarily recalibrated relative to the main DES sample yields an inferred offset of $ΔM_\rm{legacy} = 0.0646 \pm 0.0391$~mag, raising the predicted $H_0$ to $71.03 \pm 1.99$ km/s/Mpc. Ongoing SNIa surveys like DEBASS will provide a homogeneous sample of SNIa down to redshifts accessible via the distance ladder, assessing the validity of the current legacy samples and hence the feasibility of late-time solutions to the Hubble tension.

## 141. [Mean-Field Backreaction in Single-Field Inflation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26669) <a id="link141"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.26669

**Authors:** Matteo Braglia, Gabriele Franciolini, Nadir Ijaz, Marco Peloso

**Abstract:** The validity of linear perturbation theory and the role of backreaction in single-field inflationary models that enhance power at small scales have recently been under debate. We investigate scalar backreaction semi-analytically in a mean-field approach within the decoupling limit, considering popular models with a transient ultra-slow-roll (USR) stage or an oscillatory feature (OF) in the potential. We find that ultraviolet (UV) modes in the Bunch--Davies vacuum can substantially affect backreaction estimates unless removed by adiabatic subtraction. Our mean-field treatment can reproduce recent lattice results, but reveals a spurious sensitivity to UV contributions in some cases. This sensitivity is reduced in the OF model when copious particle production separates the amplified physical modes from the UV vacuum tail, making lattice results more reliable in this regime. In USR, instead, adiabatically subtracted spectra yield milder backreaction on the background evolution than previously inferred.

## 142. [Gamma-ray Bursts as Cosmological Probes Calibrated with Strongly Lensed Quasars and Neural Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27009) <a id="link142"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.27009

**Authors:** Wei Hong, Luca Izzo, Massimo Della Valle, Orlando Luongo, Marco Muccino, Tong-Jie Zhang

**Abstract:** Gamma-ray bursts (GRBs) extend distance measurements to redshifts beyond those routinely probed by Type Ia supernovae, but their use in cosmology is limited by calibration circularity and possible redshift-dependent effects. We investigate strongly lensed quasars as external calibrators of the Amati and Combo GRB relations. We reconstruct the luminosity-distance relation using an artificial neural network trained on TDCOSMO strong-lensing distances, with the absolute scale inherited from the lensing-only calibration. To avoid extrapolation, only GRBs within the lens-supported redshift interval are used for calibration. We then infer cosmological parameters through a hierarchical Bayesian analysis of flat $Λ$CDM, with $w_0w_a$CDM examined as a consistency test. For flat $Λ$CDM, Amati yields $H_0=78.26^{+17.87}_{-16.30}\,\mathrm{km\,s^{-1}\,Mpc^{-1}}$ and $Ω_m=0.670^{+0.223}_{-0.276}$, while Combo yields $H_0=78.71^{+13.83}_{-11.29}\,\mathrm{km\,s^{-1}\,Mpc^{-1}}$ and $Ω_m=0.635^{+0.235}_{-0.242}$. Amati is relatively insensitive to the phenomenological redshift term, whereas Combo shows a stronger dependence. Injection--recovery tests indicate that an unmodelled positive Combo trend can bias $H_0$ and $Ω_m$ upward. Strong-lens calibration therefore offers a route to extending the GRB Hubble diagram, but the inferred $H_0$ is not an independent GRB measurement: it retains the TDCOSMO distance scale. The precision is limited by GRB scatter, redshift-dependent systematics, and lens-modelling uncertainties.

## 143. [Disc breaking and parametric instability in warped accretion discs driven by differential precession](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26184) <a id="link143"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.26184

**Authors:** Loren E. Held, Gordon I. Ogilvie

**Abstract:** We present the first local simulations of accretion disc breaking (or disc tearing) with differential precession. Precession can occur due to a non-spherical potential (e.g. tilted circumbinary discs), or relativistic effects (e.g. tilted discs around spinning black holes). This tends to induce a twist in the disc, potentially causing it to break into rings. We extend earlier work on free (unforced) warps in Held+2026 [arXiv:2511.18655v1] to include differential precession in the shearing box, and carry out quasi-2D, isothermal, high-resolution ($N = 32$ cells per scale-height $H$) numerical simulations in which an imposed precession drives a warp in a tilted disc in either the wavelike ($α< H/r$) or diffusive ($α> H/r$) regime. Our key finding is that, at sufficiently large tilts and precession amplitudes, the disc breaks into multiple (4-5) mutually tilted rings exhibiting a rich morphology: from wide ($\sim 10H$), flat rings to compact tori of width $\sim H$. Interactions between rings occur as a result of oblique shocks that form in the gaps between them, leading to instances of disc rejoining. Smaller tilts and precession amplitudes lead instead to parametric instability of inertial waves in the disc. We develop a linear viscous theory of bending waves under the influence of an imposed precession. Finally, we present a systematic study of the effects of grid-based dissipation on tilted discs, and find that numerical dissipation damps the tilt and induces precession. This effect decreases with resolution as $\propto (1/N)^β$, $2.3 \lesssim β\lesssim 3.9$.

## 144. [The accretion flow and jet of M87: broadband modelling across spatial scales](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26434) <a id="link144"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.26434

**Authors:** Eduardo M. Gutiérrez, Santiago del Palacio, Tomás Tobías Mazzei

**Abstract:** The nearby radio galaxy M87 is an ideal laboratory for studying accretion flows and relativistic jet emission. The EHT collaboration conducted the most comprehensive multi-wavelength observational campaign of M87, in which radio instruments with resolutions ranging from tens of microarcseconds to about an arcsecond sampled different portions of the outflow. We model these data using a radiatively inefficient accretion flow (RIAF) with winds and a new multi-zone jet model, initially magnetically dominated, in which we solve the transport equation for the non-thermal electrons as they are injected, advected, and cooled along the jet. We then compare each radio flux with the jet emission within an effective aperture set by the angular scale of the corresponding observation. The comparison across spatial scales favours a scenario in which the RIAF, rather than the jet, produces the compact $230~{\rm GHz}$ emission, while the jet dominates the lower-frequency radio, optical/UV and X-ray emission, but cannot produce the observed $γ$-rays. The RIAF connects the Bondi-scale gas supply to an accretion rate at the horizon of $1.1\times 10^{-5}$ of the Eddington rate, consistent with EHT polarimetry, and the two jets carry about a quarter of the accretion power, $1.2\times10^{43}~{\rm erg~s}^{-1}$ each. The framework presented in this work provides a simple way to disentangle the accretion-flow and jet contributions to the emission from nearby radio galaxies.

## 145. [Maximal efficiency of geometric beaming and polarization of highly super-Eddington X-ray sources](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23089) <a id="link145"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.23089

**Authors:** Varpu Ahlberg, Alexandra Veledina, Eugene Churazov, Ildar Khabibullin

**Abstract:** Geometric beaming is an often invoked explanation for the apparent super-Eddington luminosities of ultraluminous X-ray sources. It is a natural consequence of the geometrically thick envelope that is expected to surround the inner accretion disk in the super-Eddington accretion regime; as the radiation can only freely escape through a narrow funnel-shaped cavity, the flux along the funnel axis is enhanced. A fraction of the photons will nevertheless escape the funnel cavity at larger angles, reducing the effectiveness of the beaming. To explore the limits of geometric beaming, we performed Monte Carlo simulations of conservative radiative transfer in an infinitely optically thick funnel while accounting for polarization. We discovered that the beaming becomes inefficient when the funnel opening angle is small $(\lesssim15^\circ$) because the photons tend to remain trapped within the cavity until they reach the upper edge of the funnel where they can escape at larger angles. For most of the photons to escape a narrow funnel through a small solid angle, the funnel has to be unrealistically long. Consequently, geometric beaming could at most boost the apparent luminosity by a factor of a few dozen in our idealized simulations. While larger boost factors on the order of $\sim$100 are possible for funnels with a length of $\gtrsim10^5$ Schwarzschild radii, this requires very narrow opening angles of $\lesssim3^\circ$. Additionally, we found that aberration due to mildly relativistic velocities does not significantly change this result. The maximal polarization degree of light reflected by the funnel remained on the order of a few percent for face-on sources and $\lesssim10\%$ for edge-on sources.

## 146. [Simulating dust collapse: the impact of gauge choices on primordial black hole formation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23218) <a id="link146"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.23218

**Authors:** Robyn L. Munoz, Christian T. Byrnes, Marco Bruni, Ian Hawke

**Abstract:** We investigate why numerical relativity simulations of dust collapse in a matter-dominated era frequently fail before primordial black hole formation due to numerical limitations and inappropriate gauge choices. Using 3-dimensional fully non-linear simulations of a dust fluid, we detail the advantages of advanced numerical methods and the impact of the lapse and shift evolution equations. The challenge is twofold: 1) conventional lapse slicing evolution equations, highly effective in typical black hole simulations, must be adapted in a cosmological context. We demonstrate how different proposed lapse evolution equations perform, enabling fair comparison, and propose our own. 2) The shift evolution equation's role is to spread out the numerical grid observers for a stable simulation. However, the Gamma-driver evolution fails when confronted with shell-focussing of the dust fluid. We show how the shift damping choice depends on the resolution of the shell-focusing region, suggesting that future work should have spatially varying damping. Our improved gauge choices allow simulations to evolve significantly further and potentially reach black hole formation. The best-performing simulations reach close proximity to a black hole; however, further work on the gauge choice is required for this to be stable.

## 147. [Static tidal Love numbers of Schwarzschild and non-Schwarzschild black holes in quadratic gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24625) <a id="link147"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.24625

**Authors:** Soham Bhattacharyya

**Abstract:** We calculate the static electric quadrupolar response of black holes in four-dimensional quadratic gravity, with action $R-αC^2+βR^2$. The Schwarzschild solution has zero massless gravitational Love number under the stated boundary conditions. A numerical non-Schwarzschild solution instead has a nonzero negative response. At $α/r_h^2=1/2$, we find $λ_E/r_h^5\simeq-0.1224928$ and $k_2^{(r_h)}\simeq-0.1837391$, with a final outer-endpoint change in $k_2^{(r_h)}$ of about $1.60\times10^{-7}$. We explain how the background is obtained by shooting and how the tidal solution is selected by matching at an intermediate radius. The perturbations are written in Kodama--Ishibashi gauge-invariant variables. After the scalar-curvature perturbation is excluded by horizon regularity and the absence of an applied scalar tide, the remaining static sector has three coupled second-order radial equations. We define the curvature amplitudes in these equations and describe why simple attempts to separate them fail. The coupling scan shows a negative response approaching zero near the Schwarzschild intersection. We also explain why this full-theory result does not conflict with the removal of curvature-squared operators in a perturbative low-energy expansion.

## 148. [Plate-YOLO: An End-to-End Object Detection Framework for Robust Astrometric Registration of Historical Plates](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25677) <a id="link148"></a>

**Authors:** Quanfeng Xu, Zhengjun Shang, Shiyin Shen, Yong Yu, Mingyang Ma, Zhenghong Tang, Meiting Yang, Jing Yang, Jianhai Zhao

**Abstract:** Historical astronomical plates provide a long temporal baseline for time-domain astrophysics, but degradation and structural artifacts can hinder automated astrometric calibration. We present Plate-YOLO, a framework that combines source detection and stellar classification in one detector and supplies source catalogs to a separate astrometric solver. The detector adapts YOLOv5 with Space-to-Depth feature rearrangement, SimAM attention, a high-resolution P2 head, and a Normalized Wasserstein Distance loss for small bounding boxes. A Gaia-guided fine-tuning procedure uses initially solved plates to construct labels for a new archive. On 150 UBAI plates for which the conventional pipeline had failed, the Chinese-trained model solved 40 plates, and target-archive fine-tuning recovered a further 106, giving an overall recovery rate of 146/150 (97.3%). In the reported hardware configuration, source extraction took 6.61 s per plate for Plate-YOLO and 16.73 s for SExtractor. These results demonstrate the practical value of adapting source detection to degraded historical plates.

## 149. [Pulse Morphology and Spectral Study of LPT J1634+44 Using GMRT](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26128) <a id="link149"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.26128

**Authors:** Ashish Kumar, Yogesh Maan, Ayushi Shrivastava, Banshi Lal

**Abstract:** Long-period radio transients (LPTs) are a recently discovered class of Galactic coherent radio emitters characterized by rotation periods of minutes to hours and highly polarized radio pulses. Their nature remains uncertain, with proposed scenarios invoking isolated or binary systems containing neutron stars (NSs) or white dwarfs (WDs). We investigate the nature of CHIME/ILT J1634+44 through multi-band observations with the Giant Metrewave Radio Telescope (GMRT), examining its pulse morphology, spectral evolution, and timing behavior. The pulses show substantial profile variability, and the subpulse features evolve significantly across observing frequencies. Using our measurements of quasi-periodic microstructures in J1634+44 pulses as well as those of other LPTs available from literature, we show that the scaling relationship between the rotation and microstructure periods is not limited to only neutron stars but extend to white dwarfs as well. The radio spectrum of J1634+44 is highly variable and exhibits a spectral turnover that shifts toward lower frequencies across observing epochs. Interpreting this turnover as free-free absorption, we constrain the physical properties of the intervening absorbing medium. Complementing these emission diagnostics, we also present analysis of pulse arrival times spanning approximately a decade to probe the previously proposed spin-orbit resonance models for this LPT.

## 150. [Adaptive Model-Switch Thermodynamic Integration: A Bayes Factor Estimator](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24354) <a id="link150"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.24354

**Authors:** Amanda Malnati, Megan Kralj, Ken D. Olum

**Abstract:** Bayes factors are a widely used model comparison criterion, but are often quite challenging and computationally expensive to estimate well. We propose a variation of the model-switch thermodynamic integration method for estimating Bayes factors that decreases the variance of the estimator and essentially eliminates integration error. We choose samples whose density in $β$ is proportional to the standard deviation of previously drawn samples. Rather than averaging many samples at each $β$, we use a very dense set of $β$ values, collecting one sample at each in order of increasing $β$ and one sample at each in order of decreasing $β$ to minimize "thermic lag". We study our procedure with a toy model (based loosely on some signal recovery problems). In this model we find that adaptive sampling gives us a 2.7-fold decrease in the spread of the estimated Bayes factor. We test our procedure both drawing directly from the posterior distribution and using a Markov Chain Monte Carlo sampler.

## 151. [Elementary-Symmetric Ricci Gravity: Metric Formulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26980) <a id="link151"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.26980

**Authors:** Xingpao Suo, Dianxu Ma, Yuqiong Shan, Xingyan Suo

**Abstract:** We investigate a four-dimensional metric theory of gravity whose Lagrangian is a linear combination of the elementary symmetric polynomials of the Ricci tensor. We derive the field equations and characterize Einstein vacua and a real, diagonalizable class of parallel-Ricci vacua. The latter includes direct products of two constant-curvature surfaces and can exhibit a hierarchy of curvature radii. The Einstein vacua possess multiple branches associated with the distinct roots of a polynomial, whose multiple roots define boundaries in parameter space. We derive the linearized equations on maximally symmetric backgrounds, distinguish the scalar and spin-2 degrees of freedom, and then analyze their classical linear stability. We also obtain the generalized Friedmann constraint and the associated homogeneous evolution equations. An illustrative spatially flat solution evolves from the neighborhood of an unstable de Sitter solution toward a stable de Sitter solution. This behavior may motivate further investigation into early- and late-time accelerated expansion and provide a gravity-driven mechanism for automatic end of inflation.

## 152. [Testing the statistical compatibility of data sets with a common prior](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28681) <a id="link152"></a>

**Authors:** Pilar Coloma, Michele Maltoni, Thomas Schwetz

**Abstract:** The Parameter Goodness-of-fit (PG) test [hep-ph/0304176] offers a simple test statistic to study the compatibility of different data sets within a given theoretical model. In its original formulation it assumes that the data sets are statistically independent. We propose an extension of the PG test for the situation when some parameters of the model are constrained by an auxiliary data set called prior here. As the prior is imposed for all data sets, they are no longer statistically independent. We show that under the Gaussian approximation, the prior-constrained PG test statistic follows a generalised $χ^2$ distribution, which can be used to obtain $p$-values in a straight-forward way. In the approximation of a strong prior constraint, the test converges towards the standard PG test for the unconstrained model parameters.

## 153. [Hidden Sector Relic Abundances from an Effective Planck Mass](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23658) <a id="link153"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.23658

**Authors:** Donald Liveoak, Anshuman Maharana, James D. Wells

**Abstract:** Hidden sectors are generic in beyond the Standard Model constructions and string compactifications. We consider models featuring hidden sectors that have dark matter candidates. The relic density is set in part by the energy-injecting inflaton branching ratio $B$ to the sector. We develop a general framework to determine the dependence on $B$ for thermalized hidden sectors by defining an effective Planck mass for the Boltzmann equations for the hidden sector degrees of freedom. We apply this framework to several representative examples, including freeze-out/freeze-in WIMPs and multi-component dark matter. We find that the fractional energy density carried by freeze-out relics (in both single and multi-component models) and monopoles produced from second order phase transitions scale as $B^{1/4}$ and thus may lead to an overclosure problem, even with $B \ll 1$. On the other hand, the energy density of freeze-in relics scales as $B^{5/4}$ and is thus cosmologically irrelevant for sufficiently small $B$. Finally, we discuss the cumulative implications of a large number of hidden sectors.

## 154. [Gravitational Waves as a Probe of Multi-Fluid Cosmologies: General Formalism and the Case of Cosmological Stasis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23808) <a id="link154"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.23808

**Authors:** Keith R. Dienes, Fazlollah Hajkarim, Lucien Heurtier, Fei Huang, Kuver Sinha, Brooks Thomas, Tao Xu

**Abstract:** Scalar-induced gravitational waves (SIGWs) provide a powerful probe of the expansion history of the universe prior to the Big Bang. Indeed, the production of such gravity waves depends on the evolution of first-order scalar perturbations within the early universe, while their subsequent propagation depends on the background equation of state. In this paper we extend previous discussions by studying SIGWs in cosmologies containing multiple fluids. As we shall demonstrate, the gravitational-wave signal within such multi-fluid cosmologies is modified in two distinct ways: not only are there new velocity effects which have no single-fluid analogues, but there are also significant alterations to the subsequent redshifting of modes that enter the horizon before the multi-fluid era ends. Thus SIGWs provide a probe of not only the overall expansion rate within such cosmologies, but also their internal multi-fluid composition. As an example, we then consider the case of gravitational waves arising during an epoch of cosmological stasis. During such an epoch, the abundances of energy components with different equations of state remain constant, thereby giving rise to a universe with a constant effective equation of state which nevertheless remains genuinely multi-fluid despite cosmological expansion. Despite their constant equations of state, we find that such stasis cosmologies also give rise to tensor power spectra and present-day gravitational-wave abundances which are significantly different from those expected within a standard radiation-dominated universe.

**Abstract:** DESI DR2 favours dark energy whose equation of state evolves crossing $w=-1$ in the past. The coupling is bounded at $|β|<0.073$ ($95\%$), and the two potentials agree on it to within $0.15σ$. They differ instead in the equation of state they can reach: the cosine comes closer to the evolution preferred by DESI and is weakly preferred by the fit. The coupling posterior is bimodal, with modes at $β\simeq\pm0.05$; only the negative mode produces the crossing, and it is the higher of the two in every analysis. **Abstract:** The Central Molecular Zone (CMZ) of our Galaxy is a prime laboratory for interstellar chemistry because of its extreme conditions, such as shocks and high cosmic-ray ionisation rates. It is an efficient factory of molecules, with many species first discovered in CMZ sources. This document reviews key results from the study of the CMZ G+0.693-0.027 molecular cloud. Using ultra-sensitive broadband spectral surveys together with laboratory spectroscopy, more than 140 molecules have been identified, including 28 detected for the first time in the interstellar medium (ISM). ## 157. [Survey of Orion Disks with ALMA (SODA) IV: Millimeter variability of protoplanetary disks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23640) <a id="link157"></a>

**Authors:** D. Krebs, A. Hacar, J. Forbrich, S. E. van Terwisga

**Abstract:** Context. Short-term millimeter-wavelength variability in Class II young stellar objects (YSOs) has been considered minimal, often dismissed as very rare in ALMA-based studies on short timescales. However, recent findings suggest that variability on sub-day timescales is significant. Aims. This study aims to quantify short timescale (~1 hour to ~3 days) millimeter flux variability in unresolved Class II YSO disks observed as part of the SODA survey, leveraging its large dataset to assess the statistical significance of such variability across the LDN 1641 and LDN 1647 regions in Orion. Methods. We analyze ALMA Band 6 observations of 873 YSOs obtained over multiple epochs using a custom flux extraction pipeline to separate individual execution blocks. By computing variability factors and statistical deviations from the mean flux, we identify sources exhibiting significant variability, accounting for noise and calibrator stability. Results. Even with conservative selection criteria, we identify 20 variable sources within the sample (~5.3% of all detections). The maximum of the measured variability factor is >=7 over about one hour. More liberal selection criteria yield up to 60 sources that could be called potentially variable (~15.9%). Conclusions. The number of variable sources detected in our sample indicates physical processes operating on time scales of hours, likely associated with magnetic reconnection and/or synchrotron radiation or strong cases of magnetic accretion changes. These detections show the need to reconsider typical stability assumptions in single-epoch observations and calls for follow-up studies in star-forming regions with ALMA. Masses derived from unresolved measurements could potentially be biased by this sort of variability.

Across twelve configurations, each chosen inside the energy band in which that model's front meets the resonance, the reconstruction raises the ray-averaged crossing probability $P_H$ by 0.44 to 0.76 in absolute terms, median 0.56; narrowing the reconstruction thirty-fold changes each difference by at most 0.055. Along all 12878 rays of one configuration, the sudden-limit formula evaluated on each ray's own densities agrees closely with the propagated reconstructed result. The change is largest when the density jump spans the resonance, over a band of energies that can be estimated from the presupernova density once the shock radius and the compression are known, with tails outside it. In a separate calculation with a parametrised shock and a published emission model, time-dependent conversion changes the $ν_e$ charged-current count in a 40 kt argon detector by -4.4%, with a sign set by the flavour composition of the source. ## 160. [PSR~B0355+54: An Unexpected Compendium of Nearly All Scintillation Arc Morphologies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27005) <a id="link160"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.27005

**Authors:** Zhen-Long Liao, Zhen Yan, Zhi-Qiang Shen, Rui Wang, Xun Shi, Ke-Jia Lee, Ju-Mei Yao, Xiao-Wei Wang, Zhi-Peng Huang, Kuo Liu, Yong-Hua Xu, Chu-Yuan Zhang, Fan Yang, Yang-Yang Lin

**Abstract:** Scintillation arcs are sensitive probes of multipath propagation, offering a window into the distribution of scattering material in the interstellar medium. Contrary to the common observational finding that individual pulsars exhibit only a limited range of scintillation arc morphologies, we report that PSR~B0355+54, observed simultaneously at 2.25/8.60~GHz with the Shanghai Tianma Radio Telescope, displays nearly the full range of known arc patterns, many of which evolve rapidly. Across 54 observations from 2020 to 2026, we detect scintillation arcs in 53/53 usable observations at 2.25~GHz and 33/49 at 8.60~GHz. At 2.25~GHz, the secondary spectra exhibit five broad morphological types: forward arcs, inverted arclets, diffuse arcs, diffuse power, and irregular structures, with prominent displaced arclets appearing in several observations. In contrast, the 8.60-GHz spectra are generally dominated by faint forward arcs. Despite these marked morphological differences, the scaled curvatures and their annual variations agree between the two frequencies, showing that both trace the same dominant scattering screen at a fractional distance of $s=0.069^{+0.015}_{-0.014}$ near the pulsar, further supporting a possible association with the \ion{H}{ii} region Sh~2-205. Simulations show that this diversity can arise from variations in the scattered-image distribution within this screen, while brightness reconstructions at 2.25~GHz resolve localized structure down to $\sim0.02~\mathrm{mas}$. Tracking four arclets over 10~h at 8.60~GHz, including two displaced arclets, reveals a common evolutionary trend consistent with the line of sight sweeping across small-scale scattering structure, helping explain the rapid arc evolution.

## 161. [Impact of Residual Gain Errors on the Detectability of Ionized Bubbles during Reionization with SKA 21-cm observations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24843) <a id="link161"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.24843

**Authors:** Arnab Mishra, Kanan K. Datta, Iffat Nasreen, Snehasish Saha, Sk. Saiyad Ali

**Abstract:** Detecting ionized bubbles and characterizing their properties and the surrounding intergalactic medium (IGM) using the HI 21-cm signal provides direct probes of cosmic reionization. Here, we focus on the detection of individual ionized bubbles around luminous sources using a matched-filter technique. We investigate the impact of time- and frequency-correlated residual antenna gain errors on the detectability and characterization of individual ionized regions with SKA-Low. We simulate mock SKA-Low AA* observations that include the HI 21-cm signal, astrophysical foregrounds, residual gain errors, and system noise corresponding to 100 h of observing time. We subtract the foreground components from the gain-corrupted data and subsequently apply the matched-filter technique to the residual visibilities. We find that the matched-filter technique is able to detect the ionized bubble and constrain its properties in the presence of residual gain errors with the rms amplitudes up to $σ_A\sim1\%$ and rms phases up to $σ_φ\sim1^\circ$. Residual gain errors at similar levels prevent the reliable identification of ionized regions in reconstructed HI 21-cm images and bias the estimated HI 21-cm power spectrum. Furthermore, we find that increasing the temporal correlation length increases the level of gain-induced artifacts in the reconstructed image. In contrast, increasing the frequency correlation length reduces these artifacts, as some fraction of the spectrally smooth gain-induced residuals is removed during foreground subtraction. We further find that the inferred bubble and IGM parameters remain largely unaffected by residual gain errors, although their uncertainties can increase. These results demonstrate the potential and robustness of the matched-filter technique for detecting and characterizing individual ionized bubbles in the presence of substantial residual calibration errors.

## 162. [Lorentz-Invariance Violation with neutrinos from gamma-ray bursts using IceCube High-Energy Starting Events 12-year dataset](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25214) <a id="link162"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.25214

**Authors:** Mathieu Lamoureux, José Manuel Carmona, José Luis Cortés, Ardit Gkioni, Antoine Kouchner, Maykoll A. Reyes

**Abstract:** High-energy astrophysical neutrinos provide a unique probe of Lorentz-invariance violation (LIV), as small energy-dependent deviations from the speed of light can produce measurable time delays over cosmological distances. We search for LIV-induced correlations between neutrinos and gamma-ray bursts using the public 12-year IceCube High-Energy Starting Event dataset and a catalog of 746 bursts with measured redshifts. We develop a likelihood analysis accounting for neutrino directional and energy uncertainties, a possible intrinsic time offset between neutrino and gamma-ray emission, and the decay of superluminal neutrinos expected within effective field theory. For linear LIV, the data show a mild preference over the background-only hypothesis, with a $p$-value of $3.1\%$, corresponding to a one-sided Gaussian significance of approximately $1.9σ$. The best fit corresponds to a LIV scale $Λ_{\rm LIV}=5.8\times10^{17}\,\mathrm{GeV}$ and is effectively driven by the subluminal component, as superluminal neutrinos are strongly suppressed by LIV-induced decays at the analyzed energies. The preferred LIV scale and the most significant neutrino--gamma-ray burst associations are consistent with previous studies based on different statistical approaches. No evidence for quadratic LIV is found. These results motivate further searches with complementary neutrino datasets, particularly at lower energies, where the superluminal component may become observable.

## 163. [Supermassive Black Hole Winds in X-rays -- SUBWAYS. VI. Interplay between the soft X-ray excess and ultra-fast outflows](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26936) <a id="link163"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.26936

**Authors:** V. E. Gianolli, P-O. Petrucci, S. Bianchi, M. Brusa, G. Lanzuisi, G. A. Matzeu, M. Mehdipour, F. Ursini, N. Torres-Alba, E. Amenta, E. Behar, L. Borrelli, M. Cappi, A. Comastri, E. Costantini, M. Dadina, B. De Marco, A. De Rosa, M. Giustini, S. Kraemer, G. Kriss, Y. Krongold, A. L. Longinotti, S. Mathur, R. Middei, G. Miniutti, E. Nardini, S. Peluso, E. Piconcelli, G. Ponti, R. Serafinelli, F. Tombesi, A. Tortosa, C. Vignali, I. Cox, S. Marchesi, M. Parra, M. Ajello, T. Mukherjee, I. Pal, P. Patel, A. Pizzetti, D. Sengupta, R. Silver, X. Zhao

**Abstract:** We present a broadband, multiwavelength study of the interplay between the soft X-ray excess (SE), ultra-fast outflows (UFOs), and warm absorbers (WAs) in 21 active galactic nuclei (AGN; 0.1 <= z <= 0.4) from the SUBWAYS project. We combine simultaneous or quasi-simultaneous UV, optical, and X-ray data from XMM-Newton (EPIC pn and OM), NuSTAR, and the Hubble Space Telescope. We model the SE using a two-coronae framework, in which a warm, optically thick corona (WC) produces the optical/UV and soft X-ray emission, while a hot, optically thin corona produces the hard X-ray continuum. We also test a hybrid model that adds relativistic reflection. The optical/UV coverage constrains the Comptonization seed photon temperature to 0.1-5.5 eV. The two-coronae model consistently describes the broadband emission, with WC electron temperatures kT_e ~ 0.1-0.9 keV and photon indices $Γ> 2$, consistent with previous studies. However, 9/21 AGN require the hybrid model, suggesting that the SE has multiple origins whose relative contributions vary. This is especially evident in UFO hosts, where 4/7 observations favor the hybrid model. Compared with sources without UFOs, UFO hosts show lower WC temperatures, harder hot-corona spectra, and positive hardness ratios. These trends should be treated cautiously because of the small sample and their dependence on spectral parameters and instrumental response. We find no significant differences between AGN with and without WAs. Our correlation analysis finds links between the amplification factor and WC temperature, WC optical depth and hardness ratio, and WC slope and optical depth with UV luminosity. We also find a tentative anticorrelation between WC optical depth and UFO outflow velocity.

## 164. [Impact of neutron captures on the r-process: I. Propagation of statistical model uncertainties](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23395) <a id="link164"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.23395

**Authors:** Yu An, Jan Kuske, Almudena Arcones

**Abstract:** Background: The r-process is responsible for producing about half of the elements heavier than iron in the Universe, but the underlying nuclear physics is still largely inaccessible to experiments and relies on theoretical calculations. Purpose: We aim to quantify the impact of statistical model uncertainties on the r-process with a large set of neutron-capture rates within the Hauser-Feshbach framework with varied statistical model parameters. Propagating neutron-capture rates to r-process calculations of various astrophysical sites allows us to link abundance features to nuclear uncertainties. Methods: We calculate neutron-capture reactivities using different nuclear level density (NLD) and gamma-ray strength function (GSF) prescriptions. We study their impact on r-process calculations for individual trajectories, mass-integrated ejecta from a neutron-star-merger, and a broad set of 10,000 parametric trajectories with a wide range of electron fractions and entropies. Results: The final abundances are more sensitive to NLDs than to GSFs. Selected NLDs produce pronounced abundance changes between the lanthanides and the third peak. Although the variation is reduced in the mass-integrated total ejecta, a subpeak at A=172-178 and systematic abundance changes at A=180-194 persist. The parametric trajectories show that the NLD sensitivity is greater in the cold r-process regime, where neutron captures continue after photodissociations have become ineffective. Additional conditions with large NLD sensitivities are found at intermediate electron fractions, where small changes in the matter flow can lead to significant differences in the final abundances. Conclusions: Systematic differences in neutron-capture rates between NLD prescriptions affect the final r-process abundances. Therefore, improved experimental constraints and theoretical descriptions of NLDs in these regions are needed.

## 165. [Broad Line Regions behind a Haze II: A Three-dimensional Electron-scattering Model of the Broad Br$γ$ Line in NGC 3783](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23368) <a id="link165"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.23368

**Authors:** Keiichi Wada, Bert Vander Meulen

**Abstract:** We investigate the broad Br$γ$ line in the type-1 active galactic nucleus NGC 3783 using three-dimensional Monte Carlo electron-scattering radiative transfer with SKIRT. Paper-I reproduced the observed smooth core and broad wings by convolving an intrinsic Keplerian profile with the analytic electron-scattering kernel of Laor (2006). Here we instead transport photons through explicit source and scattering geometries. For a partially covered Keplerian source, three-dimensional transfer broadens only the scattered photons and leaves an unscattered double-peaked core. For the thin Keplerian source motivated by Paper-I, a comparison of four scattering-geometry classes shows that neither a disk-like scatterer nor the tested halo variants reproduce both the core and wings. We therefore construct a two-component-source (2CS) model containing an inner Keplerian source disk, a rotating disk haze, a nearly spherical halo haze, and direct emission from a slower outer disk. A reference 2CS calculation gives Xi^2 =1.154 at i=15 deg. Polar cones with an input radial optical depth tau_hole=0.1 preserve the line profile and give a measured Thomson-thin sightline. The preferred low redistribution width, full-column ionization, and weak recombination emission cannot all be realized by a uniform thermal halo. The 2CS model is therefore a physically motivated parametric description whose medium must ultimately be tested with self-consistent radiative transfer, ionization, and dynamics.

## 166. [Three-dimensional spatial modelling of nuclear de-excitation lines](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23380) <a id="link166"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.23380

**Authors:** Tomohiko Oka, Thomas Siegert, Tristan Bouchet, Laura Eisenberger, Mika A. Gelowicz, Saurabh Mittal, Rudi Reinhardt, Manuel R. H. W. Skalka, Dimitris Tsatsis, Hiroki Yoneda, Manja Zimmerer

**Abstract:** Galactic cosmic rays (GCRs) are thought to originate from shock acceleration in supernova remnants, but interstellar ionization rates suggest an additional low-energy cosmic-ray (LECR) component. LECRs excite nuclei in the ISM, producing MeV gamma-ray line emission through subsequent de-excitation. Observations of this emission can constrain the LECR flux, morphology, and acceleration mechanisms. We construct all-sky maps of Galactic de-excitation emission using three-dimensional (3D) CR distribution models. By comparing them with the Galactic diffuse gamma-ray emission, we assess the detectability of the de-excitation line components against the underlying continuum. We calculate the emission by combining 3D CR distributions from GALPROP and gamma-ray production cross sections from TALYS. We also model an additional LECR component motivated by the high ionization rates measured in the ISM. The predicted de-excitation emission is relatively weak when integrated over the broad regions commonly adopted in MeV gamma-ray analyses ($|l|\leq30^{\circ}$ and $|b|\leq15^{\circ}$), contributing only $\lesssim 1\%$ above the leptonic continuum even considering the additional LECR component. The all-sky maps reveal that, in the standard scenario, the de-excitation-to-leptonic flux ratio remains low over the entire sky, reaching at most ${\sim}20\%$ toward the Galactic anticenter. In contrast, models including an additional LECR component exhibit a pronounced enhancement toward the GC, where the ratio exceeds $100\%$, indicating favourable prospects for detection through spatially resolved analyses. The 6.1-to-4.4 MeV intensity ratio is particularly sensitive to the maximum energy of the injected LECR spectrum, varying by up to ${\sim} 30\%$. Future MeV gamma-ray observatories could thus identify a distinct LECR population and constrain its acceleration and transport.

## 167. [Balmer breaks in accreting super-massive black hole binaries](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27076) <a id="link167"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.27076

**Authors:** Christopher Tiede, Zoltan Haiman

**Abstract:** We show that truncated circumbinary accretion disks around super-massive black hole binaries can produce Balmer breaks. For binaries interacting with surrounding disks in a truncated, non-accreting phase, gas accumulates at the cavity edge, and can create conditions conducive to generating spectral discontinuities from $n=2$ hydrogen bound-free absorption. We develop a simple model for predicting the presence of a Balmer edge and estimating the break strength, and we find that the strongest discontinuities are expected for binaries with periods of months to years and total masses $\gtrsim10^6\,\Msun$. These systems share considerable overlap with those binaries anticipated to appear in upcoming time-domain surveys---like LSST and the Roman HLTDS---as periodically variable quasars. We estimate that for typical feeding rates of a few tenths of their Eddington rate, a few-to-10\% of periodic binaries at $z\lesssim1$ should show prominent Balmer breaks, and that this fraction increases for the more massive black holes that can be discovered at higher redshift. We suggest that black hole binaries that truncate their accretion flows will appear as periodically variable sources, and will additionally be intrinsically X-ray weak, deficient in ionizing emission, and in favorable cases, produce notable Balmer edges in their spectra. This combination provides a complementary observational test for massive black hole binary candidates.

## 168. [Globular Clusters GMRT Pulsar Survey (GCGPS) III: ORION - a multi-beam acceleration search pipeline](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27303) <a id="link168"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.27303

**Authors:** Nishant Deo, Jyotirmoy Das, Jayanta Roy, Saurabh Raj, Karel Adamek, Wesley Armour, Bhaswati Bhattacharyya, Jayaram Chengalur, Paulo C. C. Freire, Scott M. Ransom, Sridhar Gajendran, Sanjay Kudale, Mekhala V. Muley, Adityan S, Junhao Zhang

**Abstract:** Globular clusters host rich millisecond pulsar populations, but the known population remains incomplete. We present the observing strategy for Phase II of the Globular Clusters GMRT Pulsar Survey (GCGPS), which uses the SPOTLIGHT system to form 160 synthesised beams simultaneously, substantially expanding the instantaneous field of view compared with the two phased-array beams of Phase I. The configuration is tunable through the beam overlap and the number of contributing antennas, allowing spatial coverage to be balanced against sensitivity. We quantify the sensitivity losses from intra-channel dispersion smearing and finite sampling, which motivate the adopted dispersion-measure limits of 50 and 150 pc/cc for Bands 3 and 4. To process the resulting data, we introduce ORION, a GPU-accelerated pipeline that combines the high-throughput Fourier-domain filtering of PULSCAN with localised parameter refinement using PRESTO. In single-beam tests with simulated binary pulsars, ORION reduces processing time by a factor of approximately 15 to 30 relative to the CPU-based Phase I pipeline, with comparable or higher folded detection significance. On-sky validation with Cycle 49 observations recovered four known globular cluster pulsars with spin periods of approximately 5.4 to 406 ms. Using eight GPUs and 64 CPU cores, ORION processes full 160-beam observations in approximately 0.25 to 0.33 times the observation duration, corresponding to three to four times real-time throughput. Together, the Phase II strategy and ORION provide a scalable framework for expanding globular cluster pulsar searches, with future development targeting highly accelerated and jerked binary systems.

## 170. [Cosmic birefringence from a stiff axion field and its cosmological implications](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27082) <a id="link170"></a>

**Authors:** Andrea Minotti, Yunzhi Wu, Tiziano Zanzarella, Nicolao Fornengo, Marco Regis, Marco Taoso

**Abstract:** We propose a model in which the observed cosmic microwave background birefringence arises from the interaction of photons with a stiff ($w=1$) pseudo-scalar field that today contributes a subdominant fraction of the total energy density, distinct from both dark matter and dark energy. Big Bang Nucleosynthesis constrains the cosmological density of the stiff field, leading to a lower bound for its coupling with photons, $g_{φγ} > 8.9 \times 10^{-14}\,\mathrm{GeV}^{-1}$. Photon conversion in cluster-hosted quasars sets an upper bound, $g_{φγ} < 5.0 \times 10^{-13}\,\mathrm{GeV}^{-1}$. Therefore, there is a narrow window for the discovery of this cosmological component that can be explored in the forthcoming years with astrophysical axion searches. Moreover, we show that this scenario leads to gravitational-wave signals that can be tested with planned experiments.

## 171. [Isomer-resolved ion-molecule reaction kinetics of HCO+ and HOC+](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23452) <a id="link171"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.23452

**Authors:** Miguel Jiménez-Redondo, Olli Sipilä, Petr Dohnal, Robin Dahl, Paola Caselli, Pavol Jusko

**Abstract:** The HCO$^+$/HOC$^+$ abundance ratio has been observed to vary significantly in different astrophysical environments, making it an important diagnostic tool for exploring the interstellar medium. However, uncertainties in the reaction rates and branching ratios of key chemical reactions involving these ions affect the conclusions drawn from observations of either these species or other species that are derived on their basis. Here, we present isomer-resolved ion-molecule reaction rate coefficients of reactions involving HCO$^+$ and HOC$^+$ using a cryogenic ion trap experiment. We measured the rate coefficients of the isomerization reaction of HOC$^+$ to HCO$^+$ with H$_2$ and CO, as well as three different reactions producing both isomers: CO$^+$ + H$_2$ , H$_3^+$ + CO, and ArH$^+$ + CO. Our results show that the reaction of CO$^+$ with H$_2$ predominantly produces HCO$^+$ with a negligible yield of HOC$^+$ . This result is in agreement with recent theoretical predictions, but in major contrast to the generally accepted branching ratio based on previous experimental results. We also find that the isomerization reaction of HOC$^+$ to HCO$^+$ is efficient with both H2 and CO. We were able to assess the impact of our new measured reaction rate coefficients on the predicted HCO$^+$/HOC$^+$ abundance ratio using a state-of-the-art astrochemical model.

## 172. [Late-time distance consistency and model-independent curvature inference with strong lensing, quasars, and galaxy clusters](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26097) <a id="link172"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.26097

**Authors:** Jian Hu, Yi Liu, Jian Ping Hu, Zhongmu Li

**Abstract:** We revisit a late-time, model-independent distance-consistency framework that combines strong gravitational lensing systems (SGLS), ultraviolet/X-ray quasars (QSOs), and 38 Sunyaev--Zel'dovich/X-ray galaxy-cluster angular-diameter distances. The analysis focuses on systematics rather than on presenting a competitive curvature measurement. Starting from the 158-system SGLS catalog, we exclude 30 systems that violate the lensing-geometric requirement $D_{A,ls}/D_{A,s}<1$ in the isothermal reference case $γ_{\rm lens}=2$, leaving a fixed 128-system SGLS parent sample for cross-matching with 1598 QSOs. A deterministic maximum-cardinality, minimum-mismatch one-to-one assignment yields 100 SGLS--QSO pairs at the nominal $Δd/d=5\%$ threshold. We propagate the measured X-ray flux uncertainties and the intrinsic QSO scatter, and, crucially, we include the full $38\times38$ covariance induced by using the same QSO distances in the kernel-smoothed cluster predictions. With $Ω_K\sim\mathcal{U}[-10,10]$, the joint posterior is $Ω_K=2.085^{+1.634}_{-1.103}$ (68\%; central 95\% interval $[0.269,6.028]$). We do not interpret the positive shift as evidence for an open Universe. \added{This positive posterior offset is driven by cross-probe calibration tensions, sample-assignment effects, and the cluster anchor; it cannot be attributed to the intrinsic spatial geometry of the Universe.} The SGLS+QSO data alone leave curvature essentially prior dominated, $Ω_K=0.70^{+6.33}_{-7.12}$, whereas removing only the off-diagonal cluster covariance moves the posterior to $Ω_K=5.21^{+2.46}_{-2.07}$. Thus the cluster anchor supplies most of the curvature constraining power and its covariance materially changes the posterior location.

## 174. [MACE: Multigroup-Multiangle Integrated Thermal Compton Scattering Kernel](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25117) <a id="link174"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.25117

**Authors:** Itamar Giron, Menahem Krief, Elad Steinberg

**Abstract:** We present a comprehensive set of accurate Compton-scattering tables for use in multigroup, multiangle radiation-transport calculations. The tables contain the exact thermal Compton kernel integrated over incident-energy groups, scattered-energy groups, and angular bins. To generate them, we develop a deterministic integration method in which the pointwise kernel is evaluated with a robust numerical treatment that remains accurate across challenging regions of parameter space. The multidimensional integrals are computed using nested quadratures whose panels adapt to the Compton recoil ridge, its thermal broadening, and the nearly elastic forward-scattering boundary layer. We validate the tabulated results against published kernel profiles, angular and energy moments, mean free paths, multigroup redistribution data, and an independent Monte Carlo estimator. The resulting tables provide the radiation-transport community with accurate, reproducible, and readily usable Compton redistribution matrices and angular moments.

**Abstract:** Light curves reveal stellar variability and support studies of stellar evolution and cosmic distances. Reliable classification is therefore important for large time-domain surveys. We present HyQ-LC for ten-class stellar light-curve classification using 359 handcrafted features and 633,034 training objects. The main model combines a window multilayer perceptron (MLP) backbone with a nine-qubit readout using amplitude encoding and angle re-uploading. Both this hybrid and its pure-MLP control have 44,238 parameters. On a shared 20,000-object test set, the hybrid has macro-averaged F1 (Macro-F1) point gains of 0.8276 and 1.2393 percentage points over the pure MLP on two fine-tuning seeds. A separate fixed-fusion configuration achieves slightly higher Macro-F1 but lower accuracy than approximately budget-matched LightGBM. Its positive class-level differences are concentrated in CEP, RRC, and ROT, while the full-budget LightGBM reference scores higher on both metrics. All circuits are simulated classically. The comparisons retain existing validation-selected checkpoints and use the same test objects, with features, predictions, and recomputation code publicly available. The results identify point-estimate improvements over the tested controls while leaving the specific contribution of the quantum branch unresolved.

## 176. [Kinematics and Thermodynamics of Coronal Mass Ejections](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26219) <a id="link176"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.26219

**Authors:** Soumyaranjan Khuntia

**Abstract:** Coronal mass ejections (CMEs) are major drivers of space weather, yet their thermodynamic evolution remains insufficiently constrained. This thesis develops a comprehensive framework linking CME kinematics, thermodynamics, internal force balance, and geoeffectiveness through analytical modelling and multi-viewpoint observations. The developed FRIS model uses observed kinematics to infer otherwise inaccessible internal plasma properties, including the polytropic index. A systematic investigation of fast CMEs reveals an initial heat-release phase followed by a transition towards near-isothermal evolution, typically around 3-9 solar radii. The evolving contributions of thermal-pressure and centrifugal forces, opposed by the Lorentz force, establish a connection between expansion and thermal evolution. The findings demonstrate the limitations of assuming a fixed polytropic index in magnetohydrodynamic models of CME propagation. At 1 au, in situ observations reveal persistent non-adiabatic evolution, accompanied by turbulence and localised intermittency. The interacting CMEs associated with the May 2024 great storm show restructured magnetic and thermal configurations, producing double-flux-rope-like signatures and species-dependent heating, with electron thermodynamics as a better indicator of recent interactions. Statistical analysis of nearly 600 ME demonstrates that 45% exhibit net heating with a solar cycle dependence. Notably, High-impact ejecta preferentially exhibit stronger heating signatures, establishing thermal state as a complementary diagnostic of geoeffectiveness. Overall, this thesis establishes CMEs as intrinsically non-adiabatic systems whose thermodynamic evolution is coupled to expansion, internal force balance, solar-cycle variability, and CME-CME interactions, with important implications for understanding geoeffectiveness and improving space-weather assessment.

## 177. [Large-scale Vortices in the Solar Photosphere: Statistical Properties and the Reliability of Local Correlation Tracking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27222) <a id="link177"></a>

**Abstract:** Vortices are observed throughout the solar atmosphere, spanning a wide range of spatial scales. Although small-scale vortices have been studied extensively, mesoscale vortices remain less explored, and it is still unclear whether they can be reliably recovered from observations. Mesogranular-scale motions are recovered from the R2D2 simulations by applying Gaussian spatial smoothing to the horizontal velocity field with full width at half maximum (FWHM) values of 2.5-4.0 Mm. To evaluate the accuracy of local correlation tracking (LCT) and its suitability for identifying mesogranular vortices, we apply LCT to synthetic intensity maps at $τ= 1$ using the same spatial windows and temporal averaging windows of 10-60 minutes. Vortices are identified in both the reference and LCT-derived velocity fields using the $Γ$ method. For all tested parameter combinations, LCT underestimates the number of mesogranular-scale vortices. The best overall recovery is obtained for relatively small spatial smoothing scales (FWHM $\leq 3.5$ Mm) and short averaging windows ($\leq 20$ minutes), which provide the most reliable vorticity and lifetime statistics while maintaining strong agreement in vortex radius and area distributions. Our analysis shows that only a modest fraction of LCT-detected vortices correspond to vortices identified in the reference velocity field (3.54%-17.88%), depending on FWHM and temporal averaging. This indicates that vortex-driven dynamics inferred from LCT-detected vortices should be interpreted with caution. Our results suggest that applying the $Γ$ method to LCT-derived velocity fields can recover useful statistical properties of mesoscale vortical structures, especially their temporal trends and characteristic sizes.

## 178. [Principle of no paradoxical Universe](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23606) <a id="link178"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.23606

**Authors:** Ngo Phuc Duc Loc

**Abstract:** In the standard $Λ\rm CDM$ model, the Universe will keep expanding forever and its lifetime is infinite. Therefore, even the heaviest supermassive black hole will eventually evaporate and the universal occurrence of the black hole information paradox is unavoidable. However, the recent DESI results seem to indicate that dark energy is dynamical and, more importantly, it is possible that the Universe has a finite lifetime and will experience a ``Big Crunch" in some billion years from now. In light of this, the lightest confirmed black hole with mass of order one solar mass may not have a chance to evaporate before the Universe ends. Inspired by these observations, we thus raise an intriguing conjecture: Does the Universe choose a configuration such that the ``in-principle" black hole information paradox can never actually happen in reality? We derive a lower bound on black hole mass as a function of the Universe's lifetime such that the information paradox cannot be realized in nature and some resulting predictions are made. Some profound implications for other related big ideas in physics are briefly discussed.

**Relevance:** 1
**Novelty:** 4

## 179. [The START mission: Probing the 2029 Apophis Flyby via a Low-Cost Geocentric SmallSat](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22904) <a id="link179"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.22904

**Authors:** Yixuan Wu, Yunda Li, Bowen Zheng, Jingxiang Xu, Yuhang Yu, Wang Sun, Haozhe Du, Siyuan Wu, Zhixuan Zhao, Rongchenzhong Hu, Jiantao Liu, Haoyu Liu, Yunchong Shen, Yutong Sun, Tianchen Wang, Jing Yu, Zijie Tang, Yihong Wu, Yifei Jiao, Wen-Yue Dai, Jialong Song, Nan Zhang, Zhaokui Wang, Tianshu Wang, Junfeng Li, Hexi Baoyin, Bin Cheng

**Abstract:** The Student-led Threatening Asteroid Reconnaissance of Tsinghua (START) marks the inaugural mission of the Tianxing Program, a deep-space exploration initiative led by students at Tsinghua University. Conceived as a low-cost, rapid-response small-satellite mission, START will investigate the near-Earth asteroid (99942) Apophis during its close approach to Earth in April 2029, when significant terrestrial tidal forces will offer a rare opportunity to observe the physical and dynamical response of a rubble-pile asteroid in real time. The 300 kg spacecraft is scheduled for rideshare launch into low Earth orbit in early 2028 and will then use solar electric propulsion to transfer to a circular mission orbit with a semimajor axis of approximately 38,000 km and an inclination of 55 degrees. START will execute a high-speed flyby of Apophis from a closest distance of 7 km at the asteroid's closest approach to Earth, to capture its tidal response. The payload suite consists of a high-resolution narrow-field camera, a wide-field navigation camera, and three near-infrared multispectral imagers. By acquiring centimeter-scale imagery and meter-scale reflectance spectra, the mission will characterize the asteroid's surface morphology and composition and monitor immediate tidally induced surface modifications. Ultimately, START will provide geometrically complementary, high-cadence datasets to international flagship missions to Apophis, demonstrating a highly scalable and cost-effective architecture for future planetary defense and small-body exploration.

**Novelty:** 4

## 180. [Laboratory Characterization of a Ge-CCD Prototype for Future Near-Infrared Astronomical Instrumentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22785) <a id="link180"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.22785

**Authors:** Emi Tamura, Juan Cruz Estrada Vigil, Prashansa Mukim, Giovanni Pinaroli, Christopher Leitz, Ilya Prigozhin, Kevan Donlon, Santiago Ezequiel Perez, Sven C. Herrmann

**Abstract:** Germanium charge-coupled devices (Ge-CCDs) extend CCD imaging beyond the silicon cutoff and are candidate detectors for the near-infrared channel of future astronomical instruments, in particular the spectrographs of next-generation wide-field surveys. We report the laboratory characterization at Brookhaven National Laboratory (BNL) of a prototype fabricated at MIT Lincoln Laboratory. We describe the cryogenic test system and readout, and present measurements of the output waveform, dark current, readout noise, and X-ray response. The dark current at 94.69 K is $26.12 \pm 0.08$ e$^-$ pixel$^{-1}$ s$^{-1}$. Under $^{55}$Fe irradiation without on-chip binning, the charge generated by an X-ray is confined within $2\times2$ pixels, and the Mn K$α$ line has a full width at half maximum (FWHM) of $8.5 \pm 0.1$% for single-pixel events. Since the charge generated by X-rays above about 30 keV spreads over $3\times3$ or more pixels, the CCD was operated with $2\times2$ on-chip binning at higher energies. This yielded FWHMs of $8.4\pm0.4$% at 30.9 keV ($^{133}$Ba, ASCA grades 0, 2, 3, 4, and 6) and $7.45\pm0.04$% at 59.5 keV ($^{241}$Am, all grades). These results establish a baseline for the device and for the BNL test system. Measurements of the temperature dependence, optical response, and charge-transfer performance will connect this baseline to the requirements of near-infrared astronomical instruments.

## 181. [On the foundations of anthropic reasoning: Implications for life across the universe](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22896) <a id="link181"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.22896

**Authors:** Brendon J. Brewer, Geraint F. Lewis, Jesse Goodman

**Abstract:** With only one confirmed example, namely the Earth, the abundance of life across the universe remains deeply uncertain, and local evidence about Earth's particular circumstances can significantly shape our knowledge of life elsewhere. Such extrapolation is difficult not only because of this sample size of one, but also due to observer selection effects discussed under the Anthropic Principle. In this paper, we describe and apply a framework called Full Indexical Conditioning (FIC) for performing Bayesian inference in this situation. Inspired by, but distinct from, Full Non-Indexical Conditioning, FIC conditions on all evidence available to the reasoner while considering that this evidence could have been otherwise. We address the reference class problem associated with such reasoning, demonstrating that substantive conclusions are unaffected by the choice of reference class provided it is sufficiently large. We apply FIC to several problems in astrobiology, including the Doomsday argument, the early abiogenesis time, and our location around a G-type rather than M-type star, clarifying the foundations of these arguments. We conclude that the early abiogenesis time does not rule out pessimistic possibilities for life's abundance, and that while life around M stars may be uncommon, its temporal distribution could extend far into the future. Ultimately, FIC offers a clear conceptual framework, grounded in straightforward probability theory, for tackling problems involving anthropic selection effects.

**Relevance:** 1
**Novelty:** 4

**Abstract:** The classical nova FQ Cir (Schaefer et al. 2026) has recently been shown to have a Be star donor, and has been identified as a High Mass Cataclysmic Variable (HMCV), with an orbital period of $\sim 2.04d$ (twice the 1.02d periodogram peak). Using the extensive OGLE optical archive, together with existing observations from ASAS-SN we instead find significant modulation between $\sim 50-60$~days and propose that the orbital period is likely closer to $\sim 62d$. We interpret the low amplitude 1.02 d modulation as a non-radial pulsation of the Be star. We also reassess the HMCV classification based on properties of known Be star-white dwarf systems.
