## 183. [Understanding sub-arcsecond scale radio sources and space weather with the SKA-Low using interplanetary scintillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23220) <a id="link183"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.23220

**Authors:** Rajan Chhetri, John Morgan, Harsha Avinash Tanti, Hayley Bignall, Emily Kerrison, Abhirup Datta, Anshu Kumari

**Abstract:** Continuum surveys with SKA-Low will provide excellent spectral information for detected objects. However, the angular resolution it provides cannot clearly distinguish between extended radio galaxies and their sub-arcsecond scale counterparts. We have previously used an interplanetary scintillation (IPS) technique with wide field-of-view instruments (the MWA and ASKAP) to detect and study $<$0.3\arcsec\ objects. The ability to identify hundreds of compact sources from large areas of the sky using $<$5 minutes of data, and associated low computational cost makes widefield IPS a very efficient approach to identify sub-arcsecond scale objects at low radio frequencies. We aim to utilise SKA-Low's flexibility in data capture, frequency coverage and high sensitivity to develop widefield IPS technique with SKA-Low, enabling the identification of compact radio sources at angular scales more than an order of magnitude smaller than that achieved by SKA-Low's longest baselines. This capability will facilitate studies of compact source populations (e.g., AGN cores), their relationship to extended radio galaxies, AGN contributions in star-forming galaxies, identify candidates for pulsars and HI absorption, and provide a network of calibrators for direction-dependent ionospheric calibration for SKA-Low. Additionally, ground-based IPS observations are one of the very few remote sensing techniques to probe the Heliosphere which will enable SKA-Low to provide unique insights into the solar wind. Here, we present recent developments in the field of compact sources at low frequencies, their synergies with other fields of astronomy and space weather, and outline the technical developments and approaches that are necessary with the SKA-Low to realise these studies.

## 184. [Dynamical response of a protoplanetary disk to an FU Ori-type outburst](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25435) <a id="link184"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.25435

**Authors:** Roman Zolotarev, Yaroslav Pavlyuchenkov, Vitaly Akimkin

**Abstract:** Episodic accretion events onto young stars can significantly change the global structure of their surrounding protoplanetary disks. Using radiation hydrodynamical simulations with the HURAKAN code, we study the response of the outer passive disk to a powerful FU Ori-type outburst. Disk heating initiates a factor-of-two increase in the vertical scale-height, which develops on a local keplerian timescale. This effectively launches an outward travelling wave with a bump whose radial position changes with time as $r_{b}\sim t^{2/3}$. After the outburst ends, new waves are launched as the disk structure re-equilibrates toward a quiescent state. The vertical expansion of the disk introduces significant time-variable extinction of the central source emission. Disks with inclinations in the range $40^{\circ}<i<60^{\circ}$ go from weakly ($A_{V}<1$) to a strongly embedded state ($A_{V}\sim10^2$), with the extinction decaying on a $\sim10^2$ yr timescale after the outburst. A fingerprint of the outburst-launched shadowing wave is a long-lived drop or a transient dip in the luminosity curve occurring several years after the outburst begins. This apparent luminosity variation is relatively large, up to $1^m-3^m$ in the V band, and is not related to the intrinsic accretion variability.

## 185. [Ranking Prompt--Plateau Correlations in Gamma-Ray Bursts with Symbolic Regression](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23155) <a id="link185"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.23155

**Authors:** Fatemeh Rastegarnia, Rahim Moradi, Brwa Shukur Ismael, Mohsen Asaadi, Yu Wang

**Abstract:** Gamma-ray burst (GRB) correlations probe central engines, relativistic outflows, and radiation processes. The Dainotti relation is an anticorrelation between the X-ray plateau luminosity $L_a$ and rest-frame plateau duration $T_a^{\rm rest}$. We use symbolic regression to compare it with other low-dimensional correlations in 251 GRBs with Swift-XRT plateau properties and Swift-BAT prompt-emission parameters. We perform a Dainotti-guided search, in which $\log T_a^{\rm rest}$ is always present, and an unrestricted search over subsets of at most three variables. The guided search recovers the Dainotti dependence; a D'Agostini fit gives $\log L_a=(51.92\pm0.30)-(1.31\pm0.09)\log T_a^{\rm rest}$, with intrinsic scatter $σ_{\rm int}=0.82\pm0.05$ dex. In the unrestricted ranking of 377 feature subsets, the classical Dainotti relation remains competitive, within the top $\sim25\%$ of the tested search spaces. The highest-ranked screened expression is $\log L_a\approx0.855\log L_{\rm peak}-0.782\log T_a^{\rm rest}$, with RMSE $\approx0.46$ and $R^2\approx0.88$. The highest-ranked runs repeatedly reduce to this prompt--plateau structure, without retaining additional spectral, absorption, or afterglow-shape variables. A direct regression gives $\log L_a=4.718+0.776\log L_{\rm peak}-0.805\log T_a^{\rm rest}$, with $R_{\rm P}=0.91$ for 246 bursts. An errors-in-variables refit gives consistent coefficients. After removing shared luminosity-distance dependence, the relation retains non-negligible explanatory power ($R^2\approx0.62$), consistent with a possible intrinsic component. Restricted Monte Carlo null tests give $p\approx0.01$. Plateau luminosity therefore shows a coupled dependence on prompt luminosity and plateau duration.

## 186. [The SIMPLIFI Survey: Polarization of Dense Filaments with SOFIA III. Relative orientations of antenna temperature gradients with the magnetic field in the DR21 molecular cloud](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27090) <a id="link186"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.27090

**Authors:** Mark Heyer, Thushara G. S. Pillai, Jens Kauffmann, Juan D. Soler, Daniel Seifried, Laura M. Fissel, Gabriel A. P. Franco, Paul F. Goldsmith, Philip C. Myers, Helmut Wiesemeyer, Karl M. Menten

**Abstract:** We investigate magnetohydrodynamic (MHD) turbulence in the DR21 molecular cloud using far infrared polarimetry from SOFIA HAWC+ and molecular spectroscopy from the Nobeyama 45~m telescope. The Velocity Channel Gradient (VChG) method is applied to thin velocity slices of the 12CO and 13CO J=1-0 emission to determine the antenna temperature gradients and their orientations in the plane of the sky. The relative orientations of the gradients with respect to the magnetic field position angles enable an evaluation of theoretically predicted alignment of elongated, turbulent eddies for Alfvenic, fast and slow modes and self-gravity. The projected Rayleigh statistic is calculated for both the full cloud and 6 regions of interest to provide a quantitative assessment of these alignments. Our analysis finds mostly parallel alignment of the gradient direction and the local magnetic field orientation in the Main Ridge of DR21 that comprises most of the cloud mass. While parallel alignment can result from fast MHD modes or cloud collapse, previous studies show spectroscopic evidence for the large scale collapse of the DR21 cloud, which supports self-gravity as the primary cause of the observed parallel alignment. One of the six sub-filaments that are connected to the Main Ridge exhibits mostly perpendicular alignment of the position angles of the gradient and magnetic field. These perpendicular relative orientations may be a product of Alfven and slow modes in a magnetized, sub-Alfvenic medium even if the sub-filament is accreting onto the Main Ridge.

## 187. [Cosmological implications of two mock TianQin standard siren catalogs generated under different background cosmological models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26727) <a id="link187"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.26727

**Authors:** Jun-Xian Li, Shuang Wang, Liang-Gui Zhu

**Abstract:** Space-borne gravitational wave (GW) detectors such as TianQin are expected to detect GW standard sirens for cosmological parameter estimation. All previous studies of TianQin adopted the $Λ$CDM model to generate mock standard siren data. In this work, we quantitatively investigate how the choice of background cosmological model affects mock TianQin standard siren (TQSS) catalogs and the corresponding cosmological implications. Adopting the $Λ$CDM model and a redshift-binned dark energy (DE) reconstruction model (the wbin model), we consider the bright standard sirens from massive black hole binary mergers and generate two mock TQSS catalogs, i.e. TQSS1 and TQSS2, respectively. We investigate the difference of the catalogs and further explore their cosmological implications within the Chevallier-Polarski-Linder (CPL) model, both individually and in combination with DESI DR2 baryon acoustic oscillation (BAO) data, $\textit{Planck}$ 2018 cosmic microwave background (CMB) data, and DESY5 type Ia supernova (SN Ia) data. We find that there are only small differences between TQSS1 and TQSS2 catalogs, with a maximum relative difference of $1.48\%$ in the distance-redshift relation. In addition, either the TQSS1 or TQSS2 catalog alone gives cosmological constraints similar to those obtained from SN data, implying that these two catalogs can serve as a complement to mainstream cosmological observations. Most importantly, we find that both TQSS1+DESI DR2+CMB+DESY5 and TQSS2+DESI DR2+CMB+DESY5 are still expected to favor dynamical DE, with these two combinations showing a preference for dynamical DE at $2.3σ$ and $3.8σ$, respectively. Adopting different massive black hole seed models does not impact the cosmological implications of the mock TQSS catalogs. Our study indicates the importance of considering different cosmological models in the standard siren forecasts for DE.

## 188. [Probing the Interstellar Medium towards the Double Pulsar and the Gum Nebula using Low-Frequency Pulse Broadening Measurements](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26141) <a id="link188"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.26141

**Authors:** C. P. Lee, N. D. R. Bhat, B. W. Meyers, W. van Straten

**Abstract:** Long-term timing of the double pulsar system (PSR J0737-3039A/B) has enabled some of the most stringent constraints on strong-field gravity. Yet, despite the system's high profile, there have been very few observations at frequencies below 300 MHz, where pulse broadening due to interstellar scattering becomes significant. Measurements of the pulse broadening timescale and its frequency scaling, $τ_\mathrm{s}\proptoν^α$ (where $α$ is the scattering spectral index), provide valuable constraints on the structure of the scattering medium. Here we report on simultaneous observations of J0737-3039A and five other nearby pulsars (J0630-3824, J0742-2822, J0820-4114, J0835-4510, J0837-4135) in the frequency range 109-231 MHz using the Murchison Widefield Array (MWA). These observations have enabled the highest-quality detections ever published for these pulsars at low frequencies. For J0737-3039A, J0742-2822, and J0837-4135, we measure $α$ to be -4.1(1), -3.8(1), and -4.1(1), respectively. These measurements provide new constraints on the density and turbulence parameters of the Gum Nebula region, and we discuss implications for models of the Galactic electron density distribution. Our current data yield a dispersion measure (DM) precision of ~4 x 10^-4 pc/cm^-3 for J0737-3039A, which can be further improved with the MWA Phase III to reach ~1 x 10^-4 pc/cm^-3, far exceeding the precision of previous studies. The prospects for precisely constraining temporal variations in the DM and pulse broadening timescale strengthen the importance of regular monitoring of J0737-3039A at low frequencies, with the eventual goal of contributing to long-term timing studies.

**Novelty:** 4

## 189. [A Neural Network - Based Tool for Rapid Orbital Solution of Contact Binary Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23410) <a id="link189"></a>

**Authors:** Li-Heng Wang, Kai Li, Ya-Ni Guo

**Abstract:** With the rapid growth of large-scale time-domain surveys, vast amounts of photometric and spectroscopic data are continuously being released. A substantial number of contact binaries have been identified, accompanied by both light curves and radial velocity observations. Traditional modeling of these datasets relies on computationally intensive physical models, which limits their applicability to large-scale analyses. To this end, this paper proposes the use of a fully connected neural network and a SIREN (Sitzmann et al. 2020) model to rapidly generate radial velocity curves. After training, the models can rapidly generate radial velocity curves with high accuracy, significantly reducing computational costs and enabling efficient large-scale studies. This method was further integrated with the light curve computation framework developed by Li et al. (2025), allowing simultaneous fitting of radial velocities and light curves. To assess the reliability of the proposed model, synthetic observational datasets were constructed and used for parameter estimation. The deviations between the derived parameters and the ground-truth values were analyzed quantitatively, enabling a comprehensive evaluation of the model's accuracy.

## 190. [Models of spectral bumps of primary cosmic rays and multi-messenger tests](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24412) <a id="link190"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.24412

**Authors:** Qiang Yuan, Chuan Yue, Yi-Zhong Fan

**Abstract:** The energy spectra of individual species of cosmic ray nuclei by recent direct detection experiments show complicated structures with spectral hardenings and softenings rather than featureless power laws, which challenge the traditional understanding of the production, acceleration, and propagation of cosmic rays. Particularly, the DAMPE measurements reveal charge-dependent softenings and reject mass-dependent softenings with a high significance. Taking into account the correlated structures at similar energies in the large-scale anisotropies of cosmic rays, one of the most natural interpretations of the spectral structures is the presence of a nearby cosmic ray source. In this case, the softening energies correspond to the acceleration upper limits of such a source, forming the so-called Peters cycle of the spectra. Alternative models such as the injection/acceleration and propagation models may also reproduce the spectral bumps, but show difficulty to explain the anisotropies. Fluxes of secondary nuclei and $γ$ rays offer additional tests of different models. Future multi-messenger observations are expected to be able to pin down the origin of cosmic rays.

## 191. [Detection Forecasting for Blazars at MeV Energies with Fermi-LAT Swift-BAT Cross Match](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26658) <a id="link191"></a>

**Abstract:** Over the past two decades, the lack of an MeV $γ$-ray all-sky instrument has left an observational gap in blazar broadband emission. In this work, we forecast the capabilities of future MeV $γ$-ray instruments including COSI, GRAMS, newASTROGAM, and AMEGO-X to observe blazars. We forecast that an instrument with a sensitivity limit of $10^{-11}$ $\mathrm{erg/cm^{2}/s}$ could detect 61 of the 76 SED-modeled blazars, and expect a total population of a few tens to a few hundreds of Flat-Spectrum Radio Quasars (FSRQs), as well as a few hundred BL Lacs (BLLs). An instrument with a sensitivity limit of $10^{-12}$ $\mathrm{erg/cm^{2}/s}$ could detect all 76 of the SED-modeled blazars, and we expect a total population of a few hundred to one thousand FSRQs and roughly one thousand BLLs. As MeV blazars have been observed to be extremely luminous and MeV $γ$-rays are minimally attenuated by the extragalactic background light, we use luminosity functions to estimate that blazars could be detected out to $z\approx6$. ## 192. [Hyperonic Direct-Urca Neutrino Emission with Lattice-QCD-Informed Form Factors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25514) <a id="link192"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.25514

**Authors:** Andreas Konstantinou

**Abstract:** We investigate the sensitivity of direct-Urca neutrino emission in hyperonic neutron-star matter to the structure and momentum dependence of baryonic transition form factors. Using the zero-temperature FSU2H relativistic mean-field equation of state, we derive the relativistic emissivity for general spin-$1/2$ baryon transitions with the complete six form-factor contribution. We compare conventional static flavor-SU(3) couplings with vacuum lattice-QCD-informed form factors, using direct lattice vacuum input for $n\rightarrow p$ and $Λ\rightarrow p$ and a hybrid construction for the remaining hyperonic channels. Evaluating the latter both at $q^2=0$ and at the kinetic momentum transfer sampled at the Fermi surfaces separates changes in the zero-momentum couplings from momentum-transfer effects. The sensitivity is strongly channel dependent. The nucleonic transition probes $q^2\simeq0$ and is only weakly affected, whereas hyperonic channels can sample substantially larger momentum transfers and receive correspondingly larger corrections. These modifications propagate to the integrated neutrino luminosity of stellar configurations. Our results estimate the possible scale of the sensitivity coming from momentum transfer dependence, absent from static-coupling treatments and motivate future first-principles studies of baryonic weak currents in dense matter.

## 193. [Prospects for Simultaneously Detecting Long and Short Duration Gravitational Waves from Pulsar Glitches with Current and Future Detectors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23385) <a id="link193"></a>

**Authors:** M. Ball, D. Keitel

**Abstract:** The internal mechanisms that drive the sudden change in rotation frequency in pulsar glitches could emit gravitational waves (GWs) at multiple time scales. The potential for GW analyses to detect both long and short forms of emission ("tCW-like" and "burst-like") improves as the detectors increase in sensitivity. We aim to examine how future detectors fare in making this joint measurement. By examining past glitches from the ATNF catalog, we quantify the double-detectability prospects of current- and next-generation GW detector networks. We find that a network with at least one next-generation detector greatly increases the probability of double-detection for many known glitching pulsars and improves the detection threshold in glitch energy by nearly two orders of magnitude over existing LIGO detectors. **Authors:** M. Zacharias, A. Zech, A. Reimer, C. Boisson

**Abstract:** Blazars - active galaxies with the jet pointing at Earth - emit across all electromagnetic wavelengths. As the one-zone model becomes increasingly inadequate to describe blazar emission, more elaborate models are needed. In order to self-consistently describe the entire electromagnetic spectrum, extended jet models are necessary. Notably, kinetic descriptions of extended jets can provide the temporal and spatial evolution of the particle species and the full electromagnetic output. ExHaLe-jet is a lepto-hadronic, extended-jet code allowing for in-depth studies of the particle and radiation evolution on all scales. Here, we present our ongoing study regarding acceleration scenarios of particles within the large-scale jet structure. A single acceleration of particles at the jet base (or elsewhere) is insufficient to produce sufficient photon flux. Thus, re-acceleration through, e.g., consecutive shocks along the jet is needed. We explore two possible scenarios for this.

**Abstract:** HESS J1303$-$631 is an evolved, TeV-bright pulsar wind nebula (PWN) powered by the energetic radio pulsar PSR~J1301$-$6305, offset by $\approx7'$ northwest of the TeV centroid, with an energy-dependent gamma-ray morphology. This morphology, the hard GeV counterpart, and the energy-dependent TeV emission together support an evolved, synchrotron-underluminous PWN whose X-ray nebula is far more compact than its TeV nebula. Its large offset from HESS J1303$-$631 makes association with the TeV source unlikely.

**Abstract:** We audit an automated relativistic-reflection pipeline that fitted one XMM-Newton EPIC-pn and NuSTAR epoch for each of 57 AGN with relxillCp+xillverCp, and repeat the analysis with its failures corrected. The pipeline's apparent constraints were not controlled: Small Window pn backgrounds were never subtracted, the nearest-in-time pairing selected mismatched or nearly empty spectra, single-start fits often stopped short of the minimum, and most fits were inadequate or at a parameter limit. The corrected analysis uses in-window backgrounds, 41 sources with eligible pn--NuSTAR pairs, multi-start fits, bidirectional profiles, criteria fixed in advance and eight model variations. 24 of the 41 reference fits have $p\ge0.01$ and 18 pass all quality checks. Their 90% spin sets comprise 1 two-sided interval (NGC 4051, $0.46\le a_*\le0.93$), 6 lower bounds, 4 upper bounds, 4 disconnected sets and 3 unconstrained profiles. For 10 of the 18 the best fit lies at $i\ge75^\circ$, implausible for type-1 AGN, and most of those constraints disappear when $i\le60^\circ$ is imposed. Only 2 of the 15 constrained sets keep their type both under that limit and with pn restricted to 3--10 keV (an upper bound for Mrk 1040 and a weak lower bound for 1H 0419-577). Simulations show that, with a correct model, the 90% sets contain the true spin in 82--92% of cases without drifting edge-on, but at high inclination one low-spin simulation in six yields a false lower bound. We make no statement about the spin distribution or a spin--jet relation.

## 197. [Radar and Infrasound Observations of the 2026 May 30 Cape Cod Bay Superbolide](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24566) <a id="link197"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.24566

**Authors:** Juha Vierinen, Ryan Volz, Marc Fries, Elizabeth A. Silber, Sarah Albert, Philip J. Erickson, Matthias Clahsen, Frank D. Lind, Nico Pfeffer, John Swoboda, Allan Weatherwax, Simon G. Shepherd, Jorge L. Chau

**Abstract:** Metre-scale impactors connect near-Earth-object dynamics, atmospheric-entry physics and meteorite delivery, but their pre-impact orbits and material properties are rarely measured together, especially for daylight events. We report observations of the 30 May 2026 Cape Cod Bay bolide. A very high frequency (VHF) meteor radar network detected atmospheric-entry plasma, weather radars detected falling meteorites, and infrasound sensors recorded the associated hypervelocity shock. VHF radar range--Doppler measurements resolve the meteor head echo from about 90 to 71 km altitude, while interferometric measurements localize short-lived non-specular plasma wake echoes down to an unusually low altitude of $\sim 30$ km. We identify this as the radar terminus, where breakup is expected to occur. A combined head-echo and trail fit constrains a steep southeastward trajectory over coastal Massachusetts. Weather-radar detections refine the dark-flight solution, place the likely fall region in Cape Cod Bay and constrain probable fragment sizes. Backward propagation of the fitted trajectory gives a heliocentric orbit near the cometary--asteroidal classification boundaries, with conflicting classifications from empirical dynamical criteria. Catalogue comparisons identify no convincing parent body or established meteor-shower association. Regional infrasound independently supports the terminal fragmentation region and yields an energy estimate of 0.5 kt TNT equivalent. The radar and dark-flight evidence are consistent with a dense, mechanically strong meteoroid, possibly iron-rich. The combination of meteor-radar plasma tracking, weather-radar observations of falling meteorites and infrasound measurements can provide accurate orbital elements and trace large meteoroids from atmospheric entry to nearly ground impact.

## 198. [Velocity-resolved Ionization Mapping of the Broad-line Region. II. Signatures of Eccentric Broad-line Regions from a Quantitative Photoionization-Based Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25718) <a id="link198"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.25718

**Authors:** Jiancheng Wu, Hai-Cheng Feng, Sha-Sha Li, Qingwen Wu, Xinwu Cao

**Abstract:** Velocity-resolved reverberation mapping (RM) is one of the most powerful tools for probing the geometry and kinematics of the broad-line region in active galactic nuclei. However, its interpretation may be non-unique, as similar velocity-delay signatures can arise from different combinations of BLR geometry and kinematics. To address this degeneracy, this series of studies develops velocity-resolved ionization mapping (IM) as a complementary technique. In this second paper of the series, we construct photoionization-based BLR models to quantitatively demonstrate the diagnostic power of the joint RM+IM framework. We calculate local \ha\ and \hb\ emissivities with Cloudy, couple them with disk-like BLR models, and generate synthetic velocity-dependent line fluxes, lags, and Balmer decrements. The model reproduces the observed anti-correlation between the integrated BD and the incident ionizing continuum, with scatter naturally introduced by reverberation delays. We show that a virialized elliptical BLR can produce diverse velocity-delay maps and velocity-resolved lags that mimic RM signatures commonly attributed to virialized motion, inflow, or outflow. In contrast, the velocity-resolved BD is sensitive to the symmetry of the BLR gas distribution, providing a key diagnostic for distinguishing eccentric geometry from non-virial motions. Through proof-of-concept comparisons with one double-peaked and one single-peaked AGN, we show that an elliptical-disk BLR can simultaneously reproduce the observed line profiles, velocity-resolved lags, and velocity-resolved BDs. These results provide a quantitative basis for IM, support the presence of eccentric BLR structures in AGNs, and demonstrate the potential of joint RM+IM analyses to break the BLR geometry-kinematics degeneracy.

## 199. [Optical and Ultraviolet Emission from the Gas Photoionized by Gaia BH3: Archival Constraints and Predictions for the 2029 Periastron](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27075) <a id="link199"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.27075

**Authors:** Nico Cappelluti, Fabio Pacucci

**Abstract:** Gaia BH3, a $32.7\,M_\odot$ black hole (BH) on an 11.6-yr, $e=0.73$ orbit around a metal-poor giant at $\approx590$ pc, accretes from its wind. Neither its mass-loss rate $\dot M_{\rm w}$ nor the fraction of captured gas reaching the BH is known. We ask whether optical and ultraviolet lines from gas photoionized by the hot flow are observable and what they reveal. Unlike X-ray and radio data, they count the ionizing photons the gas absorbs and trace its density and velocity. With Cloudy we compute the H$α$ and He II 4686 emission of the wind and inflow along the orbit for $\dot M_{\rm w}=10^{-11}$-$10^{-8}\,M_\odot\,{\rm yr^{-1}}$, Eddington ratios $f_{\rm Edd}=10^{-8}$-$10^{-5}$ and wind speeds of 30 (slow) and 100 km s$^{-1}$ (fast), and test each model against archival UVES and ESPRESSO spectra. $f_{\rm Edd}$ mainly sets the lines; in dense models they come from gas falling freely onto the BH, spread over thousands of km s$^{-1}$. The archival spectra exclude $\dot M_{\rm w}>2\times10^{-9}\,M_\odot\,{\rm yr^{-1}}$ at $f_{\rm Edd}=2\times10^{-6}$ and $>2.6\times10^{-10}\,M_\odot\,{\rm yr^{-1}}$ at $10^{-5}$ (95%, slow wind), Eddington ratios already excluded by the apastron Chandra limit, $f_{\rm Edd}<7.6\times10^{-7}$. Of two simulated campaigns, only 25 epochs around the 2029 periastron go beyond existing limits. For a slow wind, whose capture rate is constant, a $\ge5σ$ H$α$ detection would imply an accretion state 2-3 times above the 2024 limit. For a fast wind, whose captured fraction rises 14-fold toward periastron, the campaign detects $\dot M_{\rm w}\simeq10^{-8}\,M_\odot\,{\rm yr^{-1}}$ for $f_{\rm Edd}\gtrsim1.2\times10^{-6}$, which the apastron limit allows. Below the Chandra limit, a slow wind produces no detectable line.

## 200. [Clumpy Outflows from Super-Eddington Accreting Black Holes. II. Conditions for Clump Formation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24660) <a id="link200"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.24660

**Authors:** Haojie Hu, Yuta Asahina, Shogo Yoshioka, Hiroyuki R. Takahashi, Ken Ohsuga

**Abstract:** Active galactic nuclei (AGNs) commonly exhibit ultra-fast outflows (UFOs) with velocities of $0.03$--$0.3~c$. Recent high-resolution X-ray observations with XRISM/Resolve suggest that UFOs may have a clumpy structure. However, the formation and evolution of these clumpy outflows remain poorly understood. In the first paper of this series, we investigated the formation of clumpy outflows using numerical simulations and find that sufficiently high spatial resolution is required to reproduce clumpy structures numerically. In this second paper, we perform a series of numerical experiments to identify the key physical processes governing clump formation. We find that spatial fluctuations of physical quantities at the disk surface are essential for the formation of clumpy outflows, whereas spatial and temporal fluctuations at the inner boundary have only a minor effect. Furthermore, density structure-function analysis suggests that the high-density regions have an elongated morphology with characteristic transverse widths of several tens of $R_{\rm g}$, possibly produced by radiation Rayleigh--Taylor instability. A more comprehensive investigation of the instability in general-relativistic radiation magnetohydrodynamic simulations, where accretion disk structures emerge self-consistently, is expected to provide deeper insights into the physical origin of clumpy outflows.

## 201. [Beyond dust in local cosmography: perturbative Vlasov predictions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25145) <a id="link201"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.25145

**Authors:** Chris Clarkson, Roy Maartens

**Abstract:** The dust approximation used in cosmography neglects the velocity dispersion generated during structure formation. This dispersion produces isotropic and anisotropic pressure, whose gradients accelerate the mean matter flow even though individual particles follow geodesics. The acceleration introduces an intrinsic dipole into the local distance-redshift relation. We calculate this dipole and the accompanying changes in expansion and shear using linear Vlasov perturbation theory about an FLRW background, with a mean dispersion history motivated by halo estimates. The assumed FLRW background prevents an independent test of the cosmological principle; the numerical predictions also depend on the adopted LCDM cosmology. Pressure also changes the growth of structure, modifying the shear and the spatial gradients that govern how the distance anisotropy varies with redshift. The largest correction at the lowest order in redshift is to the shear, while changes in the expansion gradient can produce a larger dipole at the next order. The resulting intrinsic dipole is far smaller than the observed low-redshift dipole, whose behaviour nearest the observer is dominated by motion between the matter and CMB frames.

## 202. [Gravitational filtering, magnetic-field inhomogeneity and Coulomb collisions in a kinetic model of a plasma atmosphere with stochastic heating at its base](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24678) <a id="link202"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.24678

**Authors:** Luca Barbieri, Pascal Démoulin, Daniel Verscharen

**Abstract:** We investigate the effects of stochastic heating, magnetic inhomogeneity, and Coulomb collisions in coronal loops using a reduced kinetic model. Coulomb collisions thermalize suprathermal populations generated by intermittent heating and populate velocity-space regions inaccessible from the lower boundary in the collisionless limit. We consider two regimes: when heating varies faster than the electron crossing time, the plasma develops a multitemperature nonthermal distribution; when it varies more slowly than the proton crossing time, individual heated loops approach an isothermal state. In the collisionless limit, conservation of the magnetic moment restricts the accessible velocity space, producing a strong temperature anisotropy, with parallel temperature exceeding perpendicular temperature, and additional density depletion. We find that Coulomb pitch-angle scattering efficiently suppresses these effects under typical coronal conditions. In the short-time-scale regime, density and temperature profiles approach those obtained with a constant magnetic field, while gravitational and collisional filtering sustain the temperature inversion. In the strongly collisional long-time-scale regime, magnetic-field-induced anisotropy is nearly eliminated, and individual loops remain approximately isotropic and isothermal. An increasing temperature profile then emerges only after averaging over unresolved flux tubes with different temperatures. Thus, Coulomb collisions largely suppress the macroscopic kinetic signatures of magnetic-field inhomogeneity in the transition region and low corona.

## 203. [PRIMA prototype spaceflight KID readout electronics laboratory characterization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24337) <a id="link203"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.24337

**Authors:** Adrian K. Sinclair, Chris Albert, C. Matt Bradford, Patrick Brown, Sean Bryan, Jesse Coldsmith, Jennifer Corekin, Sumit Dahal, Thomas K. Devlin, Thomas Essinger-Hileman, Logan Foote, Marc Foote, Draisy Friedman, Alessandro Geist, Jason Glenn, Christopher Green, Kyle Helson, Kevin Horgan, Tracee Jamison-Hooks, Elijah Kane, Jared Lucey, Philip Mauskopf, Lynn Miles, Sanetra Bailey Newman, Gerard Quilligan, Cody Roberson, Salman Sheikh, Manuel Vega, Eric Weeks, Christopher Wilson, Travis Wise

**Abstract:** We present further development and testing of prototype kinetic inductance detector (KID) readout electronics for the PRobe far-Infrared Mission for Astrophysics (PRIMA). PRIMA is a NASA Probe-class astrophysics mission concept with two science instruments, FIRESS and PRIMAger, which provide spectroscopic and polarimetric imaging from 24 to 261 um. Together these instruments will explore the far-infrared sky with spatial-spectral mapping speeds that exceed previous studies by orders of magnitude, opening a vast discovery space. This increase in mapping speed is achieved by densely packed ultra-sensitive KIDs and cryogenically cooled optics. The PRIMA flight readout electronics will use the build-to-print SpaceCube Mini v3.0 which houses a radiation-tolerant Kintex KU060 field programmable gate array (FPGA). A custom high-speed digitizer board is being designed along with an RF electronics (RFE) board. The RFE was designed to provide programmable gain, band filtering, and RF switching between instruments. The prototype readout electronics, including the RFE, use functionally equivalent parts to the flight parts. In this proceeding we measure the readout loopback noise performance in the second Nyquist zone and timestream cross-talk performance. We present preliminary sweeps with the prototype readout coupled to prototype FIRESS detectors. Lastly, we demonstrate a cosmic ray pulse detection and real-time removal with an algorithm implemented in firmware.

## 204. [Characterizing the Martin Luther King Event: Sun to Earth Evolution of the January 19, 2026 Geomagnetic Storm](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23704) <a id="link204"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.23704

**Authors:** Abril Sahade, Cecilia Mac Cormack, Andreas J. Weiss, Antonio Niemela, Diego G. Lloveras, Erika Palmerio, Teresa Nieves-Chinchilla

**Abstract:** We present a comprehensive Sun-to-Earth analysis of the extreme space weather event of 18 -19 January 2026, hereafter the Martin Luther King (MLK) storm, which produced a severe (G4) geomagnetic storm and one of the most intense solar radiation storms of Solar Cycle 25. The geomagnetic disturbance was driven by a fast Earth-directed coronal mass ejection (CME) associated with an X1.9-class flare from NOAA Active Region 14341. We combine multi-viewpoint remote-sensing observations, in situ measurements from Solar Orbiter and Wind, and physics-based magnetohydrodynamic simulations using the CORHEL-CME framework to reconstruct the eruption from the low corona to 1 au and identify the mechanisms governing its geoeffectiveness. Observations and simulations indicate that the eruption originated from a right-handed magnetic flux rope embedded in a complex magnetic environment dominated by a neighboring positive-polarity coronal hole. The simulations reproduce the observed CME morphology, asymmetric expansion, magnetic fields measured at both spacecraft, and Dst index evolution. The main discrepancy is attributed to filament material not included in the model. Favorable magnetic polarity alignment between the CME and coronal-hole field minimized magnetic erosion through interchange reconnection, enabling rapid escape and preservation of the strong southward magnetic field that drove the geomagnetic storm. The prolonged recovery phase resulted from interaction between the trailing CME and a following high-speed stream from the same coronal hole. These results demonstrate that storm intensity was primarily established by the coronal magnetic configuration, whereas storm duration was controlled by the ambient solar wind.

## 205. [Long-term ultraviolet variability of Taurus T Tauri stars from the IUE archive](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24780) <a id="link205"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.24780

**Authors:** Ana I Gomez de Castro

**Abstract:** Ultraviolet (UV) radiation from Classical T Tauri stars, generated primarily by magnetospheric accretion, plays a fundamental role in circumstellar disk evolution. However, its long-term variability remains poorly characterized due to the scarcity of homogeneous time-series observations. We investigate the variability of the near-UV continuum and the Mg II resonance doublet to assess whether both diagnostics trace the same underlying accretion variability. We analyzed the complete archive of long-wavelength International Ultraviolet Explorer (IUE) observations of T Tauri stars in Taurus-Auriga, comprising 475 low-dispersion spectra of 36 objects collected over 18 years. We characterized continuum variability using three windows between 2600 and 3100 Angstroms and examined continuum-subtracted Mg II fluxes, UV-V colors, and Lomb-Scargle periodograms to investigate correlations and characteristic timescales. Continuum fluxes between 2675 and 3055 Angstroms are strongly correlated, with variability dominated by changes in continuum level rather than spectral shape. Near-UV and quasi-simultaneous optical fluxes are significantly correlated in six of the seven best-sampled sources, although their relative variability differs. The Mg II-continuum relationship shows substantial object-to-object diversity, ranging from correlated variations with different fractional responses to decoupled or anti-correlated behavior, indicating no universal temporal response. Lomb-Scargle periodograms reveal formally significant features in some well-sampled objects, including near 10 and 33 days. However, irregular and clustered sampling prevents their identification as reliable periodic or characteristic timescales.

## 206. [Attitude Estimation of Falcon 9 Rocket Body Based on Photometric Characteristics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24596) <a id="link206"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.24596

**Authors:** Hui Li, Rong-Wang Li, Ru-Feng Tang, Yu-Qiang Li

**Abstract:** The proliferation of space debris, driven by increasingly frequent space activities, poses a severe risk to operational satellites and future missions. Consequently, the surveillance and characterization of space objects have become critical. Current methods for attitude estimation of space objects often rely on target-specific models, such as those with a closed set of facets, and typically employ nonlinear filtering or machine learning techniques. However, these approaches often lack general applicability. This study proposes a more general approach to attitude estimation based on photometric light curves. We define a loss function that quantifies the discrepancy between the measured light curve and a theoretical model parameterized by the attitude parameters. By leveraging automatic differentiation to compute the gradients of this function, we use a gradient descent algorithm to achieve rapid and accurate attitude estimation. The proposed method provides a versatile and efficient framework for attitude estimation, overcoming the target-specificity limitations of existing techniques, with the parameters estimation time reduced to 15 seconds and the mean square error lowered to three decimal places. Validation on the third-party database demonstrates that the method exhibits strong generalization capability.

## 207. [V347 Aurigae: from Peak to Quiescence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24765) <a id="link207"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.24765

**Authors:** Diya T. Kumar, Lynne A. Hillenbrand, Howard Isaacson

**Abstract:** We present an analysis of eight epochs of high-resolution optical spectra of the YSO (young stellar object) V347 Aur. The source exhibits periodic brightness variations spanning $\sim$2 mag over several months, interpreted as cyclic accretion-burst and quiescent phases, making V347 Aur a useful probe of the broad range of accretion and outflow variability in YSOs. When faint, V347 Aur shows an M-type photospheric absorption spectrum; when bright, it exhibits a strong emission-line spectrum. Our Keck/HIRES spectroscopy was obtained during the decay of an accretion burst. The emission spectrum includes the strong H I, Ca II, and He I transitions commonly seen in YSOs, as well as numerous weaker metal lines, for which we provide a complete inventory. As the star fades, photospheric absorption lines deepen, consistent with decreasing veiling from the accretion continuum. H I and He I emission, which trace accretion, strengthen relative to the continuum as it fades, but show the expected decline in strength with decreasing accretion after correcting for the strong early veiling. Ca II behaves similarly, with line profiles that alternate between red- and blueshifted dominance, which we interpret as the rotation of the magnetospheric accretion flows. Narrow, slightly blueshifted absorption in Ca II, Na I, and K I indicates disk winds with localized density enhancements. Forbidden [O I] and [S II] emission is blueshifted, with broad shoulders and low- and high-velocity narrow peaks, consistent with a disk geometry that obscures much of the receding outflow. Their behavior as the continuum fades is similar to that of the broad accretion lines. The V347 Aur system is a useful proxy both for the ubiquitous short-term accretion variability observed in YSOs, and for their secular evolution on longer time scales.

## 208. [MIDSSAR: MId Dispersion Spectrograph for Stellar Activity Research](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22834) <a id="link208"></a>

**Authors:** Fumihde Iwamuro, Daisaku Nogami, Hiroyuki Maehara, Masaru Kino, Kai Ikuta, Akito Tajitsu, Sanghee Lee, Miho Kawabata, Keisuke Isogai, Kenta Taguchi, Kazunari Shibata

**Abstract:** We have developed and commissioned MIDSSAR (MId Dispersion Spectrograph for Stellar Activity Research), a novel fiber-fed, dual-band spectrograph optimized for high-cadence monitoring of stellar superflares and magnetic activity phenomena, mounted on the 3.8-m Seimei Telescope at Kyoto University Okayama Observatory. Utilizing a custom-designed volume phase holographic grating and a commercially available volume binary transmission grating, the instrument achieves simultaneous observations of key chromospheric activity indicators: the CaII H&K band (382.5-406.5nm) at a resolving power of R=13,800, and the H$α$ band (641.1-667.6nm) at R=18,200. MIDSSAR is strategically integrated with the existing high-dispersion spectrograph GAOES-RV to enable concurrent photosphere-chromosphere monitoring. The control software coordinates high-speed dual-camera exposures down to 1ms while incorporating real-time telescope telemetry into the data headers. We also developed a dedicated data reduction pipeline that automatically executes calibration, spatial reconstruction of the 61-fiber bundle, and one-dimensional spectral extraction with a wavelength precision of $\sim$ 0.002nm. Initial on-sky test observations conducted from March to May 2026 successfully verified the system's quantitative readiness. The observed signal-to-noise ratio characteristics perfectly align with modeled theoretical limits under varying seeing conditions, confirming that the entire instrument complex successfully meets its primary design requirements and operational expectations for systematic stellar astrophysics surveys.

J0022+1340 has two indistinguishable branches at $B \simeq 64.4$ and 77.7 MG; J0749+3654 has competitive solutions near 29.3 and 96.4 MG; and J0035+4333 yields an indicative broad minimum at $B = 51.1\pm1.4$ MG. The ZTF data give 105.349 min for J0005+2941, with a narrow eclipse-like minimum, making it a rare eclipsing-polar candidate below the period gap; 80.4062 min for J0749+3654 from double-humped cyclotron beaming; and 143.8 min for J0035+4333. Archival mid-infrared photometry excludes donors earlier than about M4.5-M5 V in three systems and M3 V in J0035+4333. ## 210. [Inverse-designed tapers to improve throughput of on-chip astrophotonic spectrographs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24699) <a id="link210"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.24699

**Authors:** Vikram Seenivasan, Pradip Gatkine

**Abstract:** Astrophotonics offers a pathway towards compact and stable spectrographs for future large telescopes. The size of these spectrographs does not scale with the diameter of the telescope aperture; thus, they offer a cost-effective way to achieve astronomy science requirements compared to traditional optical spectrographs. A major limitation of current on-chip astrophotonic spectrographs is the significant loss incurred at interfaces between distinct components, particularly at the input and output fiber interfaces, limiting overall throughput. In this work, we investigate inverse-designed architectures to optimize transitions between these components to improve throughput. Inverse design formulates device design as an optimization problem, enabling non-intuitive geometries that can achieve high performance. We focus on optimizing coupling between an arrayed waveguide grating (AWG) spectrograph and an input single-mode fiber. Our approach explores two classes of fabrication-constrained inverse design. The first is shape optimization, which optimizes the geometry of the boundary of a coupling taper. The second is topology optimization, which optimizes the material distribution in a design region to gradually transform the optical mode between components. Both methods are implemented using the Tidy3D package from Flexcompute. We use germanium-doped silica for the core of the photonic components and silica as the cladding, and we use an SMF-28 fiber as input. This work demonstrates a pathway to improve astrophotonic spectrographs through computational inverse-design methods. Leveraging higher throughput, we can improve signal-to-noise ratios offered by photonic devices and position ourselves to design high-performance, cost-effective instrumentation for the next generation of telescopes, both on the ground and in space.

## 211. [Stability analysis of the Lane--Emden equation of index $n=5$ describing polytropic gas spheres](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35932) <a id="link211"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.35932

**Authors:** S. C. Mancas, Ronald Adams

**Abstract:** We study the Lane--Emden equation of index $n=5$, the critical polytropic case separating finite-radius from infinite-radius self-gravitating equilibria. The scaling family $θ_λ(ξ)=λ^{1/2}θ(λξ)$ generates a neutral mode $φ_0=\tfrac12θ+ξθ'$ about the Schuster profile. Schuster's transformation and a logarithmic variable reduce the equation to the autonomous Hamiltonian system $\ddotν=\tfrac14(ν-ν^5)$ with a first integral. Besides the Schuster (separatrix) and Srivastava solutions, every orbit crossing $ν=0$ is given in closed form by a Weierstrass $\wp$-function, and the closed forms agree with direct numerical integration to about $10^{-12}$. Up to scaling and sign, the Schuster solution is the only solution regular at the center, and it separates positive solutions from solutions with infinitely many zeros. In homology-invariant variables the singular solution $θ=(2ξ)^{-1/2}$ is a center exactly at $n=5$, where its stability changes. For a distance-varying Emden--Fowler system with a shifted equilibrium we prove that at $n=5$ the unique equilibrium is linearly and nonlinearly unstable. Finally, the linearization about the Schuster profile is the Schrödinger operator $L=-Δ_r-5θ_S^4$ on $L^2((0,\infty),ξ^2dξ)$. Its Friedrichs extension has essential spectrum $[0,\infty)$, a threshold resonance at $0$ carried by the non-normalizable scaling mode, and exactly one negative eigenvalue, $λ_0\approx-1.21$, so the Schuster profile is a saddle point of the associated energy functional.

**Abstract:** We report the design and cryogenic characterisation of a broadband superconducting 90deg intermediate-frequency (IF) hybrid covering 4 to 20 GHz for sideband-separating (2SB) heterodyne receivers. ## 213. [The EMA Justitia Probe: Terminator Frame-Based Deployment Orbit Selection and Surface Coverage Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26581) <a id="link213"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.26581

**Authors:** Shamil Biktimirov, Fatima Alnaqbi, Anton Ivanov

**Abstract:** The Emirates Mission to the Asteroid Belt (EMA) probe is designed to perform asteroid imaging during an uncontrolled ballistic descent to (269) Justitia. The mission design is constrained by main spacecraft proximity operations, uncertain asteroid gravity, limited knowledge of the final deployment trajectory, and requirements for illumination and safe operations. These constraints necessitate a mission design that ensures suitable illumination, attitude determination and control, and payload pointing throughout descent. The EMA probe concept of operations employs near-terminator deployment orbit geometry with continuous Sun visibility and two attitude control modes: single-axis Sun-spin for periodic imaging opportunities and three-axis asteroid-pointing for continuous asteroid visibility. Based on these modes, formal requirements are derived for deployment orbits that enable asteroid observations during ballistic descent. A deployment orbit selection method is developed using the terminator reference frame and a minimum boresight deviation margin. The method identifies families of orbit orientations satisfying illumination and payload observation constraints. Reference orbits are selected and verified through mission geometry analysis and trajectory propagation using a polyhedron gravity model. Asteroid surface coverage is then evaluated for uncontrolled tumbling, Sun-spin, and asteroid-pointing modes. The results characterize the expected imaging performance and demonstrate the feasibility of the EMA probe concept for close-proximity asteroid imaging. The proposed approach can also support future ballistic probe missions subject to similar operational and observational constraints.

**Relevance:** 1
**Novelty:** 3

## 214. [Analytical Chemistry at the Planetary Frontier](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27197) <a id="link214"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.27197

**Authors:** Mavia Anjum

**Abstract:** Planetary science depends on chemical measurements made under conditions very different from those in a terrestrial laboratory. This review treats the analytical methods of planetary science as one connected chain, from orbital remote sensing through in-situ measurements by landers and rovers and sample collection and curation, to the micro-analysis of returned material on Earth. Orbital methods, including visible and near-infrared reflectance, thermal emission and gamma-ray and neutron spectroscopy, are discussed first. In-situ techniques are then reviewed, including laser-induced breakdown spectroscopy (LIBS), micro-focus X-ray fluorescence (XRF), deep-ultraviolet Raman and fluorescence spectroscopy, evolved gas analysis with gas chromatography-mass spectrometry (GC-MS) and miniaturized mass spectrometry. Contamination control and curation practices are described, followed by laboratory techniques such as secondary ion mass spectrometry (SIMS and NanoSIMS), atom probe tomography (APT), thermal ionization mass spectrometry (TIMS), synchrotron micro-XRF and X-ray absorption near-edge structure (XANES) spectroscopy. Accuracy, calibration and reference materials are discussed as the link between in-situ and laboratory data, and the role of chemometrics and data fusion is examined. Flight and laboratory instruments are compared in terms of spatial resolution, detection limit, sample consumption and throughput. Active neutron-gamma spectroscopy planned for the Dragonfly mission to Titan is also discussed. This comparison shows that in-situ and laboratory techniques are complementary. In-situ instruments provide context and select samples, while laboratory techniques provide isotopic, molecular and speciation data that cannot yet be obtained in situ.

**Relevance:** 1
**Novelty:** 3

## 215. [Linear Polarimetry of the Solar Corona during the Total Solar Eclipse of 8 April 2024: Multi-Band Observations and HDR Imaging](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26231) <a id="link215"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.26231

**Authors:** Suprit Singh, Paras Sharma, Bharti Arora, Shivam Burman, Chaitanya Gandhi

**Abstract:** High-Dynamic-Range (HDR) multi-band linear polarimetry of the solar corona during total solar eclipses provides a vital ground-based diagnostic tool for mapping plasma morphology and magnetic topology in the inner and middle corona. Extracting reliable polarimetric signals requires rigorous observational calibration, spatial co-alignment, and noise reduction via stacking. We report optical linear polarimetric observations acquired during the 8 April 2024 total solar eclipse from Dallas, Texas, USA, near solar maximum. Using a full-frame color CMOS detector paired with an 80~mm refractor, multi-band Red, Green, and Blue (RGB) images were recorded simultaneously across three polarizer orientations ($-60^\circ$, $0^\circ$, and $+60^\circ$) using a 9-step exposure bracket ($1/800\text{~s}$ to $1/3\text{~s}$). We present an end-to-end data-reduction pipeline incorporating: (i)~a novel astrometric registration framework that reconstructs Sun-centric solar-disk coordinates via lunar-limb fitting, catalog star plate-solving, and ephemeris displacement calculations, achieving sub-pixel alignment; (ii)~a photometrically linear exposure-normalization HDR image synthesis; and (iii)~Multi-scale Gaussian Normalization (MGN) coupled with Polarization-to-RGB chromatic mapping. The resulting structurally enhanced polarization composites unravel a complex solar-maximum coronal topology characterized by helmet streamers and fine-scale striations traceable out to $\sim3\,R_\odot$. This framework establishes a high-resolution, photometrically calibrated dataset ready for subsequent polarized brightness ($pB$) and electron density inversions.

## 216. [Spectroscopic Studies of Meteors within the Skytinel Network](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25027) <a id="link216"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.25027

**Authors:** Ernest Świerczyński, Mateusz Żmija, Paweł Zaręba, Tomasz Żywczak

**Abstract:** We present 12 low-resolution meteor spectra recorded at four stations of the Skytinel network in Poland, focusing on consistent reduction and comparison of Fe, Mg, and Na emission features. Spectra spanning approximately 350--850 nm were extracted, background-subtracted, and wavelength-calibrated using IRAF. Corrections accounted for the wavelength-dependent quantum efficiencies of Sony IMX249 and IMX273 detectors and atmospheric attenuation as a function of observing geometry. The corrected spectra were decomposed into Fe, Mg, and Na components using selected atomic transitions, temperature-dependent emissivities, and pseudo-Voigt line profiles. Integrated Fe~I-15, Mg~I-2, and Na~I-1 intensities were compared in a ternary diagram. Most spectra overlap with or lie near the normal-meteor distribution in reference samples, while some extend toward Fe-rich or Na-rich regions. The most Fe-dominated event assigns approximately 81\% of the summed emission to Fe~I-15. These ratios characterize relative emission, not elemental abundances or bulk composition. Combined with trajectory and photometric data, spectral classification may help constrain plausible material properties in dark-flight models and refine predicted meteorite search areas.

## 217. [The coordinated radio and infrared survey for high-mass star formation - VI. Higher-resolution follow-up of point-like northern ultra-compact H II regions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23258) <a id="link217"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.23258

**Authors:** Ivayla E. Kalcheva, Melvin G. Hoare

**Abstract:** The CORNISH survey provided the most complete sample of ultra-compact H II (UCHII) regions in the northern Galactic plane to date, but at its 1.5 arcsec resolution the most compact, and plausibly youngest, members of the sample could not be morphologically classified. We present Karl G. Jansky Very Large Array (JVLA) A-configuration observations at 10 GHz of 69 such CORNISH UCHII regions, reaching an angular resolution of ~0.2-0.3 arcsec and a typical rms noise of ~10 microJy/beam. All but two of the imaged targets are detected; the two non-detections lie in fields that are dynamic-range limited by bright neighbours. Six targets (~9 per cent) are resolved into pairs of sources, and a further 15 CORNISH UCHII regions detected within the fields reveal complex, multi-component structure. We also report candidate massive young stellar object (MYSO) jet knots and a row of three compact sources coincident with a previously unresolved infrared-dark H II region. The targets have a median deconvolved angular diameter of ~0.9 arcsec and physical diameters of 0.004-0.17 pc (median ~0.03 pc), with no correlation between angular size and distance, consistent with a population of intrinsically compact, young regions. Spectral indices between 1.4 and 10 GHz place ~32 per cent of the targets below the thermal free-free limit, possibly indicating non-thermal emission or variability. These data enable morphological classification of nearly the entire CORNISH UCHII sample, which will be presented in a forthcoming paper.

## 218. [Characterization of integrating-sphere systematic effects for photon calibration of the KAGRA gravitational wave detector](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25581) <a id="link218"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.25581

**Authors:** Shingo Hido, Misato Onishi, Shinji Miyoki, Takahiro Sawada, Takahiro Yamamoto, Takayuki Tomaru, Yoshiki Moriwaki, Dan Chen

**Abstract:** Photon calibration (Pcal) systems are essential for accurately reconstructing gravitational-wave strain in laser interferometric detectors. In the KAGRA Pcal system, the laser power applied to the end test mass is measured using integrating-sphere power sensors. During the first part of the fourth observing run (O4a), repeated calibration measurements showed scatter larger than expected from the uncertainties assigned to individual measurements. Although this excess scatter was empirically reflected in the previously reported calibration uncertainties, its physical origin was not identified. In this study, we experimentally characterize the response of the integrating spheres used in the KAGRA calibration chain with respect to three incident-beam conditions: incidence angle, polarization, and beam position. Based on these measurements and the reproducibility of the integrating-sphere placement in each calibration configuration, we evaluate the corresponding systematic uncertainties and explicitly incorporate them into the uncertainty model for the O4a Pcal-X calibration measurements. The inclusion of these previously unmodeled effects substantially improves the consistency between the estimated uncertainties and the observed scatter, particularly for calibration parameters measured at the KAGRA end station, where the reduced chi-square values decrease from values as large as $10^3$ to values of order unity to several. These results indicate that variations in the incident-beam conditions of integrating-sphere power sensors account for a substantial part of the previously unexplained scatter and establish a more physically motivated framework for evaluating Pcal laser-power calibration uncertainty. They also provide a basis for future reduction of the calibration uncertainty through improved control of the incident-beam conditions.

## 219. [A Multiwavelength View of SPLUS J102139.31-282247.5: an X-ray Luminous and Eclipsing Dwarf Nova](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22790) <a id="link219"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.22790

**Authors:** Amanda Silva de Araujo, Raimundo Lopes de Oliveira, Koji Mukai, Raymundo Baptista, Albert Bruch, Romualdo Eleuterio, Angela Krabbe, Claudia Mendes de Oliveira

**Abstract:** We report the independent discovery of the cataclysmic variable (CV) SPLUS J102139.31-282247.5 using Southern Photometric Local Universe Survey 12 band photometry, following its initial identification via ASAS-SN variability. Here, we investigate the system using optical photometry alongside optical and X-ray spectroscopy. High-cadence optical light curves from publicly available TESS data and a dedicated campaign at the Pico dos Dias Observatory in Brazil revealed a highly inclined system exhibiting pronounced eclipses with an orbital period of 1.81 hours. We found no evidence of a WD spin period. Despite seasonal gaps, the long-term ASAS-SN light curve revealed several outbursts (15 with $Δ$mag $\sim$ 1.5 brighter than the mean magnitude) and one superoutburst over the past decade. During Swift X-ray telescope observations, the system exhibited X-ray luminosity of $8.5 \times 10^{31}$ erg s$^{-1}$ in the 0.3-10\,keV band, originating from thermal plasma emission at a temperature consistent with accretion shocks. Optical spectroscopy from the Gemini Multi-Object Spectrographs on the Gemini South telescope confirms the CV nature of the system, showing strong, double-peaked H and HeI emission lines alongside a weak HeII4686 line. Altogether, these features strongly support the classification of SPLUS J102139.31-282247.5 as a quiescent, eclipsing dwarf nova with an unusually high X-ray luminosity.

## 220. [A blazar is a blazar is a blazar ... Comparing long-term varaibility properties of PKS 1510-089 and PKS 2155-304](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23359) <a id="link220"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.23359

**Authors:** M. Zacharias, A. Wierzcholska

**Abstract:** Blazars are typically divided by spectral appearance; may that be strength of optical emission lines or the frequency of the synchrotron peak. However, this may say nothing about the variability properties of the various types. Here, we study the long-term variability properties with nearly 20 years of multiwavelength data of one flat-spectrum radio quasar, PKS 1510-089, and one high-frequency-peaked BL Lac object, PKS 2155-304. Both exhibit strong variability with an energy-dependent variability amplitude. Using the total data sets, we do not find any correlation between the various bands. While the flux distributions of PKS 2155-304 are compatible with log-normal functions, this is not the case for PKS 1510-089. We conclude that the one-zone model is inadequate to describe the long-term evolution of blazars, requiring multi-zone or stratified models. Nevertheless, the underlying jet physics appear similar between the two types and in both sources the variability is, in fact, variable.

## 222. [Simulations of Particle Acceleration by an Interplanetary Shock Observed at 0.07 au and 0.7 au](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24995) <a id="link222"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.24995

**Authors:** F. -J. Kong, G. Qin, Y. Wang

**Abstract:** Parker Solar Probe (PSP) observed a coronal mass ejection (CME)-driven shock at 0.07 au on 2022 September 5, and the same CME-driven interplanetary shock was subsequently observed by Solar Orbiter (SolO) at 0.7 au on 2022 September 6. Such a large difference in heliocentric distance between the two shock events enables our comparative study of particle acceleration in near-Sun and far-Sun regions. Using a backward-in-time test-particle approach in a slab-2D turbulent magnetic field model, we perform particle acceleration simulations for the 0.07 au PSP shock and 0.7 au SolO shock, covering the energy ranges measured by PSP/EPI-Lo (0.0697-8.06 MeV) and SolO/EPD (0.0495-5.83 MeV). With an initial kappa = 3 kappa distribution, the particle fluxes at the shock passage are successfully reproduced using effective local shock angles of 70 degrees for the PSP shock and 80 degrees for the SolO shock. Both the observed and simulated energy spectra exhibit a double power-law form. The 0.07 au PSP spectrum softens above 0.75 MeV, while the 0.7 au SolO spectrum hardens slightly above 0.51 MeV, revealing distinct near-Sun and far-Sun acceleration environments. Compared with the observations, the simulations yield harder energy spectra with higher break energies of 2 MeV at 0.07 au and 3 MeV at 0.7 au, indicating efficient particle acceleration under the initial kappa = 3 distribution. Both the observed and simulated spectral indices are harder than the standard diffusive shock acceleration (DSA) prediction, likely arising from non-local acceleration at the large-angle quasi-perpendicular shocks, efficient injection of pre-accelerated suprathermal particles, and the finite shock lifetime effect.

## 223. [Twenty-five years of XMM-Newton EPIC-MOS2 Unfocused Particle Background: Solar Cycle Modulation, Galactic Cosmic-Ray Correlation, and Implications for NewAthena](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27121) <a id="link223"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.27121

**Authors:** B. Vaia, F. Gastaldello, S. Molendi, I. Bartalucci, M. Balboni, S. Ghizzardi, L. Lovisari, M. Marelli, M. Rossetti, A. Tiengo, C. Macculi, S. Lotti, V. Fioretti, T. Mineo, G. Schellenberger, C. E. Grant

**Abstract:** We present a study of the unfocused particle-induced background measured by the XMM-Newton EPIC-MOS2 detector, extending previous analyses to the end of 2025 and covering approximately twenty-five years of observations. Using out-of-field-of-view (outFOV) data, we investigate the long-term evolution of the particle background over two complete solar cycles and compare it with independent tracers of the Galactic cosmic-rays. We find an extremely tight correlation between the MOS2 outFOV count rate and the AMS proton flux, with an intrinsic scatter of only about 1.1\%, confirming that variations in the unfocused background are primarily driven by changes in the Galactic cosmic-rays flux. The extended temporal baseline clearly reveals the modulation associated with the solar cycle and the characteristic signatures of the 22 year Hale cycle. A Lomb-Scargle analysis identifies a dominant periodicity at 11.28 yr, while structure-function and wavelet analyses reveal a persistent $\sim$27 day modulation associated with solar rotation. We also confirm the presence of a residual background component that does not scale with the Galactic cosmic-ray flux, consistent with previous studies and possibly associated with Compton-scattered high-energy X-ray photons. This work places the particle-induced background measured by the XMM-Newton EPIC-MOS2 detector in the broader context of Galactic cosmic-ray studies, while also providing direct implications for the development of background-monitoring and mitigation strategies for future X-ray missions such as \textit{NewAthena}.

## 224. [An Enhanced Intersecting-Lines Method for Optical Loss Characterization in SIS Heterodyne Receivers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24838) <a id="link224"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.24838

**Authors:** François Joint, Magnus Strandberg, Denis Meledin, Igor Lapkin, Sven-Erik Ferm, Mathias Fredrixon, Leif Helldner, Alexei Pavolotsky, Erik Sundin, Victor Belitsky, Vincent Desmaris

**Abstract:** Accurate knowledge of radio-frequency (RF) losses in front of superconductor--insulator--superconductor (SIS) mixers is essential for optimizing millimeter- and terahertz heterodyne receivers. However, such losses are difficult to extract from conventional hot/cold-load measurements because the measured receiver noise combines contributions from the input optics, the mixer, and the intermediate-frequency chain. In this work, we present a numerical implementation of the straight-line, or intersecting-lines, method for estimating input-referred RF noise and optical loss from standard Y-factor measurements. Hot/cold IF spectra are acquired while sweeping the local oscillator (LO) pumping level from under-pumped to over-pumped operation, so that each IF frequency bin gives a family of linear relations between receiver output power and input load temperature. We evaluate all pairwise intersections between these lines and analyze the crossing cloud to obtain the effective RF input noise. We repeat the analysis across the IF band and multiple LO tunings which provides a frequency-resolved characterisation of the input losses over the full band. Our implementation turns the straight-line technique into a reproducible framework for receiver characterisation and relative optical-loss comparison in low-loss front-end configurations.

## 226. [The Atmospheric Chemistry and Surface Interactions on Mars (ACSIOM) Workshop 2025](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23500) <a id="link226"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.23500

**Authors:** Benjamin Benne, Megan A. J. Brown, Bethan S. Gregory, Paul M. Streeter, The ACSIOM Team

**Abstract:** The ACSIOM workshop was held in Cambridge from 10th-12th November 2025. Its primary aims were twofold: 1) to gather UK researchers involved in Mars atmospheric science to identify current shortcomings and challenges in the field, and 2) to discuss possible collaborations and future steps needed to maximise the research output from current and future Mars missions. Workshop attendees spanned a wide range of subjects and disciplines, from atmospheric modellers and surface scientists to experimental chemists and Earth/Venus/exoplanet scientists. One of the primary outcomes of the workshop as identified by the attendees was the need for more collaborations between these different communities. It was agreed that we currently lack sufficient interaction between disciplines and this is a key challenge we need to overcome in order to properly utilise and exploit the vast pool of martian expertise available in the UK. Modellers need reaction rates from experimentalists; experimentalists need modelling context to prioritise their experiments; geologists need modelling context to understand the features they see; modellers need geological context to constrain their results. Attendees stressed that the UK Mars community has a critical mass which is internationally recognised, and improving the communication between communities would allow significant progress to be made in martian research, allowing the UK to maintain leadership in Mars science for years to come. We highlight that this is a pivotal moment for such investments as many planetary missions, both for Mars and other terrestrial bodies, are about to be launched or are being considered in the coming years. Having a strong and close-knit community will be key to better identifying needs and capabilities in order to optimise investments in missions and to have the best scientific return possible.

# Paper Selection Prompt

## System Prompt

> You are a helpful paper reading assistant whose job is to read daily posts from ArXiv and rank the papers in order according to your friend's interests.
> Your job is to carefully read the paper titles and abstracts below and determine how well they match the criteria below.

## User Prompt

> ## Instructions
> 
> Write the response in JSONL format with {ARXIVID, COMMENT, RELEVANCE, NOVELTY} on each line, one for each paper.
> 
> - ARXIVID: should be the ArXiv ID.
> - COMMENT: for papers with RELEVANCE 7-10, briefly identify the specific matching criterion in at most 10 words. For papers with RELEVANCE 1-6, use an empty string. Never explain why a paper is irrelevant.
> - RELEVANCE: should be a score from 1-10.
> - NOVELTY: should be a score from 1-10.
> 
> 
> ## Scoring Criteria
> 
> > The "Relevance" score measures how closely the paper aligns with the core topics of the prompt.
> > The "Novelty" score assesses the originality and impact of the paper.
> > They are two **ORTHONORMAL** axes and **SHOULD NOT** be confused with each other.
> > When determining the final ranking of papers, the relevance score should be given twice as much weight as the novelty score.
> 
> ### Relevance Scoring
> 
> - Relevance 9-10 (Completely Relevant)
>   - Focus: Fully aligned with core topics with no deviation, score the highest if contains relevant keywords in it.
>   - Examples: Papers focused on foundational methods or theoretical research whose topic directly relates to core topics.
> 
> - Relevance 7-8 (Relevant)
>   - Focus: Retain a solid link to the main research area, though may touch on peripheral elements.
>   - Examples: Papers whose topics generally align with the core topics, but may other aspects or applications not directly related to the core topics.
> 
> - Relevance 5-6 (Borderline)
>   - Focus: Maintains a link to the core topics but also extends into at least one other domain/area beyond the primary focus.
>   - Examples: Papers whose results have implications for the core topics but are not directly related to the core topics.
> 
> - Relevance 3-4 (Irrelevant)
>   - Focus: Largely outside our interests with only very weak association to our topics.
>   - Examples: Papers whose results may have limited impact on the core topics and are not directly related to the core topics.
> 
> - Relevance 1-2 (Ignore)
>   - Focus: Purely unrelated to our topics. Completely a different domain.
> 
> ### Novelty Scoring
> 
> - Novelty 9-10 (Breakthrough)
>   - Definition: Groundbreaking methods/theory/application introducing new directions or solving major challenges.
>   - Examples: Entirely new paradigm for core topics.
> 
> - Novelty 7-8 (Improvements)
>   - Definition: Substantial insights/enhancements, though not a full paradigm shift.
>   - Examples: Modifications on existing methods yielding significantly better results. Applications to new datasets that directly address the core topics. Re-analysis of old datasets using new methods that directly address the core topics.
> 
> - Novelty 5-6 (Borderline)
>   - Definition: Incremental contributions with possible long-term benefits, not immediately transformative.
> 
> - Novelty 3-4 (Tangential)
>   - Definition: Minor or domain-specific improvements with limited broader impact.
> 
> - Novelty 1-2 (Low)
>   - Definition: Methods/application not applicable to core topics.
> 
> 
> ## Papers
> 
> [PAPER LIST HERE]
> 
> ## Relevant Topics
> 
> Use the following relevance criteria when considering which papers to keep. To be rated as highly relevant, a paper must fit at least one of the relevant topics below. Papers which fit multiple categories should be rated more highly.
> 
> 1. Near-Field Cosmology
>    - Relevant: Astrophysics work focusing on dwarf satellite galaxies of the Milky Way, extragalactic satellite luminosity functions, measurements of dark matter density profiles (core-cusp), abundances of dwarf galaxies, signs of historic interactions between the Milky Way and other galaxies (e.g., the accreted stellar halo). Both theoretical and observational work is relevant.
>    - Irrelevant: Papers that focus on Milky Way properties like the stellar disk, bar, and Galactic center. Papers that focus on higher mass galaxies, like massive spirals and elliptical galaxies.
> 
> 2. Resolved Star Formation Histories
>    - Relevant: Observational work deriving star formation histories from observed color-magnitude diagrams of galaxies. Theoretical work measuring star formation histories of simulated galaxies is also relevant, but should be weighted lower.
> 
> 3. Star Formation Quenching
>    - Relevant: Observational work discussing the quenching of star formation in galaxies, **particularly dwarf galaxies**. Work on higher mass galaxies may still be relevant, but should be weighted lower. Theoretical work on the quenching of star formation of dwarf galaxies is also relevant.
> 
> 4. Stellar Models
>    - Relevant: Theoretical work presenting new stellar models, either stellar evolutionary tracks (i.e., stellar interior models) or stellar atmosphere models. Work presenting new tables of bolometric corrections is also relevant.
> 
> 5. Numerical Galaxy Simulations
>    - Relevant: Theoretical work presenting new numerical simulations of galaxies is relevant, especially high-resolution simulations. Simulations that include new physics models (e.g., ionizing radiation, cosmic ray transport) should be given increased weight. Work analyzing previously published simulations should be weighted lower, unless the work also pertains to one of the other relevant topics. Semi-analytic work on galaxies that matches the other criteria is also relevant.
>    - Irrelevant: Numerical simulations that do not focus on galaxies and do not fulfill other criteria are not relevant. Examples include simulations of tidal disruption events, black hole accretion disks, and planet formation.
> 
> 6. Star Formation Driven Outflows
>    - Relevant: Observational and theoretical work examining the role the star formation processes play on driving galaxy-scale gas outflows. Measurements of mass-loading factors are particularly relevant. Work commenting on the phase structure of the outflows is also relevant. Work on galaxies more massive than the LMC should be weighted lower.
>    - Irrelevant: Papers that focus on outflows driven by sources other than stellar feedback, such as AGN-driven feedback. Papers are relevant if they include discussion of both star-formation-driven outflows and other types of outflows.
> 
> 7. Circumgalactic Medium
>    - Relevant: Observational and theoretical work examining the circumgalactic media (CGM) of dwarf galaxies is relevant. Increased weight should be given to work that connects the CGM to ISM outflows from the galaxy.
> 
> 
> 
> **Keywords:**
> 
> - Relevant: Near-field cosmology, satellite luminosity function, core-cusp, substructure, resolved star formation history, satellite quenching, mass-loading factor
> - Irrelevant: bar, Galactic center, tidal disruption event (TDE), supermassive black hole (SMBH)
